mechanism: A Cloudflare Pages project with Pages Functions, behind Access for the owner's email
only. While the page is open it calls the function every 60 seconds per connected project; the
function holds a read-only fine-grained GitHub token, makes conditional GitHub REST reads and hands
the raw records back; the page parses them and shows what waits on the owner. The owner answers
cards and records plans through prefilled GitHub new-file links that the owner commits, so the desk
holds no write credential.
problem: The owner runs several AI-built projects that stop whenever they need a ruling. Today the owner
learns of waits from a read-only page rebuilt hourly (Operations-Hub, no longer in use) or by
scrolling Claude Code sessions, so waits are missed or slow and "quiet" can't be trusted
(PROJECT.md §2, Part 2 "requests get buried"). Only the owner is affected.
milestones: M1, M2, M2B, MD1, MD2, MD3, M3A, M3B, M3C

**How this spec is used (owner's ruling of 2026-10-06, decisions/questions/P-001-build-path.md,
question 0).** P-001's automated pipeline closed with `re-scope` (decisions/P-001/stop-6.md). The
owner chose to build the desk outside the pipeline, from the approved brief and this spec, in three
milestones (as ruled then; the owner's design-step ruling of 2026-10-07 added a fourth, MD, below). Each piece is checked by the Reviewer and merged by the owner; there is no further
Critic gate, and the experiments run during Build. This spec stays `status: draft`: it is the
Builder's working brief, not a frozen spec (see Risks, "No freeze").

**Where the build stands (amendment of 2026-10-08).**
- **M1 is accepted** (2026-10-07, decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md: "I accept M1"). PR #40
  and the fix-forward PR #41 both passed the Reviewer and are deployed to the preview. The
  experiment outcomes are recorded under "Experiments in Build".
- **The owner added a design milestone, MD, between M2 and M3**
  (decisions/questions/P-001-design-step.md). It starts with research (Q-010, design; Q-011, phone
  notifications), runs a design lab and a sandbox view, and ends with the chosen front end running
  on the real back end on the preview, before launch. Its criteria follow the owner's eight answers
  (decisions/questions/P-001-design-discovery.md). So the milestones are now M1, M2, MD and M3.
- **M2 gains** CI read from workflow runs and the Reviewer's PR #41 findings (AC29–AC34), and
  flags the Orchestrator's hold cards and merge cards (AC46, AC47).

**Where the build stands (amendment of 2026-10-14).**
- **MD now carries the design lab's rulings and the owner's comments on the screens**, so the
  Builder builds the sandbox (AC43, process B) from this spec, not from a conversation. This is
  the revision decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-1.md names in its follow-ups.
  - Rulings 1, 2 and 6 (the agent panel and who has the ball; one timeline per project; each
    project's own queue, grouped by kind of action, with a response sheet): AC49, AC51, AC52.
  - Rulings 3 and 4 (the six phases, and milestones per project) are in the model now (v3.3.0,
    L-0008; docs/PROJECT.md section 3). The screens use them: AC50.
  - Ruling 5 (Chief of Staff sessions recorded) is a model change not yet made. The desk shows
    "not recorded" where it would need those records ("What this spec does not cover").
  - The owner's comments of 10 to 13 Oct on screens 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 (screen 7, the response
    card, sits inside screen 6): AC48 to AC53, each quoted where it applies.
  - How the screens join up and move between each other: AC48. What they read: J11.
- **Nothing here records the owner's pick.** The lab ends only when the owner picks a direction,
  and the Chief of Staff records it (AC43; CS10). AC48–AC53 describe the screens as the lab has
  settled them so far (docs/design/README.md, 13 Oct). If the owner picks differently, the Definer
  revises them before the sandbox is built.
- **Not to be built:** "Ask for something in this project" (the owner has not asked for it), and
  anything that would need a record no project keeps today (each listed as a model change).
- **Not built, by the owner's ruling:** Home's "Tell your Chief of Staff…" bar. It was deferred
  until the owner ruled; the owner's answer to design-lab question 1 is "I see no need for it if
  it’s not doing anything right now." (decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md), so it is dropped, and Home's
  bottom band with it (AC48).

**Where the build stands (amendment of 2026-10-14, trial-view notes 1).**
- **The owner tried the sandbox on the preview and sent its Home screen back with four changes**
  (decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-1.md, the notes and two answers quoted there
  verbatim). That record reads it as the owner sending the candidate back with changes (AC44
  step 3); the pick of the five screens stands.
- **AC54 carries the four changes**, on Home only:
  - each card shows a brief project summary, the first paragraph of the project's own README
    (as first written, whole and never clipped; since 2026-10-15, its first three lines with the
    whole summary one tap away: see the tile-summary amendment below);
  - the "latest" line leaves the card and opens in a bubble from a small control on it;
  - the cards stay a two-column grid on the phone;
  - the projects sit in three collapsible groups: Active; Done; Closed or not applicable.
- **Changed in place to match:** AC8's box order now holds within each group (AC35); AC37's
  where-it's-heading line moves into the bubble on Home and stays on the Status screen (AC37,
  AC48); J11 adds README.md as an optional read, for every connected project, and A3 counts it.
- **One reading is mine, and the owner is asked to confirm it** (Open questions, trial-view notes
  1): which group a project whose own item was closed with `re-scope` sits in. Service-Desk's own
  item is such a project.

**Where the build stands (amendment of 2026-10-15, trial-view notes 1, notes 5 to 15).**
- **The batch is closed** ("That’s everything for this batch", 15 Oct). The same record now holds
  notes 5 to 15, each with "How it is read", and the owner's answer on note 7, "Rebuild in new
  design (Recommended)". Each note's change is below; the pick of the five screens still stands.
- **Note 5 needs no criterion.** The Status screen was right about the records: the asks were only
  in the session. The fix is in the process: every ask of the owner is filed as a question or card
  in its project, so the desk sees it. Those two asks are filed in their own pull requests, one of
  them questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md (the eleven design-lab questions; its question 11 is
  AC54's `re-scope` reading, unanswered, so the recommendation, Active, is built; answered
  "Yes" since, see the design-lab answers below).
- **New criteria:**
  - AC55, the Status screen: "Your last action" above the waiting bar (note 6), and "More detail"
    rebuilt in the new design inside the sandbox (note 7).
  - AC56, moving about and refreshing: a back control that goes one screen back beside the Home
    one (note 8); a visible close control on every sheet and bubble (note 9); a refresh that keeps
    the screen as the owner left it (note 14).
- **Changed in place:**
  - AC52: "Done today" rows say what was ruled, in words (note 10);
  - AC49: the running log's branch drawing (note 11);
  - AC51: the Timeline brought back to the agreed screen 4, with milestones as headings and each
    run's steps grouped under them (notes 12 and 13);
  - AC53 and J3: a question and its own answer box for each question asked, prefilling the
    ruling file (note 15);
  - AC36: inside the sandbox its detail is reached through AC55's rebuilt views.
- **No new reads.** Everything above uses records J2, J9, J10 and J11 already read, so A3's
  arithmetic is unchanged.
- **Draft 2, after plan review round 1** (2026-10-15):
  - only the owner's rulings count in "Your last action" and "Done today" (AC55, AC52): a file
    whose line 1 starts `Ruling:`, or `Decision:` for a profile approval; a Critic's verdict
    there is ignored;
  - a refresh is every redraw the owner did not cause, the 15-second tick included, and that
    tick updates only the time-dependent text (AC56);
  - the "Latest" bubble stays closed after Escape while focus stays on its control (AC54,
    AC56); the Timeline opens on AC50's now run (AC51); a lane open at the oldest row has no
    fork (AC49); and AC53 says what a card's answer carries and why, how the recommendation
    line is split, and how an answer with a line break or `; ` is joined.
- **Draft 3, after plan review round 2** (2026-10-15):
  - AC52's test checks that a file whose line 1 starts neither `Ruling:` nor `Decision:` is not
    shown, in place of words for a ruling not in form (B1);
  - AC53 joins an answer's line breaks first, then turns `; ` into `, `, and tests "a;", a line
    break and "b" giving "a, b" (N1);
  - AC51's neutral milestone colour cites the owner's answer, "Bold headings, no colour
    (Recommended)" (N3);
  - AC53 indents each line of the owner's note on a phase-gate card by two spaces, so no note
    line is read as a gate card's field, and tests it (N5).

**Where the build stands (amendment of 2026-10-15, tile summary).**
- **The owner found the Home cards too tall** and sent this one change through on its own, not
  as a second batch (decisions/questions/P-001-tile-summary-clip.md, the note and two answers
  quoted there verbatim, with "How it is read"). The summary was what made each card run on.
- **AC54(a) changes in place:** the summary shows its first three lines on the card, faded at
  the cut. One tap on it opens the whole summary, word for word and never cut, in a bubble that
  closes as AC56 requires of every sheet and bubble. A summary of three lines or fewer shows
  whole, with no fade and no control. The cut never changes the words, only how many show.
- **Nothing else on Home changes:** the grid, the "Latest" bubble and the three groups stay as
  AC54 has them. AC56's list of pop-ups gains the summary bubble.
- **No new reads.** The summary comes from the same README read (J11), so A3 is unchanged.
- **Draft 2, after plan review round 1** (2026-10-15):
  - "every tile the same height", from the answer the owner chose, is carried: the summary area
    always takes three lines' height, and cards in one grid row share one height (B1);
  - AC56's refresh test keeps an open summary bubble (B2);
  - the summary control is named "Show the whole summary of <project name>"; a second tap on the
    summary closes its bubble; opening either bubble closes the other; and the test covers 360 px
    and 414 px with a summary that crosses the three-line mark (N1, N2, N5, N6).

**Where the build stands (amendment of 2026-10-16, the design-lab answers).**
- **The owner answered all eleven design-lab questions** in
  decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md. Its line 1 starts, in the owner's words: "Ruling:
  11 Ruling: 1 I see no need for it if it’s not doing anything right now., 2 Yes, 3 Agree, 4
  Agree, 5 Yes, 6 Yes, 7 Under decisions, 8 Keep, 9 Linked, 10 Go with the recommendation., 11
  Yes", and runs on.
  - The line has a stray "11 Ruling: " after its leading "Ruling: ".
  - The question block ("Question: …" and "Questions, as put: …") runs onto the same line,
    because the record's own line breaks were lost.
  - It is read as eleven answers, numbered 1 to 11 and joined by ", ", as quoted above. The stray
    words and the question block are not read as answers. The file stays exactly as the owner
    filed it (the follow-up ruling below, answer 2).
  - The desk's numbered-ruling rule line (AC53) would read this line as one answer, numbered 11,
    and say no answer is given for 1 to 10, since it splits answers on "; " and the owner used
    ", "; but it checks only the text in an answer box before the owner commits, never a saved
    record, so nothing the desk shows changes from it.
- **Answers 2, 3, 4, 7, 8, 9 and 11 match what the build already carries**, so nothing the
  sandbox does changes for them: the panel inside each project, lit on Home (2, AC49); the now
  run's rule (3, AC50); the phase count in words with no bar (4, AC50); hold cards under
  Decisions (7, AC52); three upcoming lines and 30 minutes (8, AC49); the gate document linked
  only (9); a project closed with `re-scope` in Active (11, AC54).
- **Answers 5 and 6 are "Yes", and the desk stays as it is until the model change each names
  lands.** The question put both to a later operating-model change the owner approves
  separately (its blast radius: "for 5, 6 and 10, a later operating-model change you approve
  separately").
  - 5: the model records when the owner accepts a milestone (the model change "A project
    milestone's 'reached'", "What this spec does not cover"). No record the desk reads says a
    milestone is reached, so until then AC50 shows "work filed" or "no work filed yet", never
    "reached".
  - 6: waiting deploys count as needing the owner. No record the desk reads names who must
    approve a waiting deploy, so it waits on the model change "deploy approvals tied to the
    owner" ("What this spec does not cover"). The owner confirmed this in a follow-up: "After the
    model change (Recommended)" (decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-answers-followup.md, answer
    1; the other option offered was "Now, Service-Desk only"). Until then AC52's Deploys line
    stays as built, marked with the owner's answer.
- **Answer 10 is "Go with the recommendation."** The model change that goes first is "Chief of
  Staff sessions recorded like pipeline sessions" ("What this spec does not cover"). It goes to
  the model under its own process; until it lands, the desk shows "not recorded" where it needs
  it, as today.
- **Question 1 differs from the recommendation.** The recommendation was to leave the bar
  deferred; the answer drops it. AC48 changes in place: Home has no "Tell your Chief of Staff…"
  bar, no "Deferred: waiting on your ruling" words, and nothing fixed to the bottom of Home's
  screen, with no sheet or bubble open. AC21's V5 line stays on Home, after the groups, as before. "Ask for something in this
  project" stays unbuilt, as the question said.
  - Dropping the band with the bar is my reading (C), not the owner's words: the band held only
    the bar in the build (src/public/sandbox.js) and only the compose bar and its mic in the lab
    (docs/design/screens/01-home.html).
- **Two things in the build lean on the band, and change with it** (plan review round 1, B1):
  - the AC51 + AC48 stacking test (tests/desk/ac51-timeline-line.test.mjs:174-193, marked
    `ac-test: S-001/AC51`) checks that both `.sb-band` and the project screens' `.sb-dock` sit
    above every tile and below a sheet and its scrim. It drops `.sb-band` and keeps `.sb-dock`;
  - the bubble placement in src/public/sandbox.js (`placeBubble`, AC54's bubbles) measures the
    band and keeps each bubble clear of it. With no band it no longer clears one; each bubble
    still sits wholly inside the viewport, as AC54 and AC56 require.

  AC54's and AC56's own rules name no band, so neither changes for it. AC48's test checks that
  the bar, its deferred words and the band are absent, in place of checking that the bar opens
  and writes nothing.
- **Long ruling lines are clipped on display** (the follow-up ruling,
  decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-answers-followup.md, answer 2: "Desk clips long lines
  (Recommended)"). Wherever the sandbox shows a ruling's line 1, AC55's "Your last action" and
  AC52's "Done today", a line longer than three lines shows its first three, faded, with the
  whole line one tap away, as AC54(a)'s summary does: the same control rules, the same bubble
  rules under AC56, and the record never edited. AC55, AC52 and AC56 change in place.
  - A bubble taller than the viewport is held to the viewport's height less a margin and
    scrolls up and down inside itself, never sideways, with its "Close" in view (C); the owner's
    record needs it at 360 px. The tests use 360×740 and 414×896. AC54's bubbles follow the same
    rule. Each bubble also sits clear of the project screens' bottom bar.
  - The time and code after a clipped line sit outside the button (C).
  - **Carried risk, the control's name:** the control is named "Show the whole of <record's
    code>" (C), as AC54(a)'s is named "Show the whole summary of <project name>". Its visible
    text is the owner's own words, which the name does not contain; that is against WCAG 2.5.3
    ("Label in Name"), so a voice-control user saying the words they see would not reach it.
    S-001 sets no WCAG standard, so no criterion breaks; it is kept for consistency with AC54(a),
    and both names would change together if the owner asks.
- **No new reads.** A3 is unchanged.
- **Open, not this spec's to change:** docs/design/README.md:35 still says Home keeps its "Tell
  your Chief of Staff…" bar. This spec governs where they differ; the README is not in the
  Definer's lane, so it is left for its owner to bring in line.
- **Draft 2, after plan review round 1** (reviews/S-001-design-lab-answers/_plan-review-round1-verbatim.md):
  line 1 quoted as it starts, with its stray words and run-on block named (B2); only 2, 3, 4, 7,
  8, 9 and 11 said to match the build, with 5 and 6 waiting on their named model changes and
  AC52's Deploys line marked (B3); the stacking test and the bubble placement named as leaning
  on the band, and dropping the band marked (C) (B1, N2); the malformed line's effect on the rule
  line (N4); AC48's test put as "nothing fixed to the bottom of Home's screen" (N5); "Home's
  neutral look" reworded (N6); and the follow-up ruling's clip added to AC55, AC52 and AC56.
- **Draft 3, after plan review round 2** (reviews/S-001-design-lab-answers/_plan-review-round2-verbatim.md):
  a rule for a bubble taller than the viewport, with the test viewports named, for AC55's and
  AC52's bubbles and AC54's (B1); the clip for rulings only, as AC52 says (choice 4); the time and
  code outside the button (choice 1); the control's name kept, with its WCAG 2.5.3 risk carried
  (choice 3); each bubble clear of the bottom bar (N1); the "Done today" bubble in AC56's close
  and refresh checks (N2); and "with no sheet or bubble open" in AC48's absence check (N3).
- **Draft 4, after build review 1** (B1, N1, N3; all (C)): AC55's line could change under an open
  whole-line bubble, against AC56. Now, while the bubble is open, a refresh leaves it unchanged
  at its scroll position, with one line saying a newer action is arriving; it closes only by the
  owner's ways, and the line then shows its new state. AC56's refresh step 2b gains that case. A
  short ruling keeps the one-line form "<words> · <time>"; the bubble holds line 1 only, with
  "(proxy)" outside it.
- **Draft 5, after build review round 2** (reviews/S-001-design-lab-answers/_build-review-round2-verbatim.md;
  R2-B1, N-R2-1; all (C)): the hold is spelled out for "Done today": a refresh that would remove
  or change a row with its bubble open, such as the day turning at midnight, leaves the bubble
  unchanged with the same notice, and the fold shows its new state once the owner closes it. When
  a held bubble closes and its control is gone, focus goes to "Your last action" or "Done
  today"'s header. AC56's refresh checks gain the midnight case and the focus fallback.
- **Draft 6** (C): one notice fits both cases, a newer action and a row leaving: "This has
  changed since you opened it. Close this to see the latest.", for "Your last action" and "Done
  today", in the rule and the tests. It replaces draft 4's "A newer action is arriving. Close
  this to see it.".

**Where the build stands (amendment of 2026-10-16, trial-view notes 2, notes 1 to 27).**
- **From 2026-10-16: the owner's notes of 2026-10-16 on the sandbox's Status and Timeline
  screens, notes 1 to 27 of trial-view batch 2.** The record is
  decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-2.md: the owner's words verbatim, the follow-up
  answers, and a "How it is read" section for each note. This spec reads each note as that section
  reads it. Where a later note's reading replaces an earlier one, the later one governs:
  - note 15 makes everything read from the bottom up, in place of note 12's top-down line;
  - note 27's Context replaces note 17's: Context is the facts that were true and led to the why,
    each naming its source, not the owner's question and answer;
  - note 24 renames the level under milestones from tasks to sprints;
  - note 25 picks option B for the branch drawing's background (the dots inside each phase's own
    box), out of note 20's three;
  - note 26 makes the Timeline's line thinner and lighter again;
  - note 1's third answer on the first mockup ("the Timeline screen uses the same branch
    drawing") gives way to the Timeline rebuilt by notes 7, 8 and 21 and drawn in version 6,
    which shows each run as its own band. The owner approved version 6 and then agreed to this
    reading (below, "Settled by the owner").
- **The design is approved.** The owner: "This is it, approve all of these changes, begin any
  rework you need to get working on" (same record, last paragraph). The approved design is mockup
  version 6, docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html, with note 23's status icons as drawn;
  for the Home tile it is docs/design/mockups/phase-branches.html (notes 1 and 2), read from the
  bottom up as note 15 says. The owner's reference images and snippet are in
  docs/design/references/trial-view/ (the files dated 2026-10-16). Where a mockup and this spec
  differ, this spec governs and the Builder raises the difference.
- **Notes 28 to 31, on the other screens** (added in draft 3, 2026-10-16). The owner asked
  whether the other screens should change to match ("are their changes / additions you would make
  to the other tabs to remain consistent and coherent") and picked all four options offered:
  "Home tile phases (Recommended), Queue: why it matters, Team: what each is on, One phase marker
  everywhere" (the batch file, "Follow-up question and the owner's answer"). The owner then

**What this spec does not cover, and where it goes instead:**
- D7.1, recording each session's usage and context figures: a model change, specified and built
  in Personal-Org-Operating-Model under its own process. Done as model S-020, which froze the
  `usage` form J10 now reads.
- D7.2, Service-Desk adopting that model version: the Orchestrator's lane, approved by the owner.
  Done as L-0005 (2026-10-08).
- D1.3 / U3, running sessions from the desk: out of the first version (PROJECT.md §1).
- Spend: out of the first version (decisions/questions/P-001-spend-and-planning.md, question 1).
- A side-by-side period with Operations-Hub: dropped by the owner (P-001-build-path.md, answer 4:
  "the current desk isn't being used, so no need to run in parallel"). The draft's 7-day comparison
  is gone; the desk takes over the address once M3 is accepted.
- Setup outside the Builder's surface: listed under "Setup outside the Builder's surface" as Chief of
  Staff tasks.
- **The design lab** (MD, process A): mockup pages the Chief of Staff publishes and changes from the
  owner's words and screenshots. They are not desk code, need no pull request or deploy, and are
  outside the Builder's surface (AC43).
- **Two agent behaviours the owner asked for, which are model changes, not desk work** (owner's
  answers 5 and 6, P-001-design-discovery.md). They belong to Personal-Org-Operating-Model, queued
  with the discovery-step model change (track B). Nobody builds them into the desk:
  - agents stop at a safe point before their context window forces compaction mid-action ("I would
    want the agent to identify a stopping point without having to compact the conversation mid
    action");
  - pull requests lead with a plain-language, practical explanation ("the PRs themselves need to
    lead with a more practical explanation when proposed").

  The desk only shows what the records say: AC23's context figures, and a PR's title and link.
- **Records the lab's screens would use that no project keeps today** (2026-10-14). Each is a
  model change for the owner to rule on, in Personal-Org-Operating-Model under its own process,
  not desk work. Until one is made and adopted, the desk shows "not recorded" where it would
  need it, and never fills the gap with a value of its own (AC41):
  - **Chief of Staff sessions recorded like pipeline sessions** (ruling 5 of
    decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-1.md, accepted by the owner: "If it's something that you
    will be doing on every project moving forward I will want your agents captured as well").
    Without it, the Chief of Staff's tile, running log and Working now sheet show "not recorded"
    for its start, its time so far and its cost (AC49).
  - **A written plain-English summary for each recorded event**, by the Chief of Staff, stating
    only what the record supports (docs/design/README.md, the owner's direction of 10 Oct). Until
    then each event shows the standard summary built from its record's own fields (AC49).
  - **A recorded "who has the ball" state, and deploy approvals tied to the owner**
    (docs/design/README.md). Until then the desk builds who has the ball only from the records it
    already reads (AC49), and a deploy waiting for approval names no approver (AC52).
  - **A project milestone's "reached"**: docs/PROJECT.md section 3 lists each milestone's sprints
    but no record says it is reached. Until then the desk shows a project milestone as "work filed"
    or "no work filed yet", never "reached" (AC50).
  - **The verdicts a pull request still needs, and the Reviewer's statement before it is
    committed**, in a record the desk reads. Until then the "Verdicts to write" group shows "not
    recorded" and the desk has no Reviewer's text to prefill (AC52, AC53).
  - **A dependency between waiting items** ("after #54"), recorded where the desk reads it. Until
    then the queue names none (AC52).
  - **Why lines, standard-milestone lines, and each sprint's Context and Why** (added 2026-10-16,
    trial-view notes 2, notes 6, 21 and 27; the owner's answer on note 6: "A line in the project's
    plan (Recommended)", whose option text says "This is also a small change to the operating
    model, so every project does it"). Being drafted in Personal-Org-Operating-Model as model
    S-032 (its draft, Personal-Org-Operating-Model specs/S-032-why-and-context.md, which also adds a
    `Milestone:` line to each pull request body). The desk reads the forms AC61 gives. Until a
    project's records carry them, the desk
    shows "not recorded" (Status) or a flagged "not recorded yet" (Timeline), and never writes a
    why or a fact of its own (AC41).
  - **A `Milestone:` line and a `Context` section in each question file and card** (added in
    draft 3, 2026-10-16, AC66, note 29). S-032's draft adds them to pull requests only. For the
    Queue to name, from a record, the milestone a question or card unblocks and the facts behind
    it, the model would add the same two parts to `questions/_TEMPLATE.md` and the Orchestrator's
    card form: a further model change, to join S-032 or follow it. Until then the Queue shows
    "Unblocks: milestone not recorded" and "Context: not recorded" for a question, and a card's
    milestone only through its item (AC66).
- **"Ask for something in this project"** (the lab's "+" in the bottom bar, screen 6): not built.
  The owner asked whether it writes anything back ("Im assuming this doesnt write back into
  anything?", 13 Oct) and has not asked for it to be built. It would need the owner's say and a
  revision of this spec naming the record it writes (AC48).
- **Home's "Tell your Chief of Staff…" bar:** not built. The lab agreed it
  (docs/design/README.md) and the sandbox showed it deferred; the owner then dropped it ("I see no
  need for it if it’s not doing anything right now.", decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md, answer 1).
  Bringing it back would need the owner's say and a revision of this spec naming the record it
  writes (AC48).
- **A record of why a phase run began, for runs the plan's order builds** (added in draft 4,
  AC70): the model records a reason only through a gate's boundary decision; a run that follows
  from the plan's milestone order (Service-Desk's Design i2) has none, so the desk shows "why it
  went back: not recorded" there. Recording it, for example a line naming the ruling that started
  the run, would be a model change for the owner to rule on.
- **The owner's later-work picks from the audit of 2026-10-16** (an ask-or-jot box; an
  indicator of how long the agents believe their work will take, from their own estimates, never a
  set date; every ask of the owner filed with the model changes behind it; M3A planned at
  milestone level; the upkeep fixes): not this spec's work. They are recorded in
  decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-2.md, "The audit for missing elements", for planning
  as their own pieces of work after this batch.
- **Starting sessions from the desk** (the lab's dropped "New project" card): "No, maybe in the
  future - not now. Get rid of the new project function here since it doesnt apply." (13 Oct).

## Value targets this spec traces to
V1 to V5 are the brief's (docs/PROJECT.md §6, lines 177–181 at 3117150). V5 was added by the owner and is
confirmed in this form:

V5: Owner response time: the median time from a card appearing in a connected project to its answer being committed, counting only 07:00 to 22:00 Monday to Friday (America/Chicago), with a card still open at the window's end counted at its wait so far, tracked and shown on the desk, under 4 hours, over the first 14 days after launch, 2026-11-25 (14 days after V4's date; moves with it)

- **Ruling:** decisions/questions/P-001-build-path.md, question 3 (the measure, "tracked", and the
  goal of under 4 hours on weekdays).
- **Confirmed** (decisions/questions/P-001-s001-owner-questions.md, answer 1: "confirm as
  recommended"):
  - **the window and date:** the 14 days after launch, ending 2026-11-25;
  - **open cards:** an open card counts with its wait so far;
  - **the counting clock:** only 07:00–22:00, Monday to Friday, in America/Chicago. Nights and
    weekends don't count.

  These are J7's settings (`ownerTimeZone`, `v5Clock`). The footer (AC21) carries no
  "(provisional)" label.
- **Recorded in PROJECT.md §6** (Setup CS2, done): the V5 line above is docs/PROJECT.md:181 (line 170
  when CS2 was done).
- **The display time zone** is America/Chicago too (same ruling).
- **Terms for V5:**
  - **Card appearing:** the `time` of the dispatch-log `card` entry (J2).
  - **Answer:** the `time` of the dispatch-log `decision` entry for the card's answer record.
  - **Counted time:** the length of the overlap between [appearing, answer) and the `v5Clock`
    windows, in `ownerTimeZone` (J2 gives the computation).

## Terms used in this spec
- **Connected project:** an entry in `src/config/projects.json` (J7) that the read token can read.
  At launch there are two: Service-Desk (operating model v3: `status/`, `queue/`, `dispatch-log/`)
  and Personal-Org-Operating-Model (governed under v2.5.1, with `docs/sprints/` and
  `docs/LEDGER.md`, and no `status/`, `queue/` or `dispatch-log/`; checked 2026-10-06 at 677fe5a).
  The design is sized for up to 5.
- **Model of a project:** `v3` or `v2.5`, set in J7. It decides which records J9 reads.
  - v3 projects get the full pipeline view.
  - v2.5 projects get the **reduced view** (J9): sprint, headline and "next" from the ledger, with
    PRs, questions and CI. They have no per-item stages and no "running now", because v2.5.1
    records neither.
- **Card:** a file `queue/<item>-<gate>-<n>.md` directly under `queue/` in a v3 project, written by
  the Orchestrator. Its **answer** is `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<n>.md`, which is also the path the
  card names on its own `answer:` line (J2). Card files are kept after answering: on 2026-10-06 all
  12 cards in Service-Desk `queue/` have answer files.
- **Hold card** (from M2, AC46): a file `queue/ci-hold-<sha12>.md` in a v3 project, written by the
  Orchestrator when its loop has held for over 60 minutes because the latest governance run on the
  default branch's tip is not green (governance/checks/orchestrator_git.py:1198 and :1233–1245). It
  has no answer file: the owner fixes or re-runs the governance run. J2 says when it is open.
- **Merge card** (from M2, AC47): a file `queue/<item>-merge-conflict.md` or
  `queue/<item>-merge-by-hand.md` in a v3 project, written by the Orchestrator's merge gate when a
  finished item can't be merged without the owner (orchestrator_git.py:2737 and :2887–2910). It has
  no answer file: the owner merges the item by hand or changes it. J2 says when it is open.
- **Owner question:** a file `questions/<name>.md` (`<name>` holds no `/`) other than
  `questions/_TEMPLATE.md`, in either model, in the form of `questions/_TEMPLATE.md`
  (`# Ruling needed:`, `WHY:`, `OPTIONS:`, `RECOMMENDATION:`, `RISK CLASS:`, `DEFAULT:` and
  `TIMEOUT:`; J2).
  - **Its answer (v3 projects):** `decisions/questions/<name>.md`, whose first line is
    `Ruling: <choice>`. That is how questions are answered today:
    - Service-Desk e012edb added `decisions/questions/Q-005-sources.md`, whose line 1 is
      `Ruling: A`, and deleted `questions/Q-005-sources.md` in the same commit;
    - f45d10e did the same for `P-001-acting-route` and `P-001-planning-scope`.
  - **When it is open:** while the question file exists and no answer file exists.
  - **v2.5 projects:** there the ruling goes to the ledger, and the file is deleted
    (Personal-Org-Operating-Model f2ac482 recorded the rulings on Q-005 to Q-014 as L-0017 to L-0026
    and deleted `questions/Q-005-…` to `questions/Q-014-…`). So in v2.5 a question is open while its
    file exists.
- **Needs the owner (flagged):** exactly these four things, in a connected project.
  - An open card: a card with no answer file on the default branch, or an open hold card or merge
    card (J2, from M2).
  - An open owner question (above).
  - An open, non-draft pull request whose base is the project's default branch, except one the
    Orchestrator merges itself (head branch `item/<id>` in a v3 project: the merge gate merges item
    branches, model S-013 AC3). The owner's own PRs count: agents push under the owner's account,
    and GitHub won't let them request the owner's review (P-001-build-path.md, question 1).
    - **What the repositories show (2026-10-06, REST `pulls?state=all` and `pulls/<n>`; observed,
      C):** the two repositories hold 53 PRs (33 in Service-Desk, 20 in
      Personal-Org-Operating-Model).
      - Every one was opened by the owner's account (`shumpmaster`) against `main`, with no
        requested reviewers.
      - Every one has been merged, each by `shumpmaster`. None was merged by the Orchestrator.
      - None came from an `item/` branch.
    - So in practice every such PR waits on the owner's approval or merge.
  - A v3 status file in state `waiting-owner` whose card file is not yet on the default branch. This
    is a safety net against a missed decision, worded "waiting on you: card <gate>-<n> (card file
    not found)".
- **Activity:** anything shown but not flagged. That covers:
  - CI results.
  - Draft PRs, PRs whose base is not the default branch, and `item/` PRs in v3 projects.
  - Dispatch-log events: dispatches, outcomes, errors, retries and anomalies.
  - Sessions that run past their time limit.
  - Queue files that can't be parsed.
  - The Chief of Staff's `-notes.md` files.

  "A gate catching a problem, or an agent retrying, is activity" (PROJECT.md Part 2).
- **Open window:** a span during which the desk page is visible in the foreground on a device (the
  browser's page-visibility state is "visible").
- **Read successfully:** both of these hold.
  - The project's last poll call (J6) returned `state` `ok` or `unchanged`, and the page parsed
    it without error.
  - Every blob call that the poll's tree change needed has returned `ok`.

  An `unchanged` answer (every steady-poll request returned 304) counts: GitHub answered and
  nothing was new. These do not count: `partial`, `cant-read`, a 403 from the function, a Cloudflare
  error, a network failure and a page error (J6's `page` reason).
- **Quiet:** no flagged item in any connected project, **and** every connected project read
  successfully within the last 3 minutes.
- **Milestone accepted:** four steps, in order (P-001-build-path.md, question 0; the deploy route
  in "Toolchain and deploy route").
  1. The Reviewer's verdict on the milestone's pull request is PASS.
  2. The owner has merged it.
  3. The owner has approved desk-deploy's `preview` job for that merge, and it has deployed `main`
     to the preview.
  4. The owner has tried the preview and accepted the milestone.

  Problems found on the preview are fixed forward, in a new pull request, which also needs the
  Reviewer's PASS. MD adds its own steps before the owner accepts (AC44 steps 1–6).
- **Design lab:** MD's process A. Mockup pages the Chief of Staff publishes as private live pages,
  filled with a snapshot of real records and changed from the owner's plain-language requests and
  screenshots. No pull request or deploy; not desk code.
- **Sandbox view:** MD's process B. A view inside the desk, entered by `?sandbox`, that renders the
  chosen candidate designs against the desk's live data on the preview. It is built, reviewed and
  deployed like any other desk code.
- **Decision kind:** what sort of thing waits on the owner, read from the records: a card, an owner
  question (reversible, with a default; or irreversible, never defaulting, J2), or a pull request.
- **Unknown, stale and estimated values:** "not recorded", "not available", "not given", a value
  from a read older than 3 minutes, and a figure the desk derives rather than reads (for example a
  median over open cards' waits so far).
- **Phase and phase run** (from MD, AC50; model v3.3.0, S-023, L-0008): the model's phases are
  Discovery, Design, Build, Launch, Operate and Review. The desk shows the first four, then
  **Done**, which is not a phase: where a project stands once the owner passes its Launch gate.
  A **phase run** is one pass through a phase, labelled "<Phase> i<n>" (for example "Design i2"),
  exactly as the model's timeline names it (J11).
- **Milestone** (from MD, AC50): a phase's standard milestones (the model's checklist, the same for
  every project) and the project's own (docs/PROJECT.md section 3, each led by its plain-English
  name). Milestones are level two of the breakdown, the owner's "N+2".
- **Who has the ball** (from MD, AC49; ruling 1): who is moving a project's work right now, as the
  records the desk reads show it.
- **Queue** (from MD, AC52; ruling 6): everything that needs the owner (Terms, above) in one project,
  grouped by kind of action. There is no queue across projects.
- **Response sheet** (from MD, AC52, AC53): the sheet that opens over the queue when the owner taps
  an item: its options as choices, its facts prefilled, and, where the answer is a committed file,
  a prefilled text box.

## Acceptance criteria (scenarios; draft — the owner approves them by merging each milestone)
Each criterion is a plain scenario. "Within 5 minutes" is V1's figure. A "phone" screen is 360 CSS
pixels wide and a "desktop" one is 1280. The tests named in J1–J10 use the real samples quoted in J2
and J9.

### M1 — Read-only status page, behind Access, on the preview (as early as possible)
*Covers D2 and D2.2, D3.1, D3.2 (reading only), D3.3, D5 and D6.1 (preview). EXP-001, EXP-002 and
EXP-004 run on the preview after M1's merge.*

*Accepted 2026-10-07 (decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md): PR #40 and fix-forward PR #41,
Reviewer PASS on each (reviews/40/reviewer.md, reviews/41/reviewer.md), deployed to the preview by
the owner's approval, CS9 checked by the owner. The experiment outcomes are under "Experiments in
Build". AC1–AC15 below are unchanged except where M2 amends what they read (AC29 replaces check runs
with workflow runs).*

**What waits on the owner**
- AC1 [trace: V1 → AC1] — When a card is committed to a connected project's default branch while
  the desk is open on the owner's phone, then within 5 minutes the project's box shows it as
  waiting on the owner and the box moves to the top of the Universe screen.
- AC2 [trace: V1 → AC2] — When an open, non-draft pull request into a connected project's default
  branch exists, whoever opened it (the owner's own included), then within 5 minutes of it opening,
  or of a draft being marked ready, the desk flags it with its title and a link to it on GitHub.
  Within 5 minutes of it being merged, closed or turned back into a draft, it is no longer flagged.
  A draft PR, or a PR into any other branch, is shown as activity only.
- AC3 [trace: V1 → AC3] — When an owner question file (`questions/<name>.md`, not `_TEMPLATE.md`)
  is on a connected project's default branch, then within 5 minutes the desk flags it with its
  title (the text after `# Ruling needed: `). It is no longer flagged within 5 minutes of either
  of two things: its answer file `decisions/questions/<name>.md` being committed (v3), or the
  question file being deleted (either model).
- AC4 [trace: V1 → AC4] — When the owner opens the desk after it was closed, then the screen says
  "Checking…" and never "Quiet" until every connected project has been read successfully since
  opening. Once they all have, every flagged item in every connected project is shown.
- AC5 [trace: V1 → AC5] — When a connected project can't be read, then its box says "Can't read
  since HH:MM" with the reason in words, sorts above every box except those with flagged items, and
  the screen as a whole does not say "Quiet". The causes are:
  - token rejected or expired;
  - GitHub rate limit;
  - GitHub, Cloudflare or network error;
  - configured default branch not found;
  - more open PRs, CI runs or tree entries than one read can page through (`partial`, J1);
  - the page hit an error reading the project's records (`page`, J6);
  - no successful read for 3 minutes.
- AC6 [trace: V2 → AC6] — When a connected project has any of the following, then the desk shows it
  as activity in that project's box and does **not** flag it, count it as waiting, or move the box
  up for it. A failing default branch still orders the box by AC8's third rank; that is not a flag.
  - a failing CI result on its default-branch head (J1's reduction rule);
  - a draft PR;
  - a new dispatch-log event, including an agent's retry and an Orchestrator anomaly (J9);
  - a session running past its time limit;
  - a queue file that can't be parsed.
- AC7 [trace: V2 → AC7] — When a card's answer file is committed by any route, then within 5
  minutes the desk stops flagging that card while open, and on next opening it is not flagged.

**Seeing each project**
- AC8 [trace: V4 → AC8] — When the owner looks at the Universe screen, then each connected project
  has one box, ordered worst first: flagged, then can't-read, then default branch failing, then the
  rest by name. Each box shows:
  - its state: the flagged count, or "Can't read", or quiet;
  - its headline (J9);
  - its sprint (v2.5), or for v3 its open items with their stage names;
  - its default-branch CI result (J1).

  *From MD (AC54, 2026-10-14):* on Home the boxes sit in three groups, Active, Done, then Closed
  or not applicable, and this order holds within each group (AC35).
- AC9 [trace: V4 → AC9] — When the owner opens a v3 project's pipeline view, then it lists every
  item that has a status file (J9). For each item it shows:
  - its id and kind;
  - **its stage**, by name: 2 Shape, 3 Define, 4 Lock the checks, 5 Produce, 6 Launch, 8 Evidence;
  - **what is running now**: for `dispatched`, the role and how long it has run;
  - **what waits on the owner**: for `waiting-owner`, the card, flagged and linked;
  - **what is next**: for `ready`, the role the Orchestrator dispatches next; for `returned`, "the
    Orchestrator routes the <role>'s return (verdict <v>)";
  - "as of", the time of the item's latest dispatch-log entry.

  Finished items (`done`, `closed`) are folded into one line each, showing their outcome. A field
  the records don't give is shown as "not recorded", never guessed.
- AC10 [trace: V4 → AC10] — When the owner opens a v2.5 project, then the desk shows the reduced
  view (J9). It shows:
  - the open sprint's title;
  - the headline, the latest ledger entry's title;
  - "next", that entry's `licenses_next:` text;
  - the project's flagged PRs and questions, and its CI.

  "Items" and "running now" say "not recorded in this repository's records (operating model
  v2.5.1)".
- AC11 [trace: V1 → AC11] — When the owner taps a flagged card on the phone, then its full text is
  shown with every option it offers, rendered from its markdown, without sideways scrolling, plus
  the answer path the card names. (In M1 the desk links to the card on GitHub; answering from the
  desk is M2.)
- AC12 [trace: V1 → AC12] — When the owner taps a flagged owner question on the phone, then its
  full text is shown, rendered from its markdown, without sideways scrolling (J2). The view shows:
  - its title;
  - WHY;
  - every option under OPTIONS, with its letter and text;
  - the RECOMMENDATION, marked on its option;
  - RISK CLASS, REVERSIBILITY and BLAST RADIUS;
  - the DEFAULT, labelled "per the question file: defaults to <letter> at <raised time + TIMEOUT
    hours>" when the question is reversible, and "per the question file: never defaults" when it
    is irreversible (AGENTS.md §5).
    - The label only reports what the file says. Service-Desk does not enforce question timeouts:
      no script adopts a default. The desk must not imply that one will.

  A field the file lacks is shown as "not given". The question is still flagged.

**Trust, budget and the first deploy**
- AC13 [trace: V4 → AC13] — When any request reaches the desk's function without a valid
  `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` for the owner's email address, then it answers 403 and makes no GitHub
  call. "Not valid" covers a JWT that is missing, expired, for the wrong audience, wrongly signed, or
  for another email. A `POST` without `Content-Type: application/json`, or with a foreign `Origin`,
  is refused (J6's request checks). The preview's `pages.dev` address and its preview deployments
  prompt for Access login before anything is served. The Reviewer's security review of the login and of the
  read token's handling (D5.2) has passed before M1 is accepted.
- AC14 [trace: V4 → AC14] — *Limit: the hosting spend ceiling of $0 a month (PROJECT.md §5,
  decisions/questions/P-001-move-to-build-values.md). The trace checker accepts only V targets, so
  the formal trace is to V4, the replacement within its limits.* When the desk's open windows add
  up to 16 hours in a day across all devices, with 5 connected projects, then the function stays
  within the Free plan:
  - fewer than 10,000 function requests that day (our own margin, a tenth of Free's 100,000);
  - no Error 1027;
  - no Error 1102 on any call.

  The arithmetic and its assumptions are in "Facts relied on". A day with more than 16 window-hours
  is outside this scenario: only staying under Free's 100,000 is promised for it.
  - **Test:** a test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC14` drives the page's real poll scheduler with a fake
    clock and a fake function. It simulates 5 connected projects for 16 window-hours, with 10
    openings and 100 head changes. It asserts that fewer than 10,000 function calls are made and
    that none is a retry sooner than the next poll.
  - **Live check:** EXP-001 checks the 1027 and 1102 parts on the preview. After M1, the Chief of
    Staff reads the preview's daily request count from Cloudflare's dashboard.
- AC15 [trace: V4 → AC15] — When M1's pull request has passed the Reviewer and the owner has
  merged it, then:
  - once the owner approves it in the `preview` environment, desk-deploy's `preview` job deploys
    `main` to `https://service-desk-preview.pages.dev`, behind Access. The Builder never deploys and
    holds no secret.
  - EXP-001, EXP-002 and EXP-004 run on that deployed preview, and their results are recorded.
  - M1 is accepted only when the owner has tried the preview and the results are applied, each in a
    fix-forward pull request where code or config must change: J1's batch size, the launch route
    for M3, and the J7 values.
  - **As accepted:** no code or config had to change. `BLOB_BATCH` stays 25, the launch route is
    AC28's direct deploy into `needs-you`, and J7's link values stand (see "Experiments in Build").

### M2 — Answering cards, time asked, agent usage and context windows; CI and robustness
*Covers D3.2 and D4.1 (answering), D3.5, V5 and D3.6, plus what M1's acceptance carried forward:
CI from workflow runs (AC29) and the Reviewer's PR #41 findings (AC30–AC34); and the
Orchestrator's hold cards and merge cards (AC46, AC47), so nothing that waits on the owner shows
only as "can't parse". EXP-003 runs at M2's
start. M2 also records the EXP-002 and EXP-004 results in `docs/handover/experiments/` (Brief).*

- AC16 [trace: V4 → AC16] — When the owner picks an option on an open card (and optionally types a
  note), then the desk opens GitHub's new-file page for that card's repository and default branch.
  The page has the file name `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<n>.md` and J3's content filled in, and the
  owner commits it there; the desk itself writes nothing to GitHub. If the answer file already
  exists, the desk shows "already answered: <verdict>" and offers no link.
- AC17 [trace: V4 → AC17] — When the prefilled link would be longer than the cap
  (`linkCap` in J7, 6,000 characters; EXP-004 found every length up to 8,000 opened, so the cap
  keeps a margin), or that repository's
  `contentPrefill` is `false` (J7), then the desk instead shows the answer text with a "Copy" button
  and opens the new-file page with only the file name filled in.
- AC18 [trace: V4 → AC18] — When a connected project's `webCommitsToDefault` is `false` (J7), then,
  before opening the link, the desk shows: "This repository doesn't take commits to <default
  branch> from the web. On GitHub, choose 'Create a new branch' and then open the pull request
  GitHub offers; the card stays flagged until that pull request is merged."
  - The link itself is J3's URL, unchanged.
  - The desk creates no branch and opens no pull request.
  - The card stops being flagged only when the answer file is on the default branch (AC7). Until
    then the open pull request is flagged too (AC2).
- AC19 [trace: V4 → AC19] — When the owner picks an option on an open owner question in a v3
  project (and optionally types a note), then the desk opens GitHub's new-file page for that
  repository's default branch.
  - The page has the file name `decisions/questions/<name>.md` and J3's question content filled in:
    `Ruling: <letter>`, then `Question: questions/<name>.md`, then the note if there is one. The
    owner commits it there.
  - AC17's and AC18's fallbacks apply to it as to cards.
  - If the answer file already exists, the desk shows "already answered: <ruling>" and offers no
    link.
  - The question stops being flagged once the answer file is on the default branch (AC3).
  - Deleting the question file stays with the Chief of Staff, as today. That is a step in the
    operating notes (AC27), not the desk's.
  - **For a v2.5 project,** whose rulings go to its ledger, the desk shows the ruling text
    (`Ruling: <letter>` and the note) with a "Copy" button and the note "this repository records
    rulings in its ledger; give this to your session". The desk opens no link.
- AC20 [trace: V5 → AC20] — When the owner opens "Time asked of you" for a v3 project, then it
  lists each card raised in the last 30 days, with:
  - the time it was raised;
  - the time it was answered;
  - the wait between them;
  - whether the answer was a proxy (J2).

  Open cards show their wait so far. A summary line gives the count and median wait for the last
  14 days.
  - **Owner questions:** the view also lists every owner question raised in the last 30 days, open
    and answered, with the same four columns. "Raised" is the oldest commit of
    `questions/<name>.md`. "Answered" is the oldest commit of `decisions/questions/<name>.md`
    (v3 only, J2).
  - **Rulings with no question file** (asked in a session, like most of
    `decisions/questions/P-001-*.md`) have no raised time. They are not listed.
  - **A v2.5 project** shows "no cards in operating model v2.5.1" and its open questions only, with
    their wait so far. Its answered questions show as "answered questions: not recorded (operating
    model v2.5.1 records rulings in the ledger)", exactly as J2 says.
- AC21 [trace: V5 → AC21] — When the owner looks at the Universe screen, then a footer line shows
  V5 against its goal: "Median answer time, weekday cards, last 14 days: <h>h <m>m (goal under 4h;
  <k> cards)".
  - It covers every v3 project, computed as J2 says. An open card counts with its wait so far.
  - When no weekday card falls in the window, it says "no weekday cards in the last 14 days".
- AC22 [trace: V4 → AC22] — When the owner opens a v3 project's "Agents" view, then it lists every
  agent session of the last 14 days (J10), with:
  - the item and role;
  - the start (dispatch time) and end (outcome time);
  - the run time;
  - the result and verdict;
  - "retry" when it re-dispatches a role whose previous session ended in `error`.

  A session still running shows its elapsed time. Each item has a timeline: its sessions and its
  card waits on one time axis, in time order, readable on the phone.
- AC23 [trace: V4 → AC23] — When a session's record carries usage figures (J10, in model S-020's
  form), then the Agents view shows, for that session, behind the session's drill-down (tapping the
  session), not on its timeline row, so the timeline stays readable on the phone:
  - its tokens by kind (input, output, cache read, cache write: J10's `input_tokens`,
    `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`);
  - its turns (`num_turns`);
  - its run time as the agent tool reports it (`duration_ms`, rounded to the nearest second);
  - its peak context-window use as tokens and as a percentage of the window (`context_peak`,
    `context_window`), labelled "derived from per-turn usage" when J10's `derived` names it;
  - the peak also as a percentage of the compaction threshold (`context_peak` over
    `autocompact_threshold`; the owner's answer 5 is about stopping before compaction);
  - "would have stopped: yes" or "no" (`would_have_stopped`);
  - the compaction count (`compactions`);
  - the agent tool's cost estimate as "estimate $x.xx" (`cost_usd_estimate`, two decimals), never
    presented as a charge: the account is billed by subscription.

  **One rule for missing figures, everywhere in the desk:**
  - **"not recorded":** the outcome line has no `usage` key. That is a session that ran before
    model S-020 was adopted (L-0005), an Orchestrator `error` line (no session ran, S-020 AC2), or a
    line whose `usage` `record` dropped as malformed (S-020 AC2, "NOTE: usage dropped"). A single
    `usage` key with a value of the wrong kind (J10) is also "not recorded", for that figure only;
    the rest of the line still shows.
  - **"not available":** the figure is `null` in `usage`, a field the agent tool doesn't report
    (EXP-003), per the owner's O6 fallback (decisions/questions/P-001-o6-fallback.md, option A).
    A percentage is "not available" unless both its figures are present (not null), and also
    when its divisor (`context_window` or `autocompact_threshold`) is 0, since it can't be
    computed; the figures themselves still show. This covers
    the threshold percentage, "would have stopped" and the compaction count too: at the pinned
    agent-tool version all three are null on today's real lines.

  No paid route is used to get a missing figure.

**Carried from M1's acceptance** (P-001-m1-accept.md, "Carried to M2")
- AC29 [trace: V4 → AC29] — When the owner looks at a connected project's CI result (AC8's box,
  AC8's third rank, AC6's activity), then the desk has read it from the GitHub Actions workflow runs
  of the default-branch head (J1), for every connected project, and no longer from check runs.
  - **Why:** a fine-grained token offers no Checks permission. On private
    Personal-Org-Operating-Model the check-runs read answers 403, so its CI line reads "can't read
    checks" on the preview today (P-001-m1-accept.md).
  - **With the read token holding Actions: read** (Setup G3, the owner's step): both projects' CI
    lines show `passing`, `failing`, `running` or `none` by J1's reduction rule.
  - **Without it** (a 403 on the workflow-runs read only): CI shows "can't read CI (the read token
    needs Actions: read)", and the project stays readable, as J1 says.
  - **Covered on every head:** push runs and `workflow_dispatch` runs are both read, so a head the
    Orchestrator made (whose governance run is a dispatch run) shows its governance result, and a
    failing governance run there reads `failing` and ranks the box by AC8's third rank.
  - desk-deploy and Orchestrator runs are activity, not CI (J1).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC29` uses recorded workflow-runs responses, including the real
    samples quoted in J1 (e2ab9c5's dispatch-only head among them), a failing dispatch-run
    governance, and a 403.
- AC30 [trace: V1 → AC30] — When one connected project's records hold something the page can't
  parse or draw, then only that project's box shows the problem, and every other project's box, the
  screen's state line and the footer are drawn as normal.
  - **A failure while reading** that project shows as "Can't read since HH:MM" with reason `page`
    (AC5).
  - **A failure while drawing** its box shows that box as "Can't show this project: <error in
    words>". Each box is built separately, so one box's error never stops the others being drawn.
  - The screen is not "Quiet" while any box shows either message.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC30` gives one of two projects a record that throws in parsing,
    then one that throws in drawing, and checks the other project's box is unchanged each time.
- AC31 [trace: V1 → AC31] — When a record line or field doesn't have the shape J9 or J10 gives it,
  then the page names it in that project's `notes` (file, line and field) and skips it, and uses the
  rest of the record.
  - **Examples:** `stage = "three"`, `card = "6"`, a dispatch-log line whose `time` isn't a string,
    an `outcomes.jsonl` line whose `session` is a number.
  - A skipped field shows as "not recorded", never as a wrong value or as zero.
  - A skipped field never un-flags anything: a `waiting-owner` status whose `card` is skipped is
    still flagged through its card file (J2), or through Terms' safety net.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC31` covers each J9 field type with a wrong-typed value.
- AC32 [trace: V1 → AC32] — When the function answers 403 for a project (the Access session is
  gone), then the screen's state line says "Signed out — reload to sign in", even if building any
  box or the rest of the screen throws an error. The signed-out line is set before and apart from
  the boxes, and a render-error message never replaces it.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC32` makes a box throw while signed out and checks the line.
- AC33 [trace: V4 → AC33] — When `ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN` is entered with capital letters or
  surrounding spaces, then the function trims and lowercases it before comparing the JWT's `iss`
  with `https://<team domain>` (J8). A valid owner session is accepted, and a token another Access
  team issued is still refused (AC13).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC33` sets `ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN` to a mixed-case value with a
    trailing space.
- AC34 [trace: V1 → AC34] — When a project's reads fail with reason `page` several times in a row
  and the error changes, then its box shows the latest error's words. "Since HH:MM" keeps the time
  of the first failure in that run. The same holds for any reason whose words carry detail.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC34` raises two different page errors in two polls.
- AC46 [trace: V1 → AC46] — When the Orchestrator's hold card `queue/ci-hold-<sha12>.md` is on a v3
  project's default branch and still open (J2), then within 5 minutes the desk flags it in that
  project's box, like any waiting card, and no longer shows it as "can't parse" activity.
  - **What the desk shows:** its title, "the default branch's governance run is not green", its
    `tip`, `state` and `held for` values from the card's third line, and its "What to do" section
    rendered from its markdown, with a link to the repository's Actions page for governance.
  - **No answer link:** the Orchestrator reads no answer from it, so AC16's link is not offered.
    The desk says "No answer needed: fix or re-run the governance run; this clears once it passes."
  - **When it stops being flagged:** within 5 minutes of the latest governance run on the
    default-branch head concluding `success` (J1's 3a and 3b), or of a newer hold card replacing
    it, or of its file being deleted.
  - It counts in V5's and AC20's figures as nothing: it has no `card` log entry and no answer. It is
    not listed in "Time asked of you".
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC46` uses a hold-card fixture in the real form (J2) and covers:
    flagged while governance is `failed` or `running`; cleared when it passes; only the newest of
    two hold cards flagged; never offered an answer link.
- AC47 [trace: V1 → AC47] — When an Orchestrator merge card (`queue/<item>-merge-conflict.md` or
  `queue/<item>-merge-by-hand.md`) is on a v3 project's default branch and still open (J2), then
  within 5 minutes the desk flags it in that project's box, like any waiting card, and no longer
  shows it as "can't parse" activity.
  - **What the desk shows:** the item, the step ("merge conflict" or "merge by hand"), the item
    tip, and the card's "The decision", "Why" and "What to do" sections rendered from its markdown,
    with a link to the item branch `item/<item>` on GitHub.
  - **No answer link:** the Orchestrator reads no answer from it, so AC16's link is not offered.
    The desk says "No answer needed: merge item/<item> by hand, or change the item and let the
    merge gate try again."
  - **When it stops being flagged** (J2): within 5 minutes of the item's tip being merged into the
    default branch (by the gate, recorded in `status/merges.jsonl`, or by hand), of the item being
    `closed`, or of the card file being deleted. A new card for a newer tip replaces the old one.
  - It counts in V5's and AC20's figures as nothing, as for hold cards.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC47` uses merge-card fixtures in the real form (J2) for both
    steps and covers: flagged while open; cleared by a `merges.jsonl` line for its tip; cleared by
    a compare answer of `ahead` or `identical` (a by-hand merge); cleared when the item is
    `closed`; still flagged when the compare answer is `diverged` or the compare read fails; never
    offered an answer link.

### MD — Design: research, design lab, sandbox, then the chosen front end on the real back end
*Added by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-07 (decisions/questions/P-001-design-step.md), after the
owner found M1 "very drab... almost exclusively just text". Its criteria come from the owner's
eight answers (decisions/questions/P-001-design-discovery.md). They state outcomes and process,
not visual answers: those come from research Q-010 and the design lab. MD starts once M2 is accepted, because M2 brings the data a richer page shows
(answering, time asked, usage and context).*

**Inputs, read before any mockup:**
- research Q-010 (intake/requests/Q-010.toml): practical design for a phone-first status dashboard;
- research Q-011 (intake/requests/Q-011.toml): ways to send the owner a phone notification while
  the desk is closed;
- the discovery map (decisions/questions/P-001-design-discovery-notes.md): its questions A1–J3, the
  gaps it found, and its eleven design traps;
- the owner's eight answers (decisions/questions/P-001-design-discovery.md).

**What the desk must achieve** (each from an owner answer, quoted)
- AC35 [trace: V1 → AC35] — When the owner opens the desk on the phone, then the first screen is a
  calm overview grouped by project, one box each, from which a glance of a few seconds tells the owner
  which projects need them and where to tap for more.
  - Answers 3, 4 and 7: "I want to glance for a few seconds, but pick up enough information from
    that glance to know where to press into more details"; "bucketed by project"; "a clean, modern
    look, that facilitates a drill down workflow".
  - Waits stay grouped under their project, never pooled into one combined list.
  - Everything AC1–AC7 promise still holds on that screen: what is flagged, can't-read, "Checking…"
    and "Quiet".
  - **AC8 under MD:** each project's state (its flagged count, "Can't read", or quiet) and AC8's
    box order stay on the first screen. The rest of AC8's box contents (headline, sprint or open
    items, CI) may move one tap down (AC36). The design lab decides which, and the sandbox pull
    request says so (AC43).
  - **Changed by AC54 (trial-view notes 1, 2026-10-14):** the first screen's boxes sit in three
    collapsible groups, in this order: Active; Done; Closed or not applicable. AC8's box order
    (flagged, then can't-read, then default branch failing, then the rest by name) holds within
    each group. A box in a closed group is one tap down, but its group's header still says when
    something in it waits on the owner or can't be read, so the glance still tells the owner which
    projects need them.
  - Judged by the glance test (AC44, EXP-005).
- AC36 [trace: V4 → AC36] — When the owner taps a project's box, then the dashboard-level detail is
  there, one tap down: its pipeline or reduced view (AC9, AC10), its waits (AC11, AC12), CI, time
  asked (AC20), agents and usage (AC22, AC23). None of that detail is on the first screen beyond
  what AC35 needs.
  - Answer 7: "I want to naturally live at that car dashboard level - but not get hit in the face
    with it right off the bat."
  - Every view M1 and M2 built stays reachable; nothing is dropped by the redesign.
  - **Changed by AC55 (trial-view note 7, 2026-10-15):** inside the sandbox this detail is reached
    through the views AC55 rebuilds in the new design, never through a link to the accepted M2
    view. Without `?sandbox` the accepted views are unchanged.
  - **Changed by AC68 (trial-view notes 2, note 31, 2026-10-16):** inside the sandbox, a v3
    project's Pipeline view folds into the Timeline (its items as sprints, AC60), the Queue (its
    waits, AC11 and AC12) and the Team screen (who works on what, AC67), as the owner chose ("The
    old Pipeline page under "More detail" folds into the Timeline, since they now show the same
    thing."). "Every view M1 and M2 built stays reachable" holds for the sandbox through those
    screens; any fact of AC9's that none of them shows is listed in the sandbox pull request.
- AC37 [trace: V4 → AC37] — When the owner looks at a project, then it has a "where it's heading"
  line: the current phase, the upcoming work, and what that unlocks, built only from the project's
  records.
  - Answer 2, the owner's example: "Design phase right now - we need to establish KPIs, create
    visualizations, and trial their update cadence - once this is done you'll be able to view
    progress across the project".
  - **Never guessed** (AC9's rule): each part quotes or plainly restates a record, and names it. A
    part no record gives shows "not recorded"; the desk never writes a forecast of its own.
  - Which records feed each part (for example a v3 item's stage and next role, J9; a v2.5 ledger
    entry's title and `licenses_next:`, J9; a spec's milestone list) is settled in the design lab
    against real records, and written down in the sandbox pull request (AC43). If a part needs a
    record no project keeps today, that is a model change for the owner to rule on, not desk work.
  - **Changed by rulings 3 and 4 (2026-10-14, now model v3.3.0, L-0008):** for a v3 project the
    "current phase" is the phase run AC50 marks as now, and "the upcoming work" is that run's
    milestones with no work filed yet, by their plain-English names (docs/PROJECT.md section 3).
    A v2.5 project keeps J9's ledger sources.
  - **Changed by AC54 (trial-view notes 1, 2026-10-14):** on Home this line is no longer printed
    on the card. It opens, word for word, in a bubble from a small control on the card. It stays
    on the Status screen, under its title (AC48).
- AC38 [trace: V1 → AC38] — When the owner sees what waits on them, then each decision kind (Terms)
  is shown differently, and an irreversible owner question, or one whose file names a default
  (AC12's label), is told apart from a
  routine pull request at a glance. None is ranked or styled as more or less urgent than another.
  - Answer 6: "They need to be viewed differently, not more or less urgent."
  - The difference is never colour alone (the standards lookup in Q-010).
  - AC8's box order is unchanged: kinds don't reorder boxes.
  - **Changed by ruling 6 (2026-10-14):** inside a project the waits are grouped by the kind of
    action they need (AC52), and the groups are told apart by their titles and edges, not colour
    alone. Order within a group follows the time each item was raised, never urgency, so this
    criterion's "none is ranked" still holds.
- AC39 [trace: V4 → AC39] — When the owner opens a project's activity view, then it shows which
  agents are working now, on which item, why (the stage and route that dispatched them), and what
  changed (their latest outcomes and records), from the dispatch log and status files (J9, J10).
  - Answer 8: "I want to know all the agents that are working, what they're working on, why
    they're working on it, what has changed".
  - A v2.5 project shows its reduced view's facts, and "not recorded in this repository's records
    (operating model v2.5.1)" for agents, as AC10 does.
  - **Extended by ruling 1 (2026-10-14):** this is the Team screen, with who has the ball and each
    member's page (AC49). The Agents view (AC22, AC23) stays reachable from it (AC36).
- AC40 [trace: V1 → AC40] — When the owner opens the desk after time away, then items, events and
  agent results that arrived since the owner last looked are marked "new since you last looked", on the
  overview and inside each project.
  - **"Last looked"** is the last time the desk had been visible on this device for at least 10
    seconds in a row (an open window, Terms, of 10 s or more), taken at the end of that window. A
    shorter flash, such as switching through tabs, doesn't count. 10 s is my choice (C), a
    constant to tune in the lab. It is kept on the device only, in local storage under its own key
    `desk-seen-v1`, wrapped as J5's storage is.
  - Something is new when its time (a card's raised time, a log entry's `time`, a PR's
    `created_at`) is later than "last looked". Marks clear when the current window reaches 10 s and
    ends.
  - **Known gap:** a draft PR marked ready after the last look keeps its older `created_at`, so it
    is flagged but not marked new: J2's list doesn't give the ready time (C). With no
    record (first visit, private window, cleared storage), nothing is marked new, and the desk says
    "new-since markers off on this device". Nothing is stored on Cloudflare.
  - The marker never moves a box or reorders a list under the owner's thumb (discovery map E5 and
    trap 7).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC40` drives a fake clock and visibility changes: a window of
    5 s doesn't move "last looked", one of 10 s does, items before and after it are marked
    correctly, and failing storage shows "new-since markers off on this device".
- AC41 [trace: V1 → AC41] — When a value is unknown, stale or estimated (Terms), then it is never
  shown as a real value or as zero, in a number, a chart, a gauge or a summary line.
  - Unknown shows its words ("not recorded", "not available", "not given").
  - Stale data visibly degrades, and each project shows when it was last read.
  - An estimate is labelled as one.
  - No progress bar or track implies a percentage done (discovery map I3 and trap 4).

**Hard constraints** (the sandbox build and the final front end both meet them)
- AC42 [trace: V4 → AC42] — When the Reviewer checks MD's pull requests, then the design:
  - **uses no packages:** charts, icons and layout are hand-built CSS and SVG in `src/public/`,
    under the Toolchain's "no runtime dependencies" rule (discovery map H1);
  - **keeps the security headers:** `src/public/_headers` and the page's Content-Security-Policy
    are unchanged or stricter. So no web font, script or image from another origin (H2);
  - **stays within AC14's request budget:** the sandbox and the new views reuse the page's one poll
    scheduler and its data, and add no function call. AC14's test still passes. *Clarified
    2026-10-14:* J11's reads add no new kind of call, but they do add blobs to J6's existing blob
    calls, so a cold load can make about one more `/api/blobs` call. AC14's arithmetic (A3)
    counts it. *Clarified 2026-10-16:* J12 adds one request form to GitHub (a fixed GraphQL query)
    but no function call: it travels in the blob call, and A3 counts it;
  - **works at 360 px:** nothing scrolls sideways on a phone screen;
  - **survives the extremes of discovery map H5:** 5 connected projects, 12 waits, long titles, a
    360 px screen. Fixtures for each are in `tests/desk/fixtures/`, and a test marked
    `ac-test: S-001/AC42` renders each without error or overflow;
  - **respects reduced motion**, and never uses colour alone to carry meaning (Q-010's standards
    lookup).

**How MD runs**
- AC43 [trace: V4 → AC43] — When MD runs, then it goes through two stages, in order, and no
  self-design.
  - **A, the design lab** (run by the Chief of Staff; outside the Builder's surface; no pull
    request, no deploy).
    - **Where:** the Chief of Staff publishes mockups as Claude artifacts, private to the owner's
      claude.ai account by default (C). Only the owner sees them, and anyone the owner chooses to share one
      with. Their cost is nil on the owner's existing plan (E).
    - **What they hold:** a snapshot of real records from the connected projects, including text
      from the private Personal-Org-Operating-Model repository (titles, ledger lines, question
      text). The snapshot holds no token or secret. A mockup is never shared beyond the owner
      without their say.
    - **Buildable only:** each mockup keeps to AC42's constraints (no packages, the security
      headers, the request budget, 360 px, H5's extremes), so whatever the owner picks can be built.
      The Chief of Staff checks each mockup against AC42 before the owner picks.
    - The owner looks on the phone and asks for changes in plain language, screenshots or images.
      The Chief of Staff changes the mockup and republishes.
    - Each mockup cites the Q-010 findings it rests on, and shows the H5 extremes as well as today's
      data (discovery map trap 9, "designing on ideal data").
    - **The lab ends** when the owner picks a direction: one or at most three candidates to build.
      The Chief of Staff records that pick as a ruling in `decisions/questions/`, with links to the
      chosen mockups.
  - **B, the sandbox view** (the Builder, through the normal route: Reviewer PASS, owner merge,
    owner-approved preview deploy).
    - The desk at `?sandbox` renders the chosen candidates against the desk's live data on the
      preview.
    - Every sandbox screen carries a plain marker, "Sandbox: candidate design", so it can't be
      mistaken for the real view (discovery map H4). Without `?sandbox`, the desk is exactly the
      accepted M2 view.
    - The sandbox answers cards and questions through the same J3 links as the real view, and
      nothing else.
    - The pull request names, for each AC35–AC41 criterion, where the candidate meets it, and AC37's
      record sources. **From 2026-10-14** it does the same for AC48–AC56, and lists every place a
      screen shows "not recorded" with the model change that would fill it. **From 2026-10-16**
      it does the same for AC57–AC72 and J12.
  - **No self-design** (P-001-design-step.md, question 4). The desk can't edit its own layout,
    write code or deploy. It holds no write credential, as before.
- AC44 [trace: V4 → AC44] — When MD is to be accepted, then, in order:
  1. the owner has picked a direction in the lab (AC43);
  2. the Reviewer has passed the sandbox build, and it is deployed to the preview;
  3. **the owner has picked one candidate** on the preview's sandbox, against live data. The Chief
     of Staff records that pick as a ruling in `decisions/questions/` (CS10), naming the candidate.
     With only one candidate built, the owner confirms it or sends it back to the lab;
  4. **the chosen front end is merged with the real back end:** in a pull request the Reviewer
     passes, the candidate step 3 recorded becomes the desk's default view, the sandbox and the
     unchosen candidates are removed, and the owner approves its preview deploy. The owner then
     sees exactly how the desk will run at launch;
  5. the owner has run the 5-second glance test on the preview, and it passed
     (experiments/EXP-005-md-glance-test.md);
  6. CS9's gate check;
  7. the owner accepts MD.

  M3's new views (AC24, AC25) are then built in the chosen design.
- AC45 [trace: V1 → AC45] — When MD is accepted, then the channel for phone notifications while the
  desk is closed has been decided from Q-011's result, and recorded as an owner ruling.
  - Answer 1: "Phone notifications". Polling only while the desk is open can't reach the owner
    when it is closed (discovery map gap 1).
  - **The baseline that already exists** (not desk work): Service-Desk's Orchestrator opens one
    issue labelled `needs-you`, titled "Needs you: <item> <gate>-<n>", for each waiting v3 card,
    assigns it to the owner, and closes it once the card no longer waits (model S-017 AC3;
    governance/checks/orchestrator_git.py:3057–3060 and :3317; orchestrator.yml:474–480). The
    GitHub mobile app notifies the owner of the assignment (C; Q-011 answers it). Real cases: issues #33 ("Needs you:
    P-001 failure-5") and #34 ("Needs you: P-001 stop-6"), both assigned to `shumpmaster`.
  - **Its gaps:** it covers only cards in Service-Desk. These reach the owner only when they open
    the desk:
    - owner questions (`questions/<name>.md`), in either project;
    - pull requests waiting for the owner's review or merge;
    - anything in a v2.5 project (Personal-Org-Operating-Model), which has no Orchestrator.
  - **Only extra channels need an exception or a model change.** For example:
    - a Web Push sender for the desk would need a server-side sender running while the page is
