      an open "Latest" bubble, and opening "Latest" by tap or by hover closes an open summary
      bubble;
    - **(b)** that the bubble's text equals AC37's line (v3) and AC10's headline (v2.5) exactly;
      that the line is not printed on the card; that the bubble opens on hover, on a tap and on
      keyboard focus, and closes on Escape, a second tap and a tap outside; that a tap on the
      control does not open the Project screen; that a bubble closed with Escape stays closed
      while focus stays on its control; and that at 360 px the bubble lies wholly inside the
      viewport, unclipped;
    - **(c)** two columns at 360 px and at 414 px, and H5's extremes without error or overflow
      (AC42's test);
    - **(d)** one fixture for each of the group rules above, the closed `re-scope` case among
      them; the groups' order; AC8's order within a group; the starting states; a closed group
      holding a waiting item, whose header says "1 waiting on you"; a closed group holding a
      project that can't be read; that opening and closing survive a reload through
      `desk-home-groups-v1`; and that failing storage gives the starting states with no error.

**The project screens, after the owner's trial-view notes** (added 2026-10-15)
*From notes 6 to 15 of the same record (decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-1.md), read as
its "How it is read" lines read them, and the owner's answer on note 7. Notes 10 to 13 and 15
change AC49, AC51, AC52 and AC53 in place; the two criteria below carry the rest. Where AC48 and
these differ, these govern.*

- AC55 [trace: V4 → AC55] — When the owner opens a project's Status screen in the sandbox, then a
  "Your last action" line, just above the bar that says whether anything waits on the owner, names
  the owner's most recent recorded action in that project, and "More detail" opens the Pipeline,
  Time asked and Agents views drawn in the new design inside the sandbox.
  - **"Your last action"** (note 6: "Right above that bar, can you record my last action or
    decision just so I can orient to it quickly").
    - **What it names:** the latest, by its time, of the owner's recorded actions in that project
      the desk reads:
      - **a card answer:** the dispatch log's `decision` entry (J2), in J9's decision words
        ("P-001: you answered dor-4 with build"), at that entry's `time`, linked to
        `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<card>.md`;
      - **a ruling:** a record `decisions/questions/<name>.md` whose line 1 starts `Ruling:`,
        or `Decision:` for a target-user profile approval (decisions/README.md), in its first
        line's words without that word (as AC52's "Done today"), at the time of its oldest
        commit (J2's history read, as AC20), linked to the file. Every other file there (a
        `-notes.md` file, a Critic's verdict such as
        `P-001-milestones-adoption-critic-1.md`) is not the owner's and is ignored, never shown
        (plan review round 1, B1).

      A record whose second line is a `Proxy:` line is marked "(proxy)": it was made at the
      owner's request, in the owner's words.
    - **Its form:** "Your last action: <words> · <time>", the time as J9 gives times, then the
      record's code. The words wrap.
    - **A long ruling line is clipped to three lines** (the owner's follow-up ruling,
      decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-answers-followup.md, answer 2: "Desk clips long lines
      (Recommended)"; trigger: the owner's own record decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md,
      whose line 1 runs to about 1,800 characters). When a ruling's words take more
      than three lines at the line's width, they show their first
      three lines, faded at the cut, with the whole line one tap away, as AC54(a)'s summary does:
      - the cut never changes the words, only how many show: the page holds the whole line, word
        for word, nothing is shortened, reworded or given an ellipsis, and nothing scrolls inside
        the line. Whether it runs past three lines is judged at its width as drawn, and again when
        that width changes. The fade matches the background in both themes;
      - the time and the record's code still show, after the three lines and outside the
        button: they are not part of the control, and a tap on them does not open the bubble (C);
      - three lines or fewer: the words show whole, with no fade and no control, in the one-line
        form above, "Your last action: <words> · <time>" then the code, the time and code
        following the words on the same line as for a card answer (C, from 2026-10-16). A short
        line takes its own height; AC54's "every tile the same height" does not apply here (C);
      - the three lines are a control of their own, as AC54(a)'s are: a real button, in the tab
        order, opened by a tap, a click, or Enter or Space, with the accessible name "Show the
        whole of <record's code>" (C);
      - it opens a bubble holding line 1 only, the whole of it, word for word and never cut. Any
        "(proxy)" mark is shown outside the bubble, after the clipped words, never inside it (C).
        The bubble sits wholly inside the
        viewport at every width down to 360 px, placed above or below to fit, and clear of the
        project screens' bottom bar. It closes as AC56 requires of every sheet and bubble, and a
        second tap on the control also closes it. Only one bubble is open at a time;
      - **a bubble taller than the viewport** (C) is held to the viewport's height, less a margin,
        above the bottom bar, and scrolls up and down inside itself, never sideways. Its "Close"
        control stays in view while it scrolls. The tests use viewports of 360×740 and 414×896
        CSS pixels. AC54's "Latest" and whole-summary bubbles follow the same rule;
      - **while the bubble is open, a refresh leaves it as it is** (C; build review 1, B1): when
        a refresh would change the line, for example a poll bringing a new ruling whose time is
        not yet read, so that the line would show "not available yet", the open bubble stays on
        screen unchanged, at the same scroll position. It adds one plain line, "This has changed since you opened it. Close this to see the latest.", as a sheet says so when its subject leaves (AC56). It
        closes only by the owner's own ways (AC56's close rules, or a second tap on the control).
        Once it is closed, the line shows its new state;
      - the record is never edited: the desk writes nothing, and the clip is on display only.

      The same holds for each ruling row in AC52's "Done today", the hold included (C, draft
      5; build review round 2, R2-B1): while a row's whole-line bubble is open, a refresh that
      would remove or change that row leaves the bubble on screen, unchanged and at the same
      scroll position, with the same notice, "This has changed since you opened it. Close this to see the latest.".
      The named example is the day turning at midnight in `ownerTimeZone`, when the next poll
      would drop the row from the fold. It closes only by the owner's own ways; once the owner
      closes it, the fold shows its new state.
    - **The owner's merges are not in it:** no record the desk reads holds them (AC49; J1 reads
      only open pull requests). The reason is one tap away: "your merges aren't recorded in what
      the desk reads".
    - **"not recorded"** when no record gives an action. A v2.5 project shows "not recorded
      (operating model v2.5.1 records rulings in the ledger)", as AC20 does.
    - **Not yet read:** until every ruling's time in that project has arrived, the line shows
      "not available yet" rather than an older action that may not be the latest (AC41).
  - **"More detail", rebuilt in the new design** (note 7: "The more detail section on the bottom
    of that same page takes me back to an outdated version?"; the owner's answer: "Rebuild in new
    design (Recommended)").
    - **Changed by AC68 (trial-view notes 2, note 31, 2026-10-16):** for a v3 project, "More
      detail" no longer offers Pipeline; Time asked and Agents stay. A v2.5 project keeps its
      "Reduced view and waits" (AC68 says why).
    - Inside the sandbox, "More detail" opens three views, each drawn in the sandbox's shared
      look (AC48) under AC42's constraints, as a full screen in the project with its bottom bar
      and both back controls (AC56):
      - **Pipeline:** AC9's items (AC10's reduced view for a v2.5 project), with AC11's and
        AC12's waits;
      - **Time asked:** AC20's lists and summary line;
      - **Agents:** AC22's sessions and per-item timeline (it keeps its time axis) and AC23's
        usage and context figures.
    - **The same facts:** every value, word and "not recorded" those criteria give, from the same
      records, with nothing added or dropped. Only the look changes.
    - **No link from the sandbox to the accepted views.** Without `?sandbox` the accepted M1 and
      M2 views are exactly as they are (AC43), and AC36 still holds there.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC55` checks:
    - "Your last action" for a fixture where a card answer is latest, one where a ruling is
      latest, a proxy ruling, a target-user profile approval, a project with neither ("not
      recorded"), a v2.5 project, and a project whose ruling times have not all arrived ("not
      available yet");
    - that a Critic's verdict file in `decisions/questions/` (line 1 `Verdict: FAIL`), newer than
      every ruling and card answer, is never named, and never shown in "Done today" (AC52);
    - that the line sits just above the waiting bar, with its link and code;
    - from 2026-10-16 (the follow-up ruling, answer 2): with the owner's real record
      decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md as the latest ruling, at 360×740 and 414×896,
      that "Your last action" shows the first three lines of its words, faded at the cut, then
      its time and code; that the control has its name and opens a bubble whose text equals the
      words exactly, from "11 Ruling: 1 I see no need…" to the end of line 1; that Escape, a tap
      outside, "Close" and a second tap each close it, with focus back on the control; that the
      bubble, where it is taller than the viewport, is held within it less a margin, clear of the
      bottom bar, scrolls up and down and never sideways, with "Close" in view at the top and
      after scrolling to the end; that the time and code are outside the control; and that a
      ruling of three lines or fewer shows whole, with no fade and no control;
    - that each rebuilt view, rendered from the fixtures AC9–AC12 and AC20–AC23's tests use,
      shows the same set of values and words as the accepted view does for those fixtures;
    - that no element of the sandbox links to an accepted M1 or M2 view.

- AC56 [trace: V1 → AC56] — When the owner moves about the sandbox, opens a sheet or bubble, and
  the desk refreshes, then a back control goes one screen back beside the one to Home, every sheet
  and bubble has a visible close control, and a refresh leaves the screen exactly as the owner left
  it.
  - **Back one screen** (note 8: "The back button takes you all the way back to the home page,
    please introduce a second back button that just brings you back 1 page if possible.").
    - Every screen but Home keeps its control to Home, and gains beside it a control that goes
      back one screen: to the screen the owner was on before, in the sandbox's own history of
      screens. Its accessible name is "Back" and the Home one's is "Home" (C).
    - Sheets and bubbles are not screens: opening one adds nothing to that history.
    - When there is no earlier screen in that history (the sandbox was opened on this screen),
      the control isn't shown (C).
  - **Every sheet and bubble can be closed** (note 9: "This popup screen has no easy way to just
    close out the pop up, please make sure that exist on every pop up").
    - This covers every pop-up in the sandbox: the response sheet, the pull request sheet, the
      summary sheet, the Working now sheet, the info screens, AC54's "Latest" bubble and AC54's
      whole-summary bubble on Home, and, from 2026-10-16, the whole-line bubbles of AC55's "Your
      last action" and AC52's "Done today".
    - Each has a visible close control at its top, with the accessible name "Close" and a touch
      target of at least 44 by 44 CSS pixels (C). Each also closes on a tap outside it and on
      Escape.
    - On closing, focus goes back to the control that opened it. For the "Latest" bubble that
      does not reopen it (AC54: closed with Escape, it stays closed while focus stays there).
      When a held whole-line bubble (AC55) closes and its control is gone, because the line or
      row now shows its new state, focus goes to the line or fold heading it belonged to: the
      "Your last action" line, or "Done today"'s header (C, draft 5).
  - **A refresh keeps the screen as the owner left it** (note 14: "it seems like every time the
    app refreshes it will revert popups in utilizing or it will close pages").
    - **A refresh is every redraw the owner did not cause** (plan review round 1, B2): the
      minute's poll; the sandbox's 15-second timer tick; a history read or any other read
      arriving; and a redraw with no new data at all.
    - **The 15-second tick updates only the text that depends on the time** ("time so far",
      "waiting 12 min", "last read 40 s ago" and the like), in place. It never redraws the whole
      view (this answers the plan review's open question; the sandbox's whole-view redraw every
      15 seconds is what this replaces).
    - Through it, these all stay as they were: the screen; any open sheet or bubble; the scroll
      position of the screen and of an open sheet; the focused control; anything typed in a text
      box, with its cursor; any picked choice; and every fold or group the owner opened or closed
      (AC54's groups, AC50's open phase, AC51's milestone details and standard milestones box,
      AC52's "Done today"; from 2026-10-16, AC57's folded phase boxes and AC57's and AC59's
      gathered standard milestones, and AC60's sprint lists; from draft 3, AC66's Context folds).
      The Timeline scrolls to Now only
      when the owner opens it (AC59), never on a refresh.
    - New data fills in around them. Only the owner closes what the owner opened.
    - **When the thing a sheet is about leaves** (its answer arrives, or a pull request closes),
      the sheet stays open and says so, in the tracker's words (AC52), until the owner closes it.
      What the owner typed stays.
    - Nothing reorders under the owner's thumb (AC40).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC56` drives every kind of refresh with a fake clock and
    changed fixtures: the minute's poll, the 15-second tick, a history read arriving, and a redraw
    with no new data. It checks:
    - **back:** a path Home → Status → Queue → a sheet → Timeline; then "Back" twice gives Queue
      and then Status, "Home" gives Home, and no "Back" shows on a screen opened first;
    - **close:** for each sheet and bubble (from 2026-10-16 including both whole-line bubbles,
      "Your last action"'s and "Done today"'s), the close control is present at the top with its
      name, and each of the control, a tap outside and Escape closes it, with focus back on its
      opener;
    - **refresh,** in four steps, one per screen, since the states sit on different screens.
      In each step every kind of refresh above runs, and each state stays exactly as it was,
      with the changed data shown:
      1. Home: the Done group opened, the Active group closed, and a "Latest" bubble open;
         1b. Home again, the same groups, with a card's whole-summary bubble open in place of
         the "Latest" bubble (only one bubble is open at a time, AC54);
      2. Status: the screen scrolled, and focus on its "More detail" control;
         2b. Status again, with "Your last action"'s whole-line bubble open (from 2026-10-16);
         and, from draft 4, with that bubble open and scrolled part way, a poll bringing a new
         ruling whose time is still unread: the bubble stays open, unchanged and at the same
         scroll position, with the line "This has changed since you opened it. Close this to see the latest."; once
         the owner closes it, the line reads "not available yet", and then the new ruling once
         its time arrives;
      3. Queue: a response sheet open and scrolled, a choice picked, text typed with the cursor
         mid-text, and "Done today" opened; then a poll in which its item is answered: the sheet
         stays open, says so, and keeps its text;
         3b. Queue again, "Done today" opened, with a ruling row's whole-line bubble open and
         scrolled part way (from 2026-10-16); and, from draft 5, the same with the clock run past
         midnight in `ownerTimeZone` and a poll that drops the row: the bubble stays open,
         unchanged and at the same scroll position, with the notice; once the owner closes it,
         the fold shows its new state and focus is on "Done today"'s header. Step 2b's case
         likewise checks that focus goes to the "Your last action" line when the control is
         gone;
      4. Timeline: a milestone's detail and the standard milestones box opened. *From
         2026-10-16 (AC59, AC60):* the Timeline scrolled away from Now, one milestone's sprints
         opened and a "Not tied to a milestone" line's sprints opened: each stays as it was, and
         the screen does not scroll back to Now;
         4b. Status (AC57): one phase box folded and another opened from folded, and an earlier
         run's gathered standard milestones opened; each stays as it was, and the branch
         drawing's dots stay level with their phase names.

      It also checks that the 15-second tick changes only the time-dependent text: every other
      element of the view is the same element before and after it.

**The Status and Timeline screens, after the owner's trial-view notes 2** (added 2026-10-16)
*From the owner's notes of 2026-10-16, notes 1 to 27 of decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-2.md,
read as its "How it is read" lines read them, later readings replacing earlier ones where they say
so; and from the design the owner approved there, mockup version 6
(docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html) and, for the Home tile,
docs/design/mockups/phase-branches.html. The owner's words are quoted exactly as written, typos
included. Where AC48, AC50 or AC51 and these differ, these govern. Every count, date and word
these screens show comes from a record the desk reads (J9, J10, J11, J12) or is one of the desk's
fixed labels quoted here; where a record is missing the screen says so (AC41). Nothing here is
built without `?sandbox`: the accepted M1 and M2 views stay exactly as they are (AC43).*

**Terms for these criteria:**
- **Run, on the branch drawing** (C): a column of phase runs. Take the model's timeline's phase
  runs in its order (J11). The first starts run 1. Each later phase run whose phase is the same as,
  or comes before, the phase of the run just before it starts a new run: the work went back. Every
  other phase run carries on in the run it follows. Runs are numbered from 1 in that order and
  shown as "Run <k>". For Service-Desk at 3117150 (J11's fixture): run 1 is Discovery i1, Design i1
  and Build i1; run 2 is Design i2, Build i2 and Launch i1.
- **Finished and standing:** a phase run before AC50's now run whose phase has no later run.
  At 3117150, Discovery i1.
- **Finished, then gone back over:** a phase run before the now run whose phase has a later run.
  At 3117150, Design i1 and Build i1.
- **Still to come:** a phase run after the now run. At 3117150, Build i2 and Launch i1.

- AC57 [trace: V4 → AC57] — When the owner opens a project's Status screen in the sandbox, then
  its phases read from the bottom up, Discovery at the bottom and Done at the top, each in its own
  box with its phase's colour, that folds on a tap and lists every run of that phase; one column of
  dots per run sits inside the boxes and branches right where the work went back; and every
  milestone of every run shows its state, in words, and its why line.
  - The owner's words:
    - note 1: "the app shows us back in a design iteration but also implies build is done?
      Chronologically that can’t be true", and its answer: "anywhere we have chronological phases
      like this, we should incorporate a branch style visual. By expanded out a second, third,
      fourth (however many are applicable) dot set to the right of the original milestone dot";
      on the mockup: "This is the exact picture I had in mind. Every box list it’s runs as well,
      but in smaller de emphasized text";
    - note 2: "when a phase was completed but branched off, make the dot fill lighter than a fully
      completed phase";
    - note 6 (the owner's point 4): "when we show the standard milestones that we DONT do for a
      particular project, it doesnt easily translate to what we DO take on - in chronologgical
      order. The other thing is that every milestone within the phases should have a short snippet
      of "why this phase was included or not included"";
    - notes 10 to 12: "maybe soften the text color for not utilized milestones and add a simple
      light directional line that ties the milestones/phases from the bottom to the top to
      indicate direction";
    - note 15: "I was always envisioning phases > milestones > tasks to read bottom up. So
      discovery would be at the bottom and the final thing done would be at the top.";
    - note 19: "reduce the weight on the line connecting things";
    - note 25: "Lets do B - simply because i like the ability to cleanly collapse and draw
      distinction between phases."
  - **Read from the bottom up** (note 15, replacing note 12's reading). From the top of the screen:
    Done, Launch, Build, Design, Discovery. Inside a box, the latest run sits at the top and the
    earlier runs below it. Inside a run, the first milestone sits at the bottom and the last at the
    top: the run's standard milestones at the bottom, in the model's order (`<Phase>-1` lowest),
    then the project's own milestones above them, in the order docs/PROJECT.md section 3 lists
    them.
  - **Each phase in its own box** (note 25, option B of note 20):
    - the box carries its phase's colour as a stripe down its left edge (note 13; AC48's phase
      colours), and its phase's name in words. Done's box is dashed with no stripe;
    - its header holds the phase's name and its state word (AC50, as changed by note 1): for
      Service-Desk at 3117150, Discovery "Finished", Design "Now · run 2", Build "Run 2 still to
      come", Launch "Not started", and Done "After the Launch gate" until the project is Done (C,
      the mockup's words);
    - the now phase's box keeps its place and is marked there, with a gold border and its state
      word (AC50's "emphasize it inline");
    - **it folds** (note 25): the phase's name is a real button, in the tab order, opened by a tap,
      a click, or Enter or Space, with the phase's name as its accessible name and its folded or
      open state as its accessible state and as a mark beside the name, never colour alone. It
      folds the box to its one header line and opens it again. Done has nothing to fold;
    - **how boxes start** (C, as the mockup draws them): open, except a phase whose every run is
      finished and standing (at 3117150, Discovery), which starts folded. Folding one box never
      moves or reorders any other, and a refresh keeps each box as the owner left it (AC56).
  - **The branch drawing** (notes 1, 2, 10, 16, 20 and 25):
    - **one column of dots per run** (Terms), run 1 furthest left and each later run one step to
      the right, at the same position across in every box. The dots sit inside the boxes, on the
      left (option B). Each dot is level with its box's phase name, centred on it (within 1 px),
      and stays so when any box folds or opens and when the width changes;
    - **which dots:** one for each phase run, in its run's column, in its phase's box. After the
      latest run's last phase run, that column carries on with a still-to-come dot in each later
      phase's box, through Done (C, as drawn: at 3117150, run 2 has dots at Design, Build, Launch
      and Done);
    - **each dot's mark, with its words in the run's line in the box (never colour alone):**
      - finished and standing: filled;
      - finished, then gone back over: a lighter fill with an outline (note 2), its run reading
        "finished, then gone back over";
      - the now run: a gold ring, when it holds the next milestone to reach (rule 2 below), so the
        ring means the same on a run as on a milestone. When AC50's now run comes from its second
        branch (every project milestone has work filed, so the now run is the one holding the
        latest recorded step), no milestone is next to reach: the now run's dot is then a
        half-filled circle, "now: work under way", and no gold ring shows (C; plan review round 2,
        N5);
      - still to come: an empty ring;
      - Done: an empty ring until the project is Done (AC50), then filled.

      With no now run (AC50's "not recorded"), every phase run's dot is a dashed ring and its words
      say "not recorded";
    - **the lines** (notes 12, 15 and 19): within a column, a thin, light line joins each dot to
      the next one up, crossing the gap between boxes, with a small arrowhead half way that points
      up. A line into a still-to-come dot is dashed. Where a run starts, one curve joins the last
      dot of the run before it to the new run's first dot, out to the right. There are no other
      slanted pieces. The lines are as thin and light as version 6 draws them after note 19: 1.25 px
      at 70% opacity (docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html:581 and :587);
    - **each run's column** has its own shade (run 1 neutral, run 2 gold, as drawn; a third or
      later run takes a further shade, C), and is named in words on every run in the boxes
      ("Run 2"). The drawing itself is hidden from assistive technology: the words carry it;
    - **a key** above the boxes, each entry with its mark (plan review round 2, B2): for the runs'
      dots, "finished · finished, then gone back over · now: holds the next milestone to reach ·
      still to come"; for the milestones, "reached · work under way · next to reach · no work
      filed yet · not reached · not taken on, or not recorded". The gold ring is named "next to
      reach" in both, never "now" alone.
  - **Every run in its box** (note 1's mockup answer 2):
    - each run opens with its tag, "Run <k>" (here and on every screen, <k> is the run in Terms
      above, the column, never the phase's iteration; plan review round 1, N4), then "<Phase> i<n> ·
      <state words>": "finished",
      "finished, then gone back over", "now", "still to come" or "not recorded" (for example
      "Run 1 · Build i1 · finished, then gone back over");
    - the latest run reads at full size; every earlier run is smaller and quieter: its text one
      role down on AC62's type scale and in the muted colour, never a size of its own.
  - **Every milestone of every run, in order** (note 6): the project milestones the model's
    timeline places in that run (J11), and every standard milestone of that phase, with its state
    for that run.
    - **Standard milestones:** a line "Standard milestones · <count by state>" ("4 reached · 4 not
      recorded"), then each one by name, its code after, and its state word: the model's (reached,
      not reached, not recorded, carried over or not needed), or "Skipped" where AC61's plan line
      says so. In the latest run of a phase every one is listed. In an earlier run those whose
      state is "not recorded" are gathered behind one line, "<n> not recorded, one tap away",
      which opens them in place on a tap and folds them on a second (C, as drawn for Build i1 and
      Design i1). Every count is a count of the model's lines.
    - **Project milestones:** each by its plain-English name, its code after, and its state words:
      "Work filed: <its sprints>" ("Work filed: PR #44, #52"), "No work filed yet" or "Dropped"
      (AC50), and, for the one milestone rule 2 below gives the gold ring, "· next to reach" after
      them ("No work filed yet · next to reach"; plan review round 2, B2). Never "reached" (AC50).
      Its sprints are the plan's: a pull request that names the milestone only in its own
      `Milestone:` line (AC61 (c)) never changes these words or its mark (AC60).
    - **Each milestone's mark, beside its words.** The rules are taken in this order, and the
      first that fits gives the mark (plan review round 1, N5), so no milestone fits two:
      1. skipped (AC61's plan line), not needed, or dropped: a dashed ring;
      2. **next to reach:** the gold ring, on one milestone only: the first, in section 3's order,
         of the now run's project milestones that are not dropped and have no work filed (at
         3117150, MD2). This is AC59's meaning of the gold ring; the owner ruled it holds on every
         screen ("Next to reach, everywhere (Recommended)", 2026-10-16);
      3. any other milestone of a run still to come: an empty ring, its state word "Still to come"
         in place of the model's "not recorded" (C);
      4. reached or carried over, in a run gone back over, and a project milestone with work filed
         in a run gone back over: the lighter fill (note 2's dot, carried to milestones; its words
         never say "reached" for a project milestone, AC50);
      5. reached or carried over: filled;
      6. **work under way:** a project milestone with work filed, in the now run or a run finished
         and standing: a half-filled circle, as on the Timeline (the same ruling; the mockup's
         gold ring on MD1 is not carried);
      7. no work filed yet: an empty ring;
      8. not reached: filled red, with "Not reached";
      9. not recorded: a dashed ring.
    - **The tie line** (notes 12, 15, 19): a run's milestones are joined by a thin, light line with
      a small arrowhead at its top, pointing up: 1 px in the mockup's `--tie` grey, as version 6
      draws it (docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html:87-88).
    - **Softer text** (note 11): a milestone whose mark comes from rule 1 or rule 9 above (skipped,
      not needed, dropped or not recorded) shows its name, code, state and why line in the softer
      text colour and a lighter weight. Its words still say which.
    - **Its why line** (note 6), for every milestone in every run, taken on or not: "why: <text>"
      from AC61's record, quoted as written, or "why: not recorded". It sits under the milestone's
      name in the quoted-record role (AC62), smaller in an earlier run.
  - **Kept from AC50:** no bar or track sized by a fraction of phases (AC41); "Phases not tracked"
    for a project with no phase records (a v2.5 project); "Done", never "Graduated"; the now
    run's rule; the info screen's numbered list of the six phases.
  - **Still and plain** (note 9's reading: the owner's snippet's "invented copy, its looping

      bullet. Then a why bullet. I do still like the checkmarks and such on the right side of the
      screen.";
    - note 14: "color coding of the sub bullets for the “why, what…” on the timeline. Just so it’s
      easily visible";
    - note 24: "Phases have Milestones, milestones have sprints - this is what we should show as
      the " what do we have to do to devlier thee milestones" - Every sprint, should then link to
      the more technical or granular breakdown of tasks that agents or myself have. It should be
      the most granular level that we see - but we dont need to see it here, just have a way to
      get there from here";
    - note 27: "Context is more specifically about what are the things that are true or exist
      that lead to the why?", read as agreed: "Context is what was true or existed that led to the
      why ... Each fact names its source. The owner's question-and-answer is not Context".
  - **Note 9, the owner's snippet** (docs/design/references/trial-view/2026-10-16-release-changelog-snippet.html),
    read as a style reference only: "Its invented copy, its looping animation and its version
    badges are not carried over; the feed shows only the project's real records." So every sprint
    shows only what its records give, nothing on the Timeline loops, slides in or moves unless
    the owner acts, and no sprint carries a version badge (AC57's "Still and plain"; plan review
    round 1, B1).
  - **What a sprint is** (note 24): what a project milestone's `· sprints:` list in docs/PROJECT.md
    section 3 names (J11): a pull request (`PR #<n>`) or a pipeline item (a research run such as
    `Q-013`, or a `P-` or `E-` item), placed as the model's timeline places it. Also:
    - the sprints the model's timeline lists under `untagged` in a run (J11);
    - a pull request whose body's `Milestone:` line (AC61 (c)) names a milestone that the plan's
      `· sprints:` lists do not tie it to (plan review round 2, B1). It is shown under each
      milestone it names, apart from the plan's sprints, in its own folded line, "<n> named by
      its pull request, not yet in the plan" ("1 named by its pull request, not yet in the
      plan"). **It changes nothing about that milestone:** the milestone's state, words, mark or
      icon (AC57, AC59), its place in the delivery order (AC59), AC50's now run and the next
      milestone to reach all stay as the plan and the model's timeline give them, because the
      model's timeline counts only the plan's lists (S-032 leaves it unchanged). Filing it in the
      plan's list is what makes it work filed. A project milestone it names sits where the model's
      timeline places that milestone; a standard milestone it names (`Milestone: Design-4`) sits
      in the run its merge time places it in, by the rule below, and that standard milestone then
      gets a row on the Timeline (AC59), keeping its own state ("not recorded" stays "not
      recorded"). An id that section 3 and the model's standard milestones do not hold places
      nothing: the pull request is untied, and its Technical bullet says "names <id>, which the
      plan does not hold" (C). Such a pull request is not counted under "Not tied to a
      milestone";
    - from J12, each pull request merged into the default branch that no milestone's list names
      and whose body names no milestone. It sits in the latest phase run, in the timeline's order,
      whose earliest time (AC59's "a run's times") is at or before its merge time, or in the first
      run when none is (C). An open pull request is not placed: it is in the Queue (AC52).
  - **Folded under each milestone** (note 21: folded, one tap away): a line "<n> sprints feed
    this", "1 sprint feeds this", or "No sprints filed yet" ("No sprints planned yet" for a planned
    milestone). It is a real disclosure control, opened and closed by a tap, Enter or Space, with
    its open state as its accessible state. Every list starts folded, and stays as the owner left
    it through a refresh (AC56). A sprint feeding two milestones shows under each.
  - **"Not tied to a milestone"** (note 21): in each run that has such sprints, one line with the
    slate filled diamond, the words "Not tied to a milestone" and "<n> sprints · for the agents to
    file" (slate, not coral, from draft 4: note 32, AC69), the fixed
    sentence "No milestone in the plan lists these sprints, and they name none." (C; plan review
    round 1, N1: it says only what the desk checks), and its sprints folded under it as above. A
    pull request whose body says `Milestone: none (<reason>)` (AC61 (c)) is among them, with its
    reason after its title, "no milestone: <reason>", and is not counted among those to file: the
    count reads "<n> sprints · <m> for the agents to file" when the two differ (C). A run with none has no
    such line. The desk never guesses which milestone a sprint feeds.
  - **Each sprint, top to bottom** (as drawn):
    - **its first line:** its date; its code ("PR #77", "Q-013") in the code role; and its marks
      on the right (note 8). The date, in `ownerTimeZone`: a pull request's merge date (J12); for
      a pipeline item, the date of its latest step in the model's timeline; otherwise "date not
      recorded";
    - **its marks,** each a mark and a word, never colour alone:
      - for a pull request: **merged** ("✓ merged" when J12 gives a merge time; "open"; "closed,
        not merged"); **review** ("✓ review" when the default branch's tree holds verdict files
        under `reviews/<n>/` and each decides PASS by reviews/README.md's rule, the first `Verdict:`
        at column 0, files whose names start `_` and README.md not counted; "✕ review" when one
        decides FAIL; "– review" with "review not recorded" when the tree holds none); **checks**
        ("✓ checks", "✕ checks" or "… checks" from J12's check state for the pull request's last
        commit: success; failure or error; pending; "– checks" with "checks not recorded" when J12
        gives none, and the reason one tap away: "the desk's token can't read checks" when J12's
        answer holds an error for that field (J12; plan review round 1, N7), otherwise "no checks
        recorded for this pull request");
      - for a pipeline item: **merged** ("✓ merged" when `status/merges.jsonl` holds the item, "–
        merged" with "not merged" otherwise); and, for a research run, **source-checked** ("✓
        source-checked" or "✕ source-checked" by its latest `source-checker` outcome's verdict,
        J9; "– source check not recorded" when none);
    - **its title** (note 8), in the item-name role:
      - a pull request: **its own title, shown as written**, character for character, never
        reworded, shortened or given words of its own (the owner's answer of 2026-10-16, "The PR's
        own title (Recommended)": "Says what was done, like the mockup did."). For pull request
        #77: "Trial view: roomier answer choices, Home's bar removed, long rulings clipped to three
        lines". A title that starts with a code, such as #40's "M1: the desk's read-only status
        page (S-001 AC1–AC15)", is shown as written too (AC64 allows it; plan review round 1, B2);
      - a research run: "Research: " and the first sentence of its request's `question`
        (`intake/requests/<id>.toml`, J11): its text up to and including the first `.`, `?` or `!`
        followed by a space or the text's end, as written (C);
      - another pipeline item: J9's headline words for it, or "title not recorded";

      The title wraps whole: it is never cut;
    - **three bullets, in this order** (notes 8 and 14), each with its label, "Technical",
      "Context" or "Why", in its own colour (AC48's bullet colours: slate, dusty rose, muted violet) on the
      label and down the bullet's left edge, so the label says what colour alone does not:
      - **Technical** (note 17, "The phase and milestone move into Technical"; AC64's "one step
        down"): the phase run and the milestone it feeds ("Design i2 · MD1", or "Design i2 · not
        tied to a milestone"); for a pull request, its branch (J12's head branch) and each other
        milestone that also lists it ("also feeds M2"); for a pipeline item, each of
        `research/<id>-memo.md`, `research/<id>-source-check.md` and its spec's path that the tree
        holds, linked by AC51's link rule. In the code role;
      - **Context** (note 27): AC61's `## Context` facts, as written, one under another, each with
        its source on its own line after it, in the meta role; "not recorded" when the record has
        none. A pipeline item has no form for it yet, so it reads "not recorded" (C);
      - **Why:** AC61's `## Why` section, as written, in the quoted-record role; "not recorded"
        when the record has none, and for a pipeline item;
    - **"Tasks in this sprint →"** (note 24), its last line, a link:
      - a pull request: its commits on GitHub, `https://github.com/<repo>/pull/<n>/commits`, with
        the fixed line "Opens the pull request's commits on GitHub; each one says who made it." (C);
      - a pipeline item: `https://github.com/<repo>/commits/<item_tip>` from its
        `status/merges.jsonl` line, with "Opens the history on GitHub up to this run's last
        commit, newest first; the run's own commits are at the top." (C; plan review round 1, N8:
        the page lists everything before the tip as well); with no such line, "Tasks: not
        recorded" and no link.

      The tasks themselves are not shown on the Timeline (note 24). It opens as the desk's other
      GitHub links do.
  - Every count ("4 sprints") is a count of the records above.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC60` uses J11's, J12's and AC61's fixtures. It checks:
    - which sprints sit under each milestone, against the fixture's `NOTE:` lines (M2B: PR #44
      and PR #52; MD1: Q-013), PR #44 under both M2 and M2B, and each `untagged` sprint under its
      run's "Not tied to a milestone" line;
    - the placing of untied merged pull requests by merge time, with one merged before every run,
      and that an open pull request is not placed;
    - that each list starts folded and opens and folds by a tap, Enter and Space;
    - each sprint's date, code, marks and words, a review that passed, one that failed and one not
      recorded, and each checks state with "checks not recorded";
    - each pull request's title against J12's fixture, character for character (#77's, and #40's,
      which starts with a code), and a research run's title;
    - a pull request placed by its `Milestone:` line alone (a project milestone, MD3, a standard
      one, an id the plan does not hold): shown in "1 named by its pull request, not yet in the
      plan" under MD3, with MD3's state, words, gold ring or other mark, delivery order, the now
      run and the next milestone to reach all as they are without it (B1), and the standard
      milestone's row keeping "not recorded"; one with `Milestone: none (<reason>)` under "Not
      tied to a milestone" with its reason and outside the "for the agents to file" count; and the
      fixed
      sentence;
    - the checks mark's reason when J12's fixture holds the made-up field error;
    - that nothing on the Timeline loops, makes an entrance or moves without the owner acting
      (AC57's "Still and plain", the same CSS and fake-clock checks), and that it shows no version
      badge;
    - each bullet's label, colour token and order, the Technical words for a pull request and a
      research run, and "not recorded" for Context and Why with no section;
    - the tasks link for a pull request, a pipeline item with a merge line, and one without;
    - that no sprint shows a task or step of its own on the Timeline.

- AC61 [trace: V4 → AC61] — When a project's records carry why lines and a sprint's Context and
  Why, then the desk shows them on AC57, AC59 and AC60 exactly as written; and until a record
  carries them, the desk shows "not recorded" and never a why or a fact of its own.
  - The owner's answer on note 6: "A line in the project's plan (Recommended)" (option text: "Each
    milestone in the project's plan (docs/PROJECT.md) gets a short "why" line that the agents write
    when they plan it, and you can edit it. The standard milestones a project skips get one too.
    Until a line exists, the desk shows "why: not recorded". This is also a small change to the
    operating model, so every project does it."). Note 27's reading: "The agents write the
    Context facts into each sprint's record when they file it, and the desk only shows them".
  - **The forms: those of the operating-model change being drafted in parallel as model S-032**
    ("What this spec does not cover"), as its draft stands on 2026-10-16
    (Personal-Org-Operating-Model specs/S-032-why-and-context.md, status draft: AC4's why, AC5's
    standard lines, AC6's `Milestone:` line, Context and Why, and its join J2 to the desk). The
    desk reads exactly these (plan review round 1, N1):
    - **(a) a project milestone's why:** a milestone line in docs/PROJECT.md section 3 may end
      with ` · why: <reason>`, after any ` · sprints:` and ` · dropped`, the reason at least one
      character, starting and ending with a non-space character and holding no ` · ` (S-032 AC4).
      The desk takes it off before it reads the rest of the line in J11's form, so a why never
      changes the milestone's name, "done when", phase, sprints or `· dropped` mark. A line whose
      why is empty, holds ` · `, or has another part after it (such as ` · dropped`) is out of
      form, as S-032's reader has it: skipped with J11's note, the rest still shown. Today the
      desk's reader fails every line with a why (src/lib/j11.js:161-163); from this change it
      reads them;
    - **(b) a standard milestone's line:** section 3 may hold one line per standard milestone,
      after an optional `- `: `<Phase>-<n> (standard) · taken on · why: <reason>` or
      `<Phase>-<n> (standard) · skipped · why: <reason>`, where `<Phase>-<n>` is one of the
      model's standard milestones (AC50; for example `Build-4`), and the reason is as in (a)
      (S-032 AC5). It holds for every run of that phase. The model's timeline is unchanged by it
      (S-032 AC5: a standard milestone's state still comes from its records alone), so the desk
      shows the line as follows: "taken on" leaves the shown state as it is; "skipped" shows
      "Skipped" where the model's state is not recorded or not needed, and otherwise the model's
      word with "planned as skipped" after it (C). A line that starts `<Phase>-<n> (standard)` but
      is not in this form, or names no standard milestone, is skipped with a note (J11's awkward
      case), and its milestone shows "not recorded" for its why. When two lines name the same
      milestone, the first counts and the second is noted;
    - **(c) a sprint's `Milestone:` line, Context and Why** (S-032 AC6), in a pull request's body
      after its `## What this means for you` paragraph:
      - **`Milestone:`** a line `Milestone: <ids>`, the ids one or more project milestone ids
        (`M<id>`) or standard ids (`Design-4`), separated by `, `; or `Milestone: none
        (<reason>)`. The first such line after the paragraph counts. AC60 places the sprint by it;
      - **the headings:** a `Context` heading and a `Why` heading, each at any level (`#` to
        `######`), with or without a colon after the word, case ignored. Each section runs from its
        heading to the next heading of any level, or the body's end; the first of each counts;
      - **Context** is a list of bullets. A bullet starts `- `; any indented line under it continues
        it, joined with one space. Each bullet, so joined, ends `(source: <record>)`: the source is
        the text inside the bullet's last ` (source: ` and its closing `)` at the end; the fact is
        the rest. The one exception bullet, `- none beyond the ruling quoted in Why` (case ignored,
        a final full stop allowed), is shown as written, with no source line. Any other bullet not
        in that form is shown as written with "source not recorded" (C, where S-032's check would
        fail it); a line in the section that is no bullet is not shown;
      - **Why** is the section's text, shown as written, its paragraphs kept, Markdown shown as
        plain text as AC54(a) reads a README.
    - **Where the desk differs from S-032:** it shows a Context bullet without a source, which
      S-032's check fails, with "source not recorded"; it reads research runs' Context and Why as
      "not recorded", since S-032 leaves them for later (its "out of scope"); and S-032's J2 reads
      a merged pull request with no `Milestone:` line as "Not tied to a milestone" even when the
      plan's `· sprints:` list names it, while the desk ties it through the plan, as the model's
      timeline does (AC60; plan review round 2, N6). The other way round, a `Milestone:` line the
      plan does not back is shown but changes no state (AC60). It checks no form: S-032's
      `pr-body` check does that in the project's own CI.
  - **Where they are read:** (a) and (b) from docs/PROJECT.md on the default branch (J11);
    (c) from the pull request's body (kept on the device only as the parts the desk shows, J12): an open one's from J1's open list, which already holds it,
    and every other's from J12.
  - **Shown as written:** nothing is reworded, shortened or added. A why, a fact or a source that
    is empty shows "not recorded". The desk writes nothing to any record.
  - **Until the records carry them:** no connected project's records hold these forms yet (2026-10-16).
    So on the preview the sandbox shows "why: not recorded" on Status, a flagged "Why not recorded
    yet" on the Timeline, and "not recorded" for each sprint's Context and Why, until Service-Desk
    adopts the model version that carries S-032 and its agents write them. A v2.5 project shows
    none of this (AC57, AC59).
  - **If S-032 is frozen with other forms,** the Definer revises this criterion to match before the
    Builder builds against it (C). S-032 is not yet a record this repository holds: the forms
    above are its draft's of 2026-10-16, in Personal-Org-Operating-Model.
  - **The fixture** (NOT real records, written in the forms above, and listed so in
    `tests/desk/fixtures/README.md`): a copy of J11's docs/PROJECT.md with why lines on M2B and MD1,
    a standard line `- Build-4 (standard) · skipped · why: the desk runs no experiments; each
    change is checked by its own tests` (the mockup's made-up example), one for `Discovery-1`
    taken on, one out of form, one repeated, a why holding ` · ` and a why with ` · dropped`
    after it; and pull request bodies with a `Milestone:` line and both sections as `## Context`
    and `## Why`, one with `### Context:` and `why` as headings, one with `Milestone: Design-4`,
    one with `Milestone: none (<reason>)`, one with the exception bullet and a quoted Why, one
    with a bullet missing its source, one with an indented continuation line, and one with none
    of these.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC61` checks each case above: the why text word for word on
    Status and Timeline; that a line with a why keeps its name, "done when", phase, sprints and
    dropped mark; "Skipped" and "planned as skipped"; the notes for the out-of-form and repeated
    lines and the two out-of-form whys; the `Milestone:` line read for each id kind; both heading
    forms; each Context fact and source, the exception bullet, the "source not recorded" case, the
    joined continuation line, and the Why text; and "not recorded" for each with J11's fixture as
    it is.

- AC62 [trace: V1 → AC62] — When the owner reads any sandbox screen, then its spacing comes from
  one scale and its text from one type scale whose roles each look different, and every word the
  screen showed before is still there.
  - The owner's words: note 3, "the general spacing of things. I dont hate it, but there are spots
    that seem too spaced, and then some spots that seem too tight."; note 5, "There is a lot of
    text throughout the service desk - which i want specifically - however if all of the text is
    formatted almost identically it all kind of blurs together."
  - **One spacing scale** (note 3): 4, 8, 12, 20 and 32 px, as five tokens in `sandbox.css`, the
    mockup's `--s1` to `--s5` (docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html:17). Every margin,
    padding and gap that sets the space between or around elements on every sandbox screen is 0 or
    one of the five, used so that related things sit close and separate groups sit further apart:
    4 and 8 inside an item, 12 between items of a group, 20 between groups, 32 between a screen's
    sections (C, as the mockup uses them). The only other values (C), each with a comment beside it
    in `sandbox.css` naming why, are: the page's 16 px side gutter with the safe areas (as the
    mockup draws it); the room kept for the bottom bar (`--sb-dock-h`) and the safe areas; the
    offsets that put a dot, icon or circle on its line; the 44 px touch targets (AC56); borders;
    a label chip's or tag's inner padding (as drawn, 1 px by 6 px: the mockup's `.bul .k` and
    `.bkey span`, :201 and :229); and the gap of 2 to 6 px between a mark or icon and its word
    inside one label, key entry or bullet (the mockup's `.key span` and `.bul li`, :56 and :200)
    (plan review round 1, N3). Every other spacing value in the mockups gives way to the scale.
  - **One type scale with distinct roles** (note 5): seven roles, each with its own look, their
    values the mockup's `--t-*` tokens (docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html:19-25):
    - **screen title:** each screen's heading;
    - **section label:** small, upper case and spaced: group and fold headers, band names, run
      tags, the labels "Done when", "Why", "Technical" and "Context";
    - **item name:** the names of projects, phases, milestones and members, and sprint titles;
    - **body:** sentences;
    - **meta:** state words, times, dates, counts, sources and keys;
    - **quoted record text:** words quoted from a record: rulings, summaries, why lines, the
      Purpose quotes, a sprint's Why;
    - **codes:** ids such as `MD2` and `PR #61`, in monospace (AC48).

    Every font set in `sandbox.css` uses one of the seven. A role may change its colour, and an
    earlier run or a softened milestone may take the next role down (AC57), but nothing takes a
    size of its own: the mockup's own sizes (such as the sprint title's 1.04rem, :198, or the phase
    name's 1.12rem, :72) give way to the roles. The exceptions: AC63's text boxes, at least 16 px;
    and marks drawn as text, which are icons, not text roles (the ◆, ✓, ✕ and – glyphs, and
    `.sb-badge`'s count, sized to their icon, N3).
  - **Every word kept** (note 5: "which i want specifically"): on every sandbox screen other than
    the parts AC57 to AC60 and AC65 to AC68 rebuild or add to (the Status screen's phase list,
    Home's phase checklist, the Timeline, the Queue's rows and group icons, the Team screen's
    cards, the phase marker, "More detail"'s Pipeline link, and the lines AC69 to AC72 add or
    reword), the text is the same, word for
    word, before and after. Only the look changes.
  - Judged on the phone by the owner too: the sandbox pull request shows each screen at 360 px in
    the light and dark themes.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC62` reads `sandbox.css`, as AC51's and AC54's tests do, and
    checks that every margin, padding, gap, row-gap and column-gap is 0, one of the five tokens,
    or a value with its comment; that every `font` and `font-size` uses a role's token, the text
    box rule excepted; and that the seven tokens differ from each other. It renders every sandbox
    screen from the fixtures, and checks each screen's text, outside the rebuilt parts, against a
    snapshot taken from the same fixtures at commit 2c1acb6 (committed under
    `tests/desk/fixtures/`).

- AC64 [trace: V4 → AC64] — When the desk presents a project on the screens AC57 to AC61 build,
  then it does so in plain terms, structured from the goal to phases, milestones and sprints, with
  technical detail one step down.
  - The owner's words (note 21): "I am not a coding expert, i might play around on project ideas
    that i have no prior experience in, Im a generalist with wide skill set and an ability to
    learn very quickly - but i undertand process flow & business impact, and i have a very
    structural system oriented mindset." Its reading: "a standing guide for how the desk presents
    every project: in plain terms, structured from goal to phases to milestones to tasks, with
    technical detail one step down" (tasks renamed sprints by note 24).
  - **Plain terms first:** every phase, milestone and sprint is shown by its plain-English name or
    title first, with its code after, in the code role (AC48). The desk's own fixed words are plain
    ("Done when", "Why", "Not tied to a milestone", "gone back over"). They never use the model's
    working terms: no "untagged", no stage numbers, no "dispatch", and "i<n>" only after its
    phase's name.
  - **Structured:** the Timeline has exactly these levels: what we're delivering, phases (as their
    runs), milestones, sprints, and the tasks one link away (AC59, AC60). The Status screen has
    phases, their runs, and milestones (AC57).
  - **Technical detail one step down:** in the desk's own words, file paths, branch names,
    specification and criterion ids, and test counts appear only in a sprint's Technical bullet,
    as a code after plain words, or one tap down; never as a title or as an item's first line.
  - **A record's own words are shown as written** (plan review round 1, B2; the owner's answer of
    2026-10-16 that a sprint's title is its pull request's own title): a recorded title, why line
    or Context fact is never reworded to meet this guide, even when it starts with a code (pull
    request #40's "M1: the desk's read-only status page (S-001 AC1–AC15)"). The sandbox pull
    request lists each such title on Service-Desk's Timeline, so the owner can see where the
    records fall short of the guide; better titles are the agents' to write, not the desk's.
  - **Other screens:** their words stay as AC62 keeps them. The sandbox pull request lists any
    place on them that falls short of this guide, for the owner to rule on; nothing there is

**Four additions from the audit: notes 32 to 35** (added 2026-10-16, draft 4)
*From the owner's picks on the audit for missing elements (decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-2.md,

(Recommended)" for the stalled signal; same record, last section). Everything AC57 to AC68 set
holds here too.*

- AC69 [trace: V2 → AC69] — When the desk shows a gap that the agents fill, such as a missing why
  line or sprints not filed against a milestone, then it shows it in slate grey, saying who fills
  it, with a slate filled diamond where a diamond is used; and coral appears only on what waits
  on the owner.
  - The owner's pick: "Coral only for you (Recommended)", whose option text says: "Today every
    missing why line or "needs filing" flag shows in coral, your "needs you" colour, though agents
    fix them, not you. Dozens per project would read as false alarms. Make those neutral; coral
    stays only for things that need you." (V2 is the false-alarm target.)
  - **Coral (`--you`) is used only for** the owner's own things: an item in the Queue that waits on
    the owner, the "needs you" diamond on a group or a tile, "Waiting on you" in who has the ball
    and on the owner's Team tile, the queue's pill when something waits, and what AC48 already
    gives the owner (AC49's owner tile, AC52's rows that need the owner). Nothing an agent fills is
    coral.
  - **The agents' gaps, in slate (`--muted`), each saying who fills it** (as drawn):
    - a milestone with no why line: on the Timeline, "Why not recorded yet · for the agents to
      add" (AC59), and the same words in the Queue's "Unblocks" (AC66); on Status the quieter
      "why: not recorded" stays as AC57 has it;
    - sprints not filed against a milestone: "Not tied to a milestone · <n> sprints · for the agents
      to file", with a slate filled diamond (AC59, AC60);
    - the other agent-owned gaps on these screens, which carry no colour of their own and now say
      so in slate: the Queue's "Unblocks: milestone not recorded" and "Context: not recorded"
      (AC66), a sprint's Context and Why "not recorded" (AC60), the Team card's "feeds: not tied to
      a milestone" (AC67), and "Done today"'s "sprint: not recorded" (AC66).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC69` renders Home, Status, Timeline, Team and Queue from the
    fixtures, and checks: that every element whose colour comes from `--you` is one of the owner's
    things listed above (none on a why line, a "Not tied to a milestone" line or any gap above);
    that each gap above shows its words and uses `--muted`; that the "Not tied to a milestone"
    diamond is filled with `--muted`; and the words "for the agents to add" and "for the agents to
    file".

- AC70 [trace: V4 → AC70] — When the owner looks at a place where a project went back and a new
  run began, on the Status screen's branch drawing or at the Timeline's band where that run starts,
  then the reason the run began is shown there, quoted from its record, with its source and one tap
  to the whole record; or "why it went back: not recorded" where no record gives it.
  - The owner's pick: "Why it went back (Recommended)", whose option text says: "At each branch
    point on Status and the Timeline, show the ruling that sent the project back (e.g. why Design
    ran a second time). Today the drawing shows that it branched, not why."
  - **Where the model records why a run began** (model S-023 AC6; found in
    governance/checks/orchestrator.py and v3_checks.py): an item's phase run starts again only
    after a *boundary* line in the dispatch log, an applied decision whose gate word sends the work
    back through a gate it did not pass (`BOUNDARY_WORDS`: `recycle` or `re-aim` at the `dor` gate,
    `recycle` or `revise` at `criteria` and `launch`; governance/checks/orchestrator.py:130 and :284-286). That line
    names its answer record (`record`, for example `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<n>.md`), whose
    `Reason:` line is the owner's reason (decisions/README.md: hold and recycle need one). A run
    the model's timeline builds from the plan's milestone order alone, with no line of its own
    (v3_checks.py, `place_milestones`), has no such record.
  - **The rule:** for each run that starts a new column (AC57's Terms, runs 2 and later), the
    reason is the latest boundary line of the run's item before the run's first routed line. The
    desk shows:
    - "Run <k> began · <date>" (the boundary line's `time`, in `ownerTimeZone`);
    - the text of its record's first `Reason:` line, quoted as written; and when the record has no
      `Reason:` line, plain words instead, with the gate's code and word after in the code role
      (AC64; plan review round 3, N4): "sent back at a gate, with no reason written" then
      `<gate>: <word>` (for example `dor: recycle`) (C);
    - its source, "your ruling · <record path>" when the line is not marked `proxy`, otherwise
      "ruling by proxy · <record path>";
    - **one tap to the whole record:** the source is a link to the record on GitHub, by AC51's
      link rule.

    With no boundary line, or a record the tree does not hold: "Run <k> began · why it went back:
    not recorded" (slate, AC69).
  - **For Service-Desk, plainly:** at 3117150 and on today's default branch, run 2 (Design i2) is
    one the model's timeline builds from the plan's order (MD1 to MD3 follow M1 to M2B in
    section 3); no boundary line starts it (tests/desk/fixtures/j11-3117150/trace-notes.txt: Build
    i1 and Design i2 hold no decision line). So the desk shows "why it went back: not recorded"
    there. The mockup's quote of decisions/questions/P-001-design-step.md is an illustration: no
    record the desk reads ties that ruling to the run. A record that did (a model change, for
    example a run's start line naming its ruling) is listed in "What this spec does not cover".
  - **Where it shows:** on Status, in the box of the phase where the new run starts, at the top of
    that run, beside the curve; on the Timeline, at the top of that run's band, under its name. The
    words are the same on both.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC70` checks "why it went back: not recorded" for Design i2 with
    J11's fixture, on both screens, and, with a made-up log (NOT a real record) holding a `dor`
    `recycle` line and its answer file with a `Reason:` line, a run 2 showing its date, that
    reason word for word, "your ruling · <path>" with its link, a proxy line's source, a record
    with no `Reason:` line giving "sent back at a gate, with no reason written" with `dor:
    recycle` after it in the code role, and a record the tree does not hold giving
    "not recorded".

- AC71 [trace: V1 → AC71] — When nothing has moved on an active project for 3 days or more and
  nothing in it waits on the owner, then its Home card says so in a slate line, "No movement for
  <n> days · last: <what>, <date>".
  - The owner's pick: "Stalled-project signal", whose option text says: "If nothing has moved on a
    project for a set number of days and nothing waits on you, its tile says so ("No movement for
    5 days"), so a quiet desk can't hide a stopped project." The number: "3 days (Recommended)"
    ("Long enough to skip a quiet weekend, short enough to catch a stop within the week.").
  - **What counts as moving**, each from a record the desk reads, the latest of these being the
    project's last movement:
    - the default branch's head commit, at its `committedAt` (J6's poll answer; every merge,
      filed question, filed card and committed agent record lands as a commit there);
    - a pull request merged into the default branch, at its `mergedAt` (J12);
    - an open pull request's `updated_at` (J1's open list), for work on a branch not yet merged
      (C);
    - a dispatch-log entry, at its `time` (J9): an agent's dispatch or outcome, a card raised, a
      decision.
  - **When it shows:** the project is in the Active group (AC54), was read successfully within the
    last 3 minutes (Quiet's rule), nothing in it waits on the owner (its pill's count is 0), and
    its last movement is at least 3 days (72 hours) before now. <n> is the whole number of days
    since the last movement.
  - **"last: <what>, <date>"**, the date in `ownerTimeZone`, the words for the latest movement:
    "PR #<n> merged" (J12), "PR #<n> updated" (J1), J9's headline words for a dispatch-log entry,
    or "a commit on <default branch>" for a head commit none of the others accounts for. When two
    have the same time, the first in this list is named (C).
  - **In slate, not coral** (AC69: it is a heads-up, not a decision for the owner). It sits on the
    card under who has the ball. It changes nothing else: the card's order, its group and "Quiet"
    stay as AC8, AC54 and AC1 to AC7 have them.
  - **Not shown** for a project in Done or Closed (C), for one that can't be read, while its reads
    are not all in ("not available yet" shows nowhere for it: the line simply waits), or when
    something waits on the owner. The mockup's "Moving: last change today." line for a moving
    project is not built: the owner picked only the stalled signal (C, a known difference).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC71` drives a fake clock over fixtures: no line at 71 hours; the
    line at 72 hours with "3 days"; each kind of movement as the latest, with its words and date;
    the tie order; no line when something waits, for a Done project, or for one that can't be
    read; the line in `--muted`; and the card's order unchanged.

- AC72 [trace: V4 → AC72] — *Traced to V4 because the desk takes over the live address only through
  deploys the owner approves; none of V1 to V5 measures a deploy's safety itself (plan review round
  3, N4).* When a deploy waits for the owner's approval in a project's Queue, then
  its row says whether the commit it would deploy is the merge commit of a pull request the
  Reviewer passed, with the source, or that it is not and should not be approved, or that the desk
  can't check yet; the approval itself stays on GitHub.
  - The owner's pick: "Safe-to-approve on deploys", whose option text says: "Each deploy waiting
    for your approval shows whether it matches a pull request the Reviewer passed ("matches PR #77
    · Reviewer PASS"), so a wrong tap can't ship unreviewed code."
  - **Which commit** (plan review round 3, B1): a waiting deploy in AC52's Deploys group is a
    workflow run in J1's lists for the current head (AC52's known gap still holds: a deploy waiting
    on an earlier head drops out). The commit it would deploy is:
    - for a run started by a push (`event` `push`): its `head_sha`;
    - for a run started by hand (`event` `workflow_dispatch`, as every production deploy and every
      manual preview redeploy is): the commit the owner gave it, not `head_sha`, which is only
      main's head when it was started. J1's run list does not hold the run's inputs, but it holds
      its `display_title`, which desk-deploy sets to `deploy <sha> — <note>`
      (.github/workflows/desk-deploy.yml:15, `run-name: deploy ${{ github.event_name == 'push' &&
      github.sha || inputs.sha }} — …`; .github/scripts/desk_pick_commit.sh:18 deploys
      `INPUT_SHA`; `display_title` is in GitHub's run list, as the recorded
      tests/desk/fixtures/github/runs-e2ab9c5-dispatch.json shows). The desk takes the 40 lowercase hex characters straight after `deploy ` at the
      start of `display_title`;
    - when that cannot be read (another title, a short or missing sha, another workflow), the row
      says "Can't check this deploy yet", with the reason "the deploy's commit isn't readable from
      its title" one tap away.
  - **The check**, from records the desk reads:
    1. the pull request whose merge commit is that sha: J12's `mergeCommit` of a merged pull
       request into the default branch (J12 adds the field);
    2. that pull request's `reviews/<n>/reviewer.md` on the default branch, decided by
       reviews/README.md's rule (the first `Verdict:` at column 0).
  - **What the row says** (as drawn):
    - both hold and the verdict is PASS: "Matches PR #<n>'s merge · Reviewer PASS", with the source
      on its own line, "reviews/<n>/reviewer.md · the merge commit on <default branch>", each
      linked by AC51's link rule;
    - otherwise: "Doesn't match a reviewed pull request: don't approve", with the reason on its own
      line: "no merge of a passed pull request has this commit" when no pull request's merge commit
      is the sha, "PR #<n>'s Reviewer verdict is FAIL" or "PR #<n> has no Reviewer verdict on
      <default branch>";
    - when a read it needs has failed or not yet arrived (J12, the tree, or the verdict's blob):
      "Can't check this deploy yet", with the reason one tap away (AC41). Never a pass by default.
  - **The desk only says.** It holds no write credential (mechanism): the row's link opens the run
    on GitHub, where the owner approves, as today. The row adds no colour: a "don't approve" row
    is marked by its words and a ✕ mark (AC42), and the Deploys group's own mark follows AC66.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC72` uses J1's and J12's fixtures with a waiting deploy run
    (NOT a real record) on a head that is PR #77's merge commit with `reviews/77/reviewer.md`
    reading PASS; the same with a FAIL verdict and with no verdict; a head that is no pull
    request's merge commit; and J12's read failed, giving "Can't check this deploy yet". From
    draft 5 (B1) it adds a hand-started run (`workflow_dispatch`) whose `head_sha` is a
    records-only commit on main and whose `display_title` names PR #77's merge commit
    (`deploy <sha> — …`, its `inputs.sha` differing from `head_sha`), giving "Matches PR #77's
    merge · Reviewer PASS"; the same run with a `display_title` that names no full sha, giving
    "Can't check this deploy yet"; and a push run checked by its `head_sha`. It checks each row's
    words, source and links, and that no row offers an approval of its own.

### M3 — Planning and scheduling, measurement, then launch on the needs-you address
*Covers D3.4, D4.2, the V1, V2 and V5 measurement, and D6. Starts once MD is accepted; its views
use MD's chosen design.*

**Phone notifications, ruled and still to be specified here** (added 2026-10-16, draft 4): the
owner's ruling of 2026-10-08 (decisions/questions/P-001-notifications.md) puts Web Push to the
desk on the owner's iPhone Home Screen, sent by the Orchestrator's GitHub run when it raises a
card, in M3 (AC45). Its criteria, join sheet and experiment registration are not written yet; the
Definer adds them here in a revision before M3 starts, and the Builder builds nothing for them
until then.

- AC24 [trace: V4 → AC24] — When the owner opens "Work ahead", then it lists, in one list, the open
  items of every connected v3 project (state `ready`, `returned`, `dispatched` or `waiting-owner`)
  and each v2.5 project's open sprint.
  - The owner can reorder the list and set a "not before" date and time on items, then tap "Record
    plan". The desk opens a prefilled new-file link in Service-Desk for
    `docs/planning/<YYYY-MM-DD>T<HHMM>Z-<nn>.md`, in J4's form.
  - Until that file is on Service-Desk's default branch, the plan is labelled "Draft, not recorded".
  - Once it is there, the desk shows it as the current plan (J4's ordering rule).
  - No agent acts on it automatically; the Chief of Staff carries it out
    (decisions/questions/P-001-spend-and-planning.md, question 2; P-001-build-path.md, question 2).
- AC25 [trace: V1 → AC25] [trace: V2 → AC25] [trace: V5 → AC25] — When the desk is used during a week, then on the
  device in use it records each open window's start and end, and for each flagged item the time it
  was first shown.
  - The owner can mark any flagged item "Didn't need me".
  - From Monday each week the desk offers a prefilled link that commits the previous week's figures,
    including the week's V5 median, to `docs/metrics/<YYYY>-W<ww>.md` in Service-Desk, in J5's form.
  - Nothing of this is stored on Cloudflare.
- AC26 [trace: V3 → AC26] — When the owner wants to add a connected project, then the operating
  notes give the steps: one entry in `src/config/projects.json` and one repository added to the read
  token, and nothing else changes. A 6th entry fails the build with a message saying 5 is the limit.
- AC27 [trace: V3 → AC27] — When the owner or Chief of Staff needs to run the desk, then the
  operating notes (D6.3, `docs/handover/desk-operations.md`) cover:
  - renewing the read token, with its permissions as Setup G1 lists them (Actions: read included);
  - checking the Access applications;
  - adding a project (AC26);
  - switching a project from `v2.5` to `v3` in J7;
  - rolling back;
  - questions answered from the desk: `review_check.py questions` counts a question as open until
    its `questions/<name>.md` is deleted (Setup CS7). So the owner signal may list a question the
    desk already shows as answered, until the Chief of Staff deletes the file;
  - checking the deploy gate (Setup CS9): at each milestone acceptance, both environments'
    protection settings and the audit-log entries for environment changes since the last milestone
    are shown to the owner, because agents act under the owner's admin account;
  - approving a deploy:
    - Approve only when the run's SHA equals the merge-commit SHA shown on the page of a pull
      request the Reviewer passed. Match the SHA, never the commit message or the dispatch note,
      which anyone can write.
    - Production only for a SHA already accepted on the preview.

  Each step is written so that it can be done in under 10 minutes (V3).
- AC28 [trace: V4 → AC28] — When M3 is accepted and the owner approves launch, then:
  - **the old page's deploys are stopped first.** Operations-Hub's `needs-you-page` workflow is
    still active: it runs on cron `5 0-5,12-23 * * *` with `wrangler pages deploy site
    --project-name needs-you --branch main` (bd84592, line 110), and a scheduled run succeeded at
    2026-10-07T23:32Z (seen by the Reviewer on PR #43, C). Left on, it would overwrite the desk
    within an hour. So the launch order is (Setup C8):
    1. the owner disables that workflow in Operations-Hub (Actions → `needs-you-page` → Disable
       workflow), and checks that no run of it is in progress;
    2. the owner approves the `production` job, which deploys the desk;
    3. the owner checks `needs-you` serves the desk, then archives Operations-Hub's repository.
  - **production:** the `needs-you` production deployment serves the desk at its `pages.dev`
    address (and any custom domain the project already has, each behind Access, Setup C6). It is
    deployed only by the Chief of Staff's workflow job in the GitHub environment `production`, which
    needs the owner's approval, with `--branch` set to the production branch EXP-002 recorded.
    - EXP-002 found `needs-you` is a direct-upload project, not Git-integrated
      (P-001-m1-accept.md). So the desk deploys straight into it: no new project, no redirect, and
      no custom domain is needed. The O5 fallback is dropped;
  - **rollback:** the previous Operations-Hub deployment can still be restored, and the
    `needs-you-page` workflow re-enabled (after un-archiving) so the old page keeps updating;
  - **archiving:** the owner archives Operations-Hub's repository, last (step 3 above);
  - **usage figures:** D7.2 is adopted (model S-020, L-0005), so AC23 shows at least one real session's figures (the
    owner's ruling that launch waits for usage and context, P-001-build-path.md, question 5).

  There is no side-by-side period (P-001-build-path.md, answer 4).

## Join sheets

- **J1 — GitHub REST → desk function (reader).**
  - **Every request:** each `GET` goes to `https://api.github.com` with
    `Authorization: Bearer <read token>`, `Accept: application/vnd.github+json` and
    `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28`. When the page holds an ETag for the URL, it also carries
    `If-None-Match: <etag>`.
    - `{o}/{r}` and `{default}` come from J7 (`repo`, `defaultBranch`). Nothing is looked up to
      learn the default branch, so a steady poll needs no extra request for it.
  - **Poll call** (one per project per 60 s, J6). It makes these requests in this order:
    1. `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/branches/{default}`. From the body the function takes the head commit
       `commit.sha`, `commit.commit.tree.sha` and `commit.commit.committer.date`. This is the only
       response body the function parses whole; a compare answer is cut down to its status and
       counts (J6), and every other body it passes on as text (below).
    2. `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/pulls?state=open&per_page=100`.
    3. From M2 (AC29), for every project, two workflow-run lists for the head (M1 read
       `commits/{head}/check-runs` instead):
       - 3a. `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/actions/runs?head_sha={head}&event=push&exclude_pull_requests=true&per_page=100`;
       - 3b. `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/actions/runs?head_sha={head}&event=workflow_dispatch&exclude_pull_requests=true&per_page=100`.
    4. Only when the head differs from the head the page sent (or the page sent none):
       `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/git/trees/{treeSha}?recursive=1`.

    With one page each, a steady poll is exactly four requests (three in M1).
    - **Why workflow runs:** a fine-grained token has no Checks permission, so check runs can't be
      read on a private repository (Personal-Org-Operating-Model, P-001-m1-accept.md). Workflow
      runs need Actions: read, which a fine-grained token offers (Setup G1, G3; C).
