    - **Why `workflow_dispatch` as well as `push`:** the Orchestrator pushes with the workflow's
      own token, which starts no push run, and its commit job then starts governance by
      `workflow_dispatch` (governance.yml:1–7; orchestrator.yml:459–465). So on a head the
      Orchestrator made, governance's run is a dispatch run. A push filter alone would hide it, and
      a failing governance run halts the Orchestrator's loop.
    - **Why not every event:** the API takes one `event` value per request and no exclusion.
      - Unfiltered, the list also holds the Orchestrator's scheduled runs: every 30 minutes
        (`.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml:16`), so an idle head gathers 48 a day and would pass
        3 pages in about six days.
      - It would also hold `pull_request` runs that share the head's sha when a pull request is
        merged without a merge commit. Those are review-time runs; for example governance's
        `pull_request` run at c782653 failed while its `push` run passed.
      - So scheduled and pull-request runs are not read. The latest Orchestrator run is still seen
        whenever it was a push or dispatch run (below, "activity only").
  - **Paging.** When a list response carries `Link: <…>; rel="next"`, the function follows it, up
    to 3 pages per list (300 PRs, or 300 workflow runs in each of 3a and 3b). Push runs are at most
    one per workflow per push. Dispatch runs on one head are one governance run per Orchestrator
    push plus the Orchestrator's chained runs; that these stay well under 300 is my estimate (E).
    - Each page is one request with its own ETag.
    - If a 4th page would be needed, the function returns `state: partial`, reason `too-many`. It
      can tell from the `Link` header alone, without parsing a body.
    - A tree whose JSON has `truncated: true` is detected by **the page**, which parses the tree. The
      page sets the same `partial` / `too-many` on its own record of the project. The function
      doesn't parse the tree.
    - Either way, the project is not read successfully (AC5), so a missed PR or CI run can never
      show as "Quiet".
    - Every page is requested on every poll, because a change on page 2 doesn't change page 1.
  - **Blob call** (J6): up to 25 requests, of two kinds (three from M2: blobs, history and
    compare).
    - `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/git/blobs/{sha}` with `Accept: application/vnd.github.raw+json`. If that
      returns JSON instead, the function decodes `content` from base64.
    - For owner questions and their rulings only (J2), a history request:
      `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/commits?path=<path>&sha={default}&per_page=100`.
      - **Oldest commit** (the path's creation): if the response's `Link` header has `rel="last"`,
        the function also fetches that page, and the page takes its last entry's
        `commit.committer.date`. That is at most 2 requests per path, with no limit on history
        length.
      - **Newest commit:** the first entry of page 1.
      - The function returns the raw pages, and the page reads them.
    - For open merge cards only (J2, from M2, AC47), a compare request:
      `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/compare/{tip}...{head}?per_page=1`, where `{tip}` is the card's 40-hex
      item tip and `{head}` the default-branch head sha the page last read. The function returns the
      body cut to its status and counts (J6); the page reads only its `status` (`ahead`,
      `identical`, `behind` or `diverged`; C, not in the library, confirmed by M2's join test
      against recorded answers). `per_page=1` keeps
      the commit list in the body short (C).
    - Blobs are fetched by their sha, so they never change and carry no ETag. The page keeps each
      parsed result keyed by blob sha (J6).
  - **Limits per invocation.**
    - A poll call makes at most 11 GitHub requests (from M2: the branch, 3 pages each for PRs, 3a
      and 3b, and the tree); a blob call makes at most 25. Both sit inside the 30 that J1 allows per invocation.
    - J8's key fetch adds at most 1, and redirect hops count too (F-cf-workers-02). The 50 on Free
      are never approached.
    - At most six requests wait for headers at once, and a seventh queues (F-cf-workers-03 for the
      six, A; F-cf-workers-07 for the queuing, B). So a full blob call runs in about five waves of
      six. Whether its wall time and CPU fit Free is EXP-001's measurement. If not, the blob batch
      size (one constant, `BLOB_BATCH = 25`) is lowered.
    - **EXP-001's result** (docs/handover/experiments/EXP-001-result.md): every set passed, with
      no Error 1102 or 1027, a maximum wall time of 1,938 ms and at most 25 GitHub requests per
      call. `BLOB_BATCH` stays 25, and the page's 30 s call timeout (J6) stands, about 15 times the
      longest call measured. Cloudflare showed no CPU figure for the Functions.
  - **The function parses no record content.**
    - It returns the PR pages, workflow-run pages, tree and blobs to the page as raw text (J6), and
      the page does all record parsing (J2, J9, J10).
    - The function's own work is J8's check, the branch body, the response headers (`ETag`,
      `Link`, `X-RateLimit-*`, `Retry-After`), J6's request checks, and assembling the response.
      Base64 decoding happens only if GitHub ignores the raw media type.
    - This keeps CPU per call small and independent of repository size; EXP-001 measures it anyway.
  - **CI reduction rule** (one result per head, computed on the page from all of 3a's and 3b's
    pages' `workflow_runs` arrays; the words are GitHub's `status` and `conclusion` values, which
    workflow runs share with check runs, C):
    - **Latest run per workflow:** runs are grouped by `path` (the workflow file), and only the
      run with the greatest `run_number` in each group counts. A re-run is a new attempt of the
      same run, and the list gives each run's latest attempt (C), so a run that failed and then
      passed on re-run counts as passing.
    - **Activity only, never CI:**
      - **`.github/workflows/desk-deploy.yml`** (N1): its runs wait for the owner's approval and
        may be rejected; neither says anything about the code. Its latest run is shown as activity,
        "deploy <short sha>: <what happened>":
        - `status: waiting` → "waiting for approval";
        - another status not `completed` → the status word (for example "in_progress");
        - `conclusion: success` → "deployed";
        - `conclusion: failure` → "failed or rejected": the run list gives a rejected deploy and a
          failed job the same conclusion as far as the desk can tell (C; the first rejected deploy
          confirms it), so the desk doesn't guess which;
        - any other conclusion → the conclusion word (for example "cancelled").
      - **`.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml`:** the Orchestrator's own runs, cancelled and skipped
        by design (model S-018). Its latest run is shown as activity, "Orchestrator run:
        <conclusion>", so a failed Orchestrator run is visible without colouring CI.
      - These two paths are a list in the page's code. A project whose files differ is not
        affected: paths it lacks never match.
    - Then, over the remaining runs:
    - `failing` if any run has `status: completed` and a conclusion of `failure`, `timed_out`,
      `action_required` or `startup_failure`;
    - otherwise `running` if any run's `status` is not `completed`;
    - otherwise `passing` if at least one run concluded `success`, and every other run concluded
      `success`, `neutral`, `skipped`, `cancelled` or `stale`;
    - otherwise `none`: no runs, or all of them skipped, neutral, cancelled or stale.
    - **Real samples** (`gh api` on 2026-10-08):
      - Service-Desk at e2ab9c5, a head the Orchestrator made: 3a lists nothing; 3b lists
        `governance.yml`, `workflow_dispatch`, `success` (run 292) and `orchestrator.yml`,
        `workflow_dispatch`, `success` (run 189). CI reduces to `passing` from governance; the
        Orchestrator run is activity. With a push filter alone, CI would have read `none`.
      - Service-Desk at 38722d3 (main): 3a lists `governance.yml`, `push`, `success`; 3b lists
        nothing. That reduces to `passing`. The scheduled `orchestrator.yml` run on the same head is
        not read.
      - Service-Desk at bbd7abc (PR #40's merge): 3a lists `desk-build.yml`, `desk-deploy.yml` and
        `governance.yml`, each `push`, `success`. CI is `passing` from desk-build and governance;
        the deploy is activity, "deployed".
      - Personal-Org-Operating-Model at 677fe5a: 3a lists `ci`, `push`, `success`.
    - `cancelled` counts as not failing. That is my choice (C), and it is easy to change.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **304 on every steady-poll request:** returns `unchanged` with nothing re-sent.
    - **401, or a 403 whose `X-RateLimit-Remaining` is not `0` (or absent) and which has no
      `Retry-After`:** `cant-read`, reason `token` (src/lib/github.js:54).
    - **Any 429, or a 403 with `X-RateLimit-Remaining: 0` or a `Retry-After`:** reason
      `rate-limit`, and `retryAfter` is set from `Retry-After`, else the reset header, else 60 s. The page doesn't poll that project again before
      then.
    - **404 on the branch:** reason `config`, "default branch <name> not found".
    - **403 on workflow runs only** (a token-kind 403, as above): CI shows "can't read CI (the read
      token needs Actions: read)", and the project stays readable. This is the case where the token
      lacks Actions: read (Setup G3 not yet done). A 401 there is the token failing, reason `token`,
      as on every other request (PR #40 review N8). (Under M1's check-runs read, this case was
      "can't read checks", which EXP-004 Q4 found on Personal-Org-Operating-Model.)
    - **GitHub 5xx or a network failure reaching GitHub:** reason `github`.
    - **Too many pages:** `partial`, reason `too-many`, set by the function. **A truncated tree:**
      the same, set by the page.
    - **Retries:** a failed or rate-limited read is never retried within an invocation, or sooner
      than the next scheduled poll.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J1` covers it, with recorded GitHub responses including a
    two-page PR list, the workflow-run samples above (the e2ab9c5 dispatch case among them), a
    `waiting` and a rejected deploy run, a failed Orchestrator run, and a 403 on workflow runs.

- **J2 — Cards, answers and owner questions → read model (page side).**
  - **Where they come from:** the tree (J1) lists the paths; blobs give the text.
  - **Cards (v3).** A card is a path matching `^queue/([A-Z]+-[0-9]+)-([^/]+)-([0-9]+)\.md$`. The
    groups are `<item>`, `<gate>` and `<n>`. A gate may hold hyphens, as in `dor-fail`, but never
    `/`, so nothing under `queue/merge/` can match.
    - Not cards: `queue/README.md` (ignored), anything under `queue/merge/` (merge-gate notes,
      S-013 AC5, shown as activity), hold cards (below), and any other path under `queue/` that
      doesn't match (activity, "can't parse", never flagged, never dropped silently).
  - **Hold cards (v3, from M2, AC46).** A path matching `^queue/ci-hold-([0-9a-f]{12})\.md$`. It
    doesn't match the card pattern above (no trailing `-<n>`), so it is read here.
    - **The real form** (`hold_card`, governance/checks/orchestrator_git.py:1233–1245):
      - line 1: `# Hold card — the default branch's governance run is not green`;
      - line 3: `tip: <40-hex sha>   state: <state>   held for: <minutes> minutes`, where `<state>`
        is the Orchestrator's word for the latest governance run on that tip (`failed`, `running`,
        `absent` or `unreadable`, orchestrator_git.py:1201–1230);
      - sections `## 1. The decision` and `## 2. What to do`, the second ending "The loop reads no
        answer from this card."
    - **What the desk takes:** the title (line 1 after `# `), `tip`, `state` and `held for` from
      line 3, and section 2's text. A line that doesn't match is named in `notes`; the card is
      still flagged while open.
    - **When it is open** (the desk's version of the Orchestrator's rule, orchestrator_git.py:3175–3215,
      which keeps "the newest ci-hold card until a governance run on its commit or a descendant
      passes"):
      - **Only the newest counts.** With one hold card in the tree, it is the newest. With several,
        the page reads each one's oldest commit through J1's history request (the creation time,
        fixed once known and cached by path) and takes the latest. The others are activity,
        "replaced by a newer hold card".
      - **It is open** until the latest run of `.github/workflows/governance.yml` among J1's 3a and
        3b on the default-branch head has `status: completed` and `conclusion: success`. The head is
        always the card's commit or a descendant of it.
      - **The difference from the Orchestrator, stated:** the Orchestrator also clears it when a
        governance run passed on an intermediate commit between the card and the head. The desk
        sees only the head's runs, so in that case it keeps flagging until the head's governance
        run passes. It errs towards flagging (V1 over V2). This is my choice (C).
      - A deleted hold card file is no longer flagged.
    - **No answer, no times:** it has no answer path and no dispatch-log `card` entry, so AC16 and
      AC20 don't apply to it.
  - **Merge cards (v3, from M2, AC47).** A path matching
    `^queue/([A-Z]+-[0-9]+)-merge-(conflict|by-hand)\.md$`. It doesn't match the card pattern above
    (no trailing `-<n>`), so it is read here. Merge-gate notes under `queue/merge/` stay activity.
    - **The real form** (`MergeGate.fail`, governance/checks/orchestrator_git.py:2887–2910):
      - line 1: `# Merge card — <item> (<step>)`, where `<step>` is `conflict` or `by-hand`;
      - line 3: `item: <item>   tip: <40-hex sha>   step: <step>` (the Orchestrator reads the tip
        with `^item: [PQE]-[0-9]+ +tip: ([0-9a-f]{40})\b`, orchestrator_git.py:3031);
      - sections `## The decision`, `## Why` and `## What to do`, the last ending "The loop reads no
        answer from this card."
      - `conflict` reads "the merge conflicts: resolve it on the item branch or the default branch,
        or merge it by hand"; `by-hand` reads "the gate cannot satisfy what it needs, so the owner
        merges it by hand".
    - **What the desk takes:** the item and step from the path, the tip from line 3 (by the
      Orchestrator's own pattern), and the three sections. A card whose line 3 doesn't match is
      named in `notes` and **still flagged** (its tip unknown, so only the closed and deleted rules
      below can clear it).
    - **The Orchestrator never deletes it.** It writes the file again for a newer tip, and stops
      treating it as waiting once the tip is merged or recorded, or the item is closed
      (orchestrator_git.py:3166–3173 and :3216–3225). So the file existing is not enough.
    - **When it is open** (the Orchestrator's rule, applied by the desk):
      - the file exists on the default branch; and
      - the item's status file isn't `closed` (a `done` item is exactly one waiting to merge, so
        `done` doesn't clear it); and
      - no line of `status/merges.jsonl` has this `item` and `item_tip` equal to the card's tip
        (the gate's own merges). The page reads that file as a blob, cached by sha; and
      - the tip is not merged into the default branch by hand. The desk asks GitHub once per open
        merge card and head (J1's compare request): a `status` of `ahead` or `identical` means the
        tip is an ancestor of the head, so it is merged. That answer is final for that tip and is
        cached by tip; `behind`, `diverged`, or a failed read leave the card flagged and are asked
        again on the next head change.
      - It errs towards flagging: an unreadable or missing answer never clears a card.
    - **No answer, no times:** as for hold cards.
    - The card's text is parsed from the real form. Sample: Service-Desk `queue/P-001-stop-6.md`.
      - Line 1: `# Decision card — P-001 stop-6`.
      - Line 3: `item: P-001   kind: project   gate: stop   card: 6   route: triage-return`.
      - Line 4: ``answer: write `decisions/P-001/stop-6.md`, first line `Decision: <word>` (one of
        the words below).``
      - Sections `## 1. The decision` to `## 7. Who has checked it`.
      - Under `## 4. Options`, one line per option: ``- `re-specify` — a new spec superseding…``.
    - What the desk takes from it:
      - the title, which is the first line under `## 1. The decision`;
      - the options, each the backquoted word at the start of a line under `## 4. Options`;
      - the answer path, which is the first backquoted path on the `answer:` line.
    - If the answer path differs from `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<n>.md`, the card is shown with a
      "can't parse" note and is still flagged while unanswered. A misparse never hides a card.
  - **Answers.** An answer is a path matching `^decisions/([A-Z]+-[0-9]+)/([^/]+)-([0-9]+)\.md$`.
    - Files ending `-notes.md` are the Chief of Staff's notes, not answers. Sample:
      `decisions/P-001/dor-fail-1-notes.md`.
    - `decisions/questions/` doesn't match `^[A-Z]+-[0-9]+$`, so it is skipped. It holds rulings,
      not card answers.
    - A card is answered when its answer path is in the tree, whether or not the card file still
      exists.
    - The verdict is the text after `Decision: ` on line 1 of the answer blob. The page fetches the
      answer blob only when the dispatch log has no `decision` entry for it (J6), for example in
      the minute before the Orchestrator logs it, or for AC16's "already answered". Sample: `decisions/P-001/stop-6.md` line 1 reads
      `Decision: re-scope`. Later lines such as `Proxy:` are ignored.
  - **Raised and answered times (v3), from the dispatch log** (J9's source, with no extra requests).
    - Raised: the `time` of the entry with `"action":"card"` and matching `item`, `gate` and `card`.
      Sample: `{"action":"card","card":6,"gate":"stop","item":"P-001",…,"time":"2026-10-06T18:51:54Z"}`.
    - Answered: the `time` of the entry with `"trigger":"decision"` and `"record"` equal to the
      answer path. That entry also gives `word` (the verdict) and `proxy`. Sample:
      `{"card":6,…,"proxy":false,"record":"decisions/P-001/stop-6.md",…,"time":"2026-10-06T21:31:02Z",…,"word":"re-scope"}`.
    - Checked against git (C): the Orchestrator logs a card 33–40 s before its commit, and logs an
      answer 13–42 s after the owner's commit, in the three cases checked (P-001 stop-6, P-001
      failure-5, Q-003 failure-1). That is well inside V5's hour scale.
    - Two months are read, the current one and the one before (`dispatch-log/<YYYY-MM>.jsonl`), so
      AC20's 30 days are covered across a month boundary.
  - **Owner questions** (both models). Paths `^questions/([^/]+)\.md$`, except
    `questions/_TEMPLATE.md`.
    - **The form**, from `questions/_TEMPLATE.md` lines 1–9 and the real sample
      `questions/Q-005-sources.md` at 4f2439a:
      - Line 1: `# Ruling needed: <title>`.
      - Then labelled lines `WHY:`, then `OPTIONS:`, followed by option lines `  A. <choice> —
        <text>`.
      - Then `RECOMMENDATION: <letter> — <reason>`, then
        `RISK CLASS: <c>   REVERSIBILITY: <r>   BLAST RADIUS: <b>`, then
        `DEFAULT: <letter>   TIMEOUT: <hours>`.
    - **What the desk takes from it** (AC12):
      - the title;
      - each option, from a line matching `^\s+([A-Z])\. (.+)$` between `OPTIONS:` and the next
        line starting with a capital label and `:`;
      - the recommendation letter, the first `[A-Z]` after `RECOMMENDATION:`;
      - the risk class, reversibility, blast radius, default and timeout, each the text after its
        label up to the next label or the end of the line.
      - Sample Q-005-sources gives options `A` (Accept) and `B` (Strict), recommendation `A`,
        `RISK CLASS: R0`, `REVERSIBILITY: reversible`, `DEFAULT: A` and `TIMEOUT: 24`.
      - A label that is missing shows "not given". If line 1 is missing, the file name stands in as
        the title. The question is flagged either way.
    - **The answer (v3):** `decisions/questions/<name>.md`, line 1 `Ruling: <choice>`. Samples:
      `decisions/questions/Q-005-sources.md` (`Ruling: A`) and
      `decisions/questions/P-001-build-path.md`.
      - `-notes.md` files there are notes, not answers: for example
        `decisions/questions/Q-005-sources-notes.md`.
      - A question is answered when its answer path is in the tree, whether or not the question file
        still exists. The Chief of Staff deletes it later, as in e012edb.
    - **Raised time:** the oldest commit of `questions/<name>.md` (J1's history request). That
      works for a deleted path too; GitHub REST listing a deleted path's history is C.
    - **Answered time:** the oldest commit of `decisions/questions/<name>.md` (v3).
    - **Cost:** both are fixed once known, so the page keeps them by path and never asks again.
      - For a ruling, the page first reads the answer's time, then the question's only if the
        answer falls within AC20's 30 days.
      - A ruling whose question path has no history was asked in a session, not by file. It is not
        a desk question and isn't listed (AC20).
    - **Answered questions in v2.5:** these can't be found from the tree. The file is deleted and
      the ruling goes to the ledger. AC20 shows open v2.5 questions only, with "answered questions:
      not recorded (operating model v2.5.1 records rulings in the ledger)".
  - **Pull requests.** From the raw PR pages (J1), the page reads `number`, `title`, `html_url`,
    `draft`, `base.ref`, `head.ref`, `head.repo.full_name`, `user.login` and `created_at`.
    - **`head.repo.full_name`** is the repository the PR's branch lives in. It is `null` when a
      fork has been deleted. The `item/` exemption (Terms) applies only when `head.ref` is
      `item/<id>` (one path segment of letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`) **and**
      `head.repo.full_name` equals the project's `repo` in J7, compared without regard to case. A
      fork's PR from a branch named `item/x` is flagged like any other (PR #40 review N7; built in
      PR #41). It comes in the same list response, so it costs no request.
    - The raised time shown for a flagged PR is `created_at`.
    - A PR that was a draft and later marked ready is shown with its `created_at` too; the list
      doesn't give the ready time (C).
  - **Time asked, and V5.**
    - Each card's wait is answered minus raised. An open card's wait is now minus raised.
    - AC20's median is over cards raised in the last 14 days, open ones included with their wait so
      far.
    - V5 (AC21) uses counted time across all v3 projects, not elapsed time. A card's counted wait is
      the total length of the overlap between [raised, answered) (or [raised, now) while open) and
      the `v5Clock` windows.
      - Launch values, as the owner confirmed (see V5): 07:00–22:00, Monday to Friday, in
        `ownerTimeZone`, America/Chicago.
      - Daylight-saving changes are handled by computing window edges in that zone (C: this relies
        on the browser's `Intl` time-zone data).
      - Every card raised in the last 14 days counts, and an open card counts with its counted wait
        so far. With an even count, the median is the mean of the two middle values.
      - Example: a card raised Friday 21:00 and answered Monday 08:00 counts 1 h (Friday 21:00–22:00)
        plus 1 h (Monday 07:00–08:00), so 2 h.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **Gate with hyphens:** `queue/P-001-dor-fail-2.md` → item `P-001`, gate `dor-fail`, n `2`,
      answer `decisions/P-001/dor-fail-2.md`.
    - **Answer before its log entry:** the card is answered (no longer flagged), and its answered
      time shows "recording…". It is left out of the medians until the entry appears.
    - **Answer committed before the card:** still answered.
    - **Card file deleted without an answer:** no longer flagged; activity "withdrawn".
    - **Card with no `card` log entry:** raised time "not recorded"; still flagged while open, and
      left out of the medians.
    - **Answer file with extra lines:** only line 1 is read. Sample: `decisions/P-001/dor-fail-2.md`,
      which has a `Proxy:` line.
    - **Question whose path has no history:** raised "not recorded", still flagged.
    - **A question answered and its file deleted in one commit** (e012edb): answered, with both
      times read from history.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J2` covers it. From M2 it adds two hold-card fixtures written
    by `hold_card`'s exact format, and one merge-card fixture for each step (`conflict`,
    `by-hand`) written by `MergeGate.fail`'s exact format, with a `status/merges.jsonl` line in its
    real form (`{"item", "item_tip", "merge", "spec"}`, orchestrator_git.py:2873) and recorded
    compare answers. No real hold or merge card is on main today. It uses the real files: `queue/P-001-stop-6.md`,
    `queue/P-001-dor-fail-2.md`, `queue/Q-003-failure-1.md`, `decisions/P-001/stop-6.md`,
    `decisions/P-001/dor-fail-2.md`, `decisions/P-001/dor-fail-1-notes.md`, `queue/README.md`,
    `questions/_TEMPLATE.md`, `questions/Q-005-sources.md` at 4f2439a and
    `decisions/questions/Q-005-sources.md`, plus the dispatch-log lines quoted above.

- **J3 — Desk page → GitHub new-file page (card or question answer).**
  - **URL:** `https://github.com/<repo>/new/<defaultBranch>?filename=<path>&<contentParam>=<content>`,
    with every parameter percent-encoded (UTF-8).
    - `<repo>` and `<defaultBranch>` come from J7. The branch is always the default branch, also
      when `webCommitsToDefault` is `false`; in that case the owner picks "new branch" in GitHub's
      dialog (AC18).
    - `<contentParam>` is the repository's `contentParam` in J7 (`value` unless EXP-004 records
      another name). It is omitted when `contentPrefill` is `false` (AC17).
    - `<path>` is the card's answer path, or `decisions/questions/<name>.md` for a question (J2).
    - **Card content:** `Decision: <option>\n`, where `<option>` is the card's option word exactly
      as the card lists it.
    - **Question content:** `Ruling: <letter>\n\nQuestion: questions/<name>.md\n`, where `<letter>`
      is the option's letter. Line 1 matches today's rulings (`decisions/questions/Q-005-sources.md:1`,
      `Ruling: A`). No `Proxy:` line is written: the owner commits it themselves.
    - In both, if the owner typed a note, a blank line and the note follow.
  - **From MD (AC53, 2026-10-14):**
    - **The content** is the text in the response sheet's box as the owner leaves it. The box
      starts with the forms above. For a gate card it adds, after the empty line 2, `Into: ` with
      recycle and `Reason: ` with hold and recycle.
    - **The owner's verdict on a pull request** uses the same URL form, but at that pull request's
      head branch instead of the default branch:
      `https://github.com/<repo>/new/<head.ref>?filename=reviews%2F<PR>%2Fowner.md&<contentParam>=<content>`.
      `<head.ref>` comes from J1's open-PR list, percent-encoded. When the pull request's
      `head.repo.full_name` is not `<repo>` (a fork, or a deleted one), no link is made and the text
      is offered to copy. Its content starts as
      `Verdict: PASS\n\nAny suggestions to commit along with the verdict:\n\n`. AC18's message
      doesn't apply to it. AC17's fallback does.
    - So "the branch is always the default branch" holds for cards and questions only.
  - **From MD (AC53, trial-view note 15, 2026-10-15):** in the sandbox, a question's content also
    carries the question and its options as they were put (decisions/README.md), and a question
    file with numbered questions gives one `Ruling:` line naming each answered number, as AC53
    sets out. The URL form, the path and the fallbacks are unchanged. Outside the sandbox the
    forms above stand.
  - **Example** for `queue/P-001-dor-4.md`, option `build`, no note:
    `https://github.com/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/new/main?filename=decisions%2FP-001%2Fdor-4.md&value=Decision%3A%20build%0A`.
  - **Example** for `questions/Q-005-sources.md`, option `A`, no note:
    `https://github.com/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/new/main?filename=decisions%2Fquestions%2FQ-005-sources.md&value=Ruling%3A%20A%0A%0AQuestion%3A%20questions%2FQ-005-sources.md%0A`.
  - **Sources:** `filename` is described by GitHub (F-gh-05, B). The content parameter is
    undocumented (C). Its name is config, not spec text, so EXP-004's finding changes J7 only.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **A note with `&`, `#`, `%`, emoji or newlines:** encoded, and checked by a round trip.
    - **A link longer than `linkCap`:** AC17's fallback.
    - **An answer file that already exists:** "already answered: <verdict>", and no link (AC16).
    - **A default branch that is not `main`:** taken from J7.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J3` covers it.

- **J4 — Desk page → planning record → Chief of Staff.**
  - **Where:** file `docs/planning/<YYYY-MM-DD>T<HHMM>Z-<nn>.md` in Service-Desk (the J7 entry
    with `"planning": true`; P-001-build-path.md, question 2). The owner commits it through a
    J3-form link.
    - `<nn>` is always two digits: `01` for the first plan in that minute, then `02`, up to `99`.
      The page picks the lowest `<nn>` not already in the tree under `docs/planning/`.
  - **Ordering rule:** the current plan is the file whose name is greatest in plain byte (ASCII)
    order.
    - Names have fixed-width fields, so this order is time order. Example:
      `2026-11-02T0930Z-01.md` < `2026-11-02T0930Z-02.md` < `2026-11-02T0931Z-01.md`.
    - Files not matching `^[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}T[0-9]{4}Z-[0-9]{2}\.md$` are ignored for
      ordering and shown as activity, "can't parse".
  - **Content:**
    ```
    Plan: <YYYY-MM-DD>T<HHMM>Z-<nn>
    Supersedes: <previous plan file name, or none>

    | Order | Project | Item | Not before (UTC) |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 1 | Service-Desk | P-002 | 2026-11-02T09:00Z |
    | 2 | Personal-Org-Operating-Model | sprint m1.4 | — |
    ```
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **An item finished between drafting and committing:** kept in the record; the Chief of Staff
      skips it and notes it in the ledger.
    - **Two plans in one minute:** the second link gets `-02`.
    - **Two links drafted with the same name:** GitHub refuses the second because the file exists.
      The page offers the next free `<nn>` after its next read.
    - **An empty plan:** allowed; it means no ordering preference.
    - **Owner commits to `docs/`:** `docs/**` is the Orchestrator's surface in
      governance/SURFACES.md, but a human commit may touch any path (surface_guard.py:320–322).
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J4` covers it, including `-01`/`-02` and a minute boundary.

- **J5 — Device record → weekly metrics file → Chief of Staff's weekly check.**
  - **Storage:** on the device, in the browser's local storage under key `desk-metrics-v1`. Every
    read and write is wrapped so that a failure (private window, cleared storage) shows "metrics not
    recorded on this device" and nothing else breaks.
  - **Weekly commit:** to `docs/metrics/<YYYY>-W<ww>.md` (ISO week, e.g. `2026-W46`) in
    Service-Desk.
  - **Content:**
    ```
    Week: 2026-W46   Device: phone
    V5 median (weekday cards): 2h10m over 7 cards
    ## Open windows (UTC)
    | Start | End |
    |---|---|
    | 2026-11-10T07:02:11Z | 2026-11-10T07:09:40Z |
    ## First shown
    | Project | Item | First shown (UTC) |
    |---|---|---|
    | Service-Desk | queue/P-002-dor-1.md | 2026-11-10T07:03:05Z |
    ## Didn't need me
    | Project | Item | Marked (UTC) |
    |---|---|---|
    ```
  - **The Chief of Staff's counts:**
    - V1: flagged items whose raised time falls inside an open window and whose first-shown time
      is more than 5 minutes later, or missing while windows continued.
    - V2: the rows under "Didn't need me".
    - V5: the median line.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **Two devices in one week:** one file per device, `<YYYY>-W<ww>-<device>.md`.
    - **A window still open at the week's end:** split at Monday 00:00 UTC.
    - **Local storage cleared:** the file says "records from <date>".
    - **A skipped week:** the next offer covers every uncommitted week, one file each.
    - **A link too long:** AC17's copy fallback.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J5` covers it.

- **J6 — Desk page ↔ desk function.** Both calls are `POST` with a JSON body, so the ETags travel in
  the body, not in a header.
  - **Request checks (D5.2, before J8's work is used for anything):**
    - **Content type:** the function answers 415 unless `Content-Type` is `application/json`. A
      JSON content type is not a "simple" request, so a cross-site page can't send one without a
      CORS preflight (C, browser behaviour).
    - **Origin:** the function answers 403 when an `Origin` header is present and differs from the
      origin of the request's own URL (scheme, host and port of `request.url`). That covers the
      preview, `needs-you` and each custom domain without a list.
    - **No CORS:** the function sends no CORS headers, so no other origin can read a response.
    - **Methods:** any method other than `POST` on `/api/*` gets 405.
  - **Poll call:** `POST /api/poll`.
    - Request body:
      `{ "project": "<name>", "head": "<sha>" | null, "etags": { "<GitHub URL>": "<etag>" } }`.
    - `etags` holds only the poll's own list URLs: the branch, up to 3 PR pages and up to 3
      workflow-run pages (from M2, up to 3 for each of J1's 3a and 3b), so **at most 10 entries,
      under 3 KB**.
    - Blobs need no ETags (J1), so the ETag state no longer grows with the repository. That
      resolves the plan review's finding 6.
  - **Poll response:**
    - `{ "project", "readAt": "<ISO UTC>",`
    - `"state": "ok" | "unchanged" | "partial" | "cant-read",`
    - `"reason": null | "token" | "rate-limit" | "github" | "config" | "too-many",`
    - `"retryAfter": <seconds> | null,`
    - `"head": { "sha", "treeSha", "committedAt" },`
    - `"etags": { url: etag },`
    - `"pulls": [ "<raw page>" | null ], "checks": [ "<raw page>" | null ],`
    - `"tree": "<raw>" | null }`

    A `null` page means 304: the page reuses what it parsed last time for that URL. `tree` is
    present only when the head changed or `head` was null.
    - From M2, `checks` holds the workflow-run pages (J1, AC29): 3a's pages, then 3b's, each
      named by its URL in the response as today. The field keeps its name, so the page's side of J6
      changes only in what it parses. When workflow runs answered 403, the
      response says so (today's `checksState: "cant-read"`), and CI shows AC29's words.
  - **Blob call:** `POST /api/blobs`.
    - Request: `{ "project", "blobs": ["<40-hex sha>", …], "history": ["<path>", …],
      "compare": [{ "base": "<40-hex tip>", "head": "<40-hex sha>" }, …] }` (`compare` from M2,
      AC47; optional).
    - **Budget:** each blob counts 1 against `BLOB_BATCH` (25), each history path counts 2,
      because J1 may fetch its last page, and each compare counts 1.
    - Response: `{ "project", "readAt", "state", "reason", "retryAfter",
      "blobs": { sha: "<raw text>" | null }, "history": { path: ["<raw page 1>", "<raw last page>"?] },
      "compare": { "<base>...<head>": "<cut body>" | null } }`.
    - **A compare answer is cut, not raw** (PR #44 review N1): GitHub's compare body carries
      `files` with their patches and a `commits` list, which can run to megabytes. The function
      reads the body only up to the first `"commits"` key, and never more than 65,536 characters
      (`COMPARE_SCAN_CHARS`), then stops the download. From that head it takes `status` (`ahead`,
      `behind`, `identical` or `diverged`) and, when found, `ahead_by`, `behind_by` and
      `total_commits`, and returns them as one small JSON object, for example
      `{"status":"ahead","ahead_by":2,"behind_by":0,"total_commits":2}`. The body is never parsed
      whole. The page reads only `status`.
    - A `null` compare is a failed read (the card stays flagged): GitHub refused it, or no status
      was found in the part read. Only a rate limit or a 401 stops the rest of the call.
    - **Refusals (400):**
      - a project name not in J7;
      - a sha that isn't 40 hex digits;
      - a history path that doesn't match `^(decisions/)?questions/[^/]+\.md$` or (from M2, AC46)
        `^queue/ci-hold-[0-9a-f]{12}\.md$`;
      - a compare `base` or `head` that isn't 40 lowercase hex digits (from M2, AC47);
      - a budget over 25.
    - The function only ever issues J1's request forms against configured repositories. *From
      2026-10-16 (J12):* and J12's one GraphQL query, with its fixed text, through the blob call's
      optional `pulls` field, which counts 5 against the budget; a malformed cursor is refused
      (400).
  - **The page's side.**
    - **Parsed cache:** the page keeps parsed results keyed by blob sha in local storage, and fetches
      only blobs whose sha it lacks. If storage is unavailable, it holds them in memory for the
      open window.
    - **Diffing:** it compares each new tree with the cached one to find changed paths.
    - **Which blobs it fetches:**
      - **For v3:**
        - every `status/<item>.toml`, and `governance/ROUTING.toml` (time limits, J9);
        - from M2: the newest hold card only (AC46; J2's "Only the newest counts"). With several
          hold cards in the tree, each one's history is read once to learn its creation time (and
          kept), then only the newest's blob is fetched; the older ones are never read. Also every
          merge card in the tree, and `status/merges.jsonl` when a merge card is present (AC47);
        - the two dispatch-log months, and `status/outcomes.jsonl` (M2);
        - open cards;
        - each card answer blob that has no `decision` log entry yet (J2);
        - each `decisions/questions/<name>.md` whose question is listed in AC20 (for its
          `Ruling:` line);
        - the current plan (Service-Desk only).
      - **For v2.5:** `docs/LEDGER.md`, plus the files in `docs/sprints/` other than `PROGRESS.md`
        and `SPRINT_PLAN.md`. In Personal-Org-Operating-Model that is `m1.1-foundation.md`,
        `m1.2-enforcement-and-pilot.md`, `m1.3-pilot-and-s004.md` and `m1.4-v3-build.md`.
      - **For both:** every open owner question.
      - **History reads:** those J2 names.
    - **When a project counts as read:** only when every needed blob has arrived (AC4).
  - **Reasons the page sets itself:**
    - The function sets the reasons above.
    - The page adds three on its own record of the project, never received from the function:
      - `cloudflare`: the call returned a 5xx or a Cloudflare error page, including Error 1027 and
        1102;
      - `network`: the call didn't complete, including a call with no full answer within the page's
        30 s call timeout (`CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`; EXP-001's longest call was 1.9 s, so it stands);
      - `page`: the page threw an error while applying the response or reading the project's
        records. Polling of that project and every other continues; the next poll is still
        scheduled (PR #40 review N4, built in PR #41).
    - All three show in AC5's "Can't read since HH:MM", with the reason in words. When the same
      reason repeats with different detail, the words follow the latest failure, and "since" keeps
      the first (AC34).
    - A 403 from the function means the Access JWT is invalid. The page shows "Signed out — reload
      to sign in", never "Quiet", and no render error hides it (AC32).
  - **Cost field:** each response carries `"cost": { "github": <requests made>, "bytes": <body
    bytes returned> }`, so EXP-001 and the Chief of Staff can see per-call load.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J6` covers it.

- **J7 — `src/config/projects.json` → function and page.** Committed in Service-Desk under
  `src/`, read at build time. The form with the launch values:
  ```json
  { "ownerLogin": "shumpmaster",
    "ownerTimeZone": "America/Chicago",
    "v5Clock": { "days": ["Mon", "Tue", "Wed", "Thu", "Fri"], "from": "07:00", "to": "22:00" },
    "linkCap": 6000,
    "projects": [
      { "name": "Service-Desk", "repo": "shumpmaster/Service-Desk", "defaultBranch": "main",
        "model": "v3", "planning": true,
        "webCommitsToDefault": true, "contentPrefill": true, "contentParam": "value" },
      { "name": "Personal-Org-Operating-Model", "repo": "shumpmaster/Personal-Org-Operating-Model",
        "defaultBranch": "main", "model": "v2.5", "planning": false,
        "webCommitsToDefault": true, "contentPrefill": true, "contentParam": "value" } ] }
  ```
  - **Sources for the values:**
    - `repo` and `defaultBranch`: `git remote -v` and the REST repository read on 2026-10-06 (both
      `main`; observed, C).
    - `ownerLogin`: the owner's GitHub login, as in both remotes and every PR's author.
    - `ownerTimeZone` and `v5Clock`: V5 as the owner confirmed it
      (P-001-s001-owner-questions.md, answer 1), also the display time zone. It matches the `-05:00` offsets on the owner's answer
      commits in October (C).
    - **Turning the clock off:** `"v5Clock": null` means elapsed clock time, every day.
    - `webCommitsToDefault`, `contentPrefill`, `contentParam` and `linkCap`: EXP-004's recorded
      results. Service-Desk's `webCommitsToDefault: true` already matches observation: the owner
      committed `decisions/P-001/stop-6.md` to `main` from the web (50b73d7, C).
  - **What `ownerLogin` is used for.** After the owner's ruling 1, flagging no longer depends on the
    login: every open non-draft PR into the default branch is flagged. The login is used only to
    label a PR "yours" or "opened by <login>". The Builder must not use it to filter flags.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **6 entries:** the build fails with a message (AC26).
    - **A repository the token can't read:** AC5 at run time, not a build failure.
    - **A wrong `defaultBranch`:** AC5 with reason `config`.
    - **A `model` other than `v3` or `v2.5`:** the build fails.
    - **No entry with `planning: true`:** "Work ahead" can't record plans and says so.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J7` covers it.

- **J8 — Cloudflare Access → desk function.**
  - **What is checked:** the `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` header, validated with the team's public keys
    (`https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com/cdn-cgi/access/certs`, C for the path).
    - **The signature, against the public key:** library/F-auth-04.md:8 (A). That is all the entry
      supports.
    - **The expiry (`exp`), the audience (`aud` contains one of the tags in `ACCESS_AUD`: the preview has two Access
      applications, F-cf-04) and `email`
      equal to the owner's address:** standard JWT claims, and our design. These are not in the
      library (C).
    - **The issuer (`iss`)** equals `https://<team domain>` exactly, where the team domain is
      `ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN` trimmed and lowercased first (AC33). A token another Access team signed
      never passes (PR #40 review N9, built in PR #41; PR #41 review N3). That Access writes `iss`
      in this form is C; the preview's working login is the observation so far.
    - **No test-only path.** J8 has one rule for every request. EXP-001 is driven by the owner's
      signed-in browser (EXP-001 Method). An Access service token carries no `email`, so it could
      never pass J8, and no exception is made for it.
  - **Settings:** the owner's address, the team domain and the audience tag are Pages environment
    variables (`OWNER_EMAIL`, `ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN`, `ACCESS_AUD`). The owner sets them by hand
    (Setup, step C4).
  - **When:** the check runs on every request, before any GitHub call.
  - **Key fetch:** at most one outbound request per invocation, and none while the isolate still
    holds the keys. It counts within J1's limits. Its CPU (signature verification) is part of every
    call's CPU, and EXP-001 measures it.
  - **Awkward cases:** those listed in AC13, and keys rotated mid-session (refetch once, then 403).
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J8` covers it.

- **J9 — Pipeline records → read model (page side).** The real samples below were read on
  2026-10-06.
  - **v3 status files.** Path `status/<item>.toml`, where `<item>` matches `^[A-Z]+-[0-9]+$`.
    - Other files in `status/` are not item files: `README.md`, `plan.json`, `processed.json`,
      `merge-gate.json`, `merges.jsonl` and `outcomes.jsonl` (J10's source).
    - Form: the Orchestrator's `dump_status` (governance/checks/orchestrator.py:589–593). It writes
      a first comment line, then one `key = value` line per field, in the order of `FIELD_ORDER`
      (orchestrator.py:114–116).
    - Each value is one of: `true` or `false`, an integer, a JSON string, or a JSON list
      (`_toml_value`, orchestrator.py:579–586).
    - The page reads only that subset. It matches `^([a-z_]+) = (.+)$` and parses the value as
      JSON. Any other line is named in `notes` and skipped.
    - **Field types (AC31, from M2).** Each value is checked against its type before use. A value of
      the wrong type is named in `notes` (file, line, field) and skipped, and the field shows "not
      recorded":
      - integers: `stage`, `card`, `plan_round`, `build_round`, `attempts`, `sessions`;
      - booleans: `confirm_used`;
      - strings: `kind`, `role`, `last_verdict`, `gate`, `spec`, `outcome`, and `dispatched_at` (an
        ISO UTC time);
      - `state`: a string, one of the six below;
      - unknown keys are ignored.

      Dispatch-log and `outcomes.jsonl` lines are checked the same way: `item`, `role`, `route`,
      `time`, `session`, `result`, `verdict`, `record`, `gate`, `word` and `error` are strings (or
      null where the sample shows null); `stage` and `card` are integers; `proxy` and `strike` are
      booleans. A line with a wrong-typed key it needs for its shape is named and skipped as a whole.
    - **One project at a time (AC30).** Parsing and drawing are guarded per project, so an error in
      one project's records never blanks another's box.
    - Sample (Service-Desk, `status/P-001.toml` at d4d0110):
      ```
      # Written only by the Orchestrator (model S-012 AC1).
      kind = "project"
      stage = 3
      state = "dispatched"
      role = "critic-triage"
      last_verdict = "none"
      plan_round = 3
      build_round = 0
      confirm_used = false
      attempts = 0
      sessions = 6
      dispatched_at = "2026-10-06T18:48:58Z"
      card = 5
      spec = "S-001"
      ```
      The same file is `state = "waiting-owner"` with `gate = "stop"` and `card = 6` at def1e83. It is
      `state = "ready"` with `role = "definer"` at b787280. On main today it is
      `state = "closed"` with `outcome = "re-scope"`.
    - **The rules for each field:**
      - `stage` → its name: 2 Shape, 3 Define, 4 Lock the checks, 5 Produce, 6 Launch, 8 Evidence
        (Personal-Org-Operating-Model `template-v3/intake/_TEMPLATE.md:9`; stages from
        orchestrator.py:90). Any other number shows as "stage <n>".
      - `state` is one of `ready`, `dispatched`, `returned`, `waiting-owner`, `done` or `closed`
        (orchestrator.py:91).
      - **Running now:** `state = "dispatched"`, giving `role` and `dispatched_at`.
        - Its time limit is `[time_limits]` in `governance/ROUTING.toml` (default 30 minutes) plus
          `stall_grace_minutes` (15). A session past that is activity: "running for <d>, past its
          limit".
        - The desk reads ROUTING.toml's `[limits]` and `[time_limits]` tables as `key = <int>`
          lines. If they can't be read, it uses 30 and 15 and notes it.
      - **Waiting on the owner:** `state = "waiting-owner"`, giving `gate` and `card`. It is linked to
        `queue/<item>-<gate>-<card>.md` and flagged per Terms.
      - **Next:** `state = "ready"` → "next: <role> (<stage name>)". `state = "returned"` → "next:
        the Orchestrator routes the <role>'s return (verdict <last_verdict>)".
      - **Finished:** `done` → "done". `closed` → "closed: <outcome>".
      - **As of:** the `time` of the latest dispatch-log entry with that `item`.
  - **v3 dispatch log.** Path `dispatch-log/<YYYY-MM>.jsonl`, one JSON object per line. The 192
    lines of `2026-10.jsonl` (2026-10-06) have exactly these shapes:
    - `"trigger":"request"`, with `result: "created"`: an item created.
    - `"action":"dispatch"`: a session starts.
      - Keys: `item`, `role`, `route`, `stage`, `spec`, `time`, `trigger`.
      - Its session id is `<item>:<route>:<time>`, the form S-014 AC10 fixes.
    - `"trigger":"outcome"` with `session`: a session ends.
      - Keys: `result` (`ok`, `error`, `timeout` or `usage-limit`, orchestrator.py:96), `verdict`,
        `record` and `time`.
    - `"action":"card"`: a card raised (J2).
    - `"trigger":"decision"`: an answer read (J2). It may carry `strike: true`.
    - `"action":"done"`: an item done.
    - `"trigger":"anomaly"` with `error`: shown as activity, with the error text.

    **The headline (v3)** is the latest entry, in the words for its shape below. `<t>` is HH:MM in
    `ownerTimeZone`, with the date in front when it isn't today.
    - **request:** "<item> created, <t>".
    - **dispatch:** "<item>: <role> started (<stage name>), <t>".
    - **outcome:** "<item>: <role> finished — <result>, <t>". When `verdict` is neither `none` nor
      null, it reads "— <result>, verdict <verdict>, <t>".
    - **card:** "<item>: waiting on you — card <gate>-<card>, <t>".
    - **decision:**
      - when `result` starts with `closed/`: "<item> closed: <word> (card <gate>-<card>), <t>";
      - otherwise: "<item>: you answered <gate>-<card> with <word>, <t>";
      - " (proxy)" is added when `proxy` is true.
    - **done:** "<item> done, <t>".
    - **anomaly:** "<item>: Orchestrator anomaly, <t>".

    Example: the last line of `2026-10.jsonl` (2026-10-06) gives "P-001 closed: re-scope (card
    stop-6), 16:31", at UTC−5. A line that isn't JSON, or matches no shape, is named in `notes` and
    skipped, and the headline falls back to the previous entry.
  - **v2.5 reduced view** (Personal-Org-Operating-Model at 677fe5a).
    - **Sprint:** the files `docs/sprints/*.md` other than `PROGRESS.md` and `SPRINT_PLAN.md`. The
      open one has a line `status: open`; its title is the first `# ` line.
      - Sample `docs/sprints/m1.4-v3-build.md`: `# m1.4 — v3 build: specs, tools, template and
        agent files` / `status: open`.
      - The other three say `status: closed`.
      - Zero or two open sprints: "sprint unclear", shown as a note, not a flag (AGENTS.md §0 says
        stop and ask; that is the project's business, not the desk's).
    - **Headline:** the last line in `docs/LEDGER.md` matching `^## (L-[0-9]+) — (.+)$`. Sample:
      `## L-0124 — Continue Service-Desk under the full process, with a checkpoint; S-017's
      supersession covers two more token pins`.
    - **Next:** that entry's `licenses_next:` line. Sample: `licenses_next: S-017 build review round
      2; then the owner's verdict on PR 20 and Service-Desk's adoption.`
    - **As of:** that entry's `date:` line.
    - **Items, stages and running now:** "not recorded in this repository's records (operating model
      v2.5.1)".
    - The ledger is 128,438 bytes. It is read only when its blob sha changes.
  - **v3 projects and sprints.** Sprints are retired in v3 (governance/checks/check_all.sh header:
    "Sprints are retired there"), so for `model: v3` the desk reads no sprint file. Service-Desk's
    `docs/sprints/m1.1-first-sprint.md` is the unfilled template (`# m1.1 — <name>`).
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **A dispatch with no outcome while the item is no longer `dispatched`:** shown as "no outcome
      recorded". Real case: Q-006's dispatch at 2026-10-04T01:28:40Z; its outcome was logged under
      session `Q-006:research-to-source:2026-10-04T01:25:01Z`, and the Orchestrator logged an anomaly
      at line 121.
    - **An outcome whose session matches no dispatch:** shown as it stands.
    - **A retry:** a dispatch of the same item and role right after an outcome with
      `result: "error"`. Real case: the P-001 definer, 2026-10-05T22:50:13Z. Shown as activity,
      "retry".
    - **A status file that won't parse:** the item is shown with "can't read status" and is never
      dropped. If its card file is open, it is still flagged via J2.
    - **A file missing:** the field shows null with the note "not found". The project is not
      can't-read.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J9` covers it, using the samples above committed as fixtures
    under `tests/`.

- **J10 — Session records → Agents view (M2, page side).**
  - **Timeline, from today's records.** Sessions come from the dispatch log (J9): each `dispatch`
    entry is joined to the `outcome` entry whose `session` equals `<item>:<route>:<time>`.
    - Start is the dispatch `time` and end is the outcome `time`.
    - `result` and `verdict` come from the outcome.
    - Sample pair: dispatch `{"action":"dispatch",…,"item":"P-001",…,"role":"critic",
      "route":"define-to-plan-review",…,"time":"2026-10-06T16:40:19Z",…}` and outcome
      `{"item":"P-001",…,"record":"reviews/S-001/plan.md","result":"ok","role":"critic",
      "session":"P-001:define-to-plan-review:2026-10-06T16:40:19Z","time":"2026-10-06T18:48:58Z",
      "verdict":"FAIL"}`. That gives a run time of 2 h 8 min 39 s.
  - **Usage figures, from `status/outcomes.jsonl`.** One line per session. The keys before model
    S-020 are `control`, `item`, `record`, `record_sha`, `result`, `role`, `session` and `verdict`
    (sample line 1: `{"control": "22dc…", "item": "Q-001", "record": "research/Q-001-memo.md", …,
    "session": "Q-001:ready-dispatch:2026-10-01T01:46:42Z", "verdict": "none"}`). Lines carry one
    more key, `usage`, from the first session after S-020's adoption (L-0005, 2026-10-08); older
    lines don't, and neither do the Orchestrator's own `error` lines or check runs (S-020 AC2).
    - **The form, frozen by model S-020** (AC2; this replaces the form J10 proposed to D7.1):
      `"usage": {"input_tokens": int|null, "output_tokens": int|null,
      "cache_read_input_tokens": int|null, "cache_creation_input_tokens": int|null,
      "num_turns": int|null, "duration_ms": int|null, "context_window": int|null,
      "autocompact_threshold": int|null, "context_peak": int|null, "derived": ["context_peak"],
      "would_have_stopped": true|false|null, "compactions": int|null,
      "cost_usd_estimate": number|null}`.
    - **Whole numbers or null:** `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`,
      `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `num_turns`, `duration_ms`, `context_window`,
      `autocompact_threshold`, `context_peak` and `compactions`.
    - **Three keys that are not whole numbers:**
      - `derived`, a list of figure names. Any figure named there is labelled "derived from
        per-turn usage" wherever it is shown (today always `context_peak`: the agent tool doesn't
        report it, EXP-003).
      - `would_have_stopped`: `true`, `false` or `null`. It records only; nothing stops a session
        for its context (L-0005).
      - `cost_usd_estimate`: a number or `null`, the agent tool's own estimate. Any view of it says
        "estimate", and it is never presented as a charge: the account is billed by subscription.
    - **Percentages:** `context_peak / context_window` and `context_peak /
      autocompact_threshold`, each one decimal, each needing both its figures and a divisor
      above 0; otherwise "not available" (AC23's rule), while the figures themselves still show. The second is the figure nearer the owner's answer 5 (stopping
      before compaction, P-001-build-path.md).
    - **Cost:** `cost_usd_estimate` is shown as "estimate $x.xx" (two decimals), "not available"
      when null (AC23).
  - **What the view shows for each case:**
    - **A line without `usage`:** "not recorded", whatever the reason (a session before S-020's
      adoption, an Orchestrator `error` line, or `usage` dropped by `record`; AC23).
    - **A key present with `null`, or a figure EXP-003 found the tool doesn't report:**
      "not available", including the threshold percentage, "would have stopped" and the
      compaction count (AC23).
    - **A session in the dispatch log with no `outcomes.jsonl` line:** timeline only.
    - **A `usage` key with a value of the wrong kind:** only that key is marked. It is named in
      `notes` and that figure shows "not recorded"; every other figure on the line still shows
      (not the whole `usage`).
      - For the whole-number keys above, the wrong kind is a non-integer or a negative number
        (`null` is not a wrong kind: it is "not available").
      - For `cost_usd_estimate`, it is anything but a finite number of 0 or more, or `null`.
      - For `derived`, anything but a list of strings; for `would_have_stopped`, anything but
        `true`, `false` or `null`.
      - These checks are deliberately looser than the commit job's: the desk refuses only what it
        can't display meaningfully (non-integers, negatives, non-finite numbers). The commit job's
        upper bounds (10^12 for the figures, 10^6 for the cost) and its exact `derived` value
        (`["context_peak"]`) stay the commit job's; the desk doesn't repeat them.
  - **Size:** `outcomes.jsonl` was 24,974 bytes on 2026-10-06 and is not rotated. It is read only
    when its blob sha changes (see Risks).
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J10` covers it, using the sample pair above and three
    `outcomes.jsonl` lines, each with its exact expected drill-down:
    - **Sample line 1** (real, no `usage`): every usage figure "not recorded".
    - **A fixture line in the frozen form. It is a fixture, not a real sample;** its figures are the
      ones S-020's own test synthesises from EXP-003's run 2:
      `{"control": "<fixture>", "item": "P-001", "record": "reviews/S-001/plan.md", "record_sha":
      "<fixture>", "result": "ok", "role": "critic",
      "session": "P-001:define-to-plan-review:2026-10-09T09:00:00Z", "verdict": "PASS",
      "usage": {"input_tokens": 10, "output_tokens": 707, "cache_read_input_tokens": 168160,
      "cache_creation_input_tokens": 11120, "num_turns": 7, "duration_ms": 18392,
      "context_window": 1000000, "autocompact_threshold": 784000, "context_peak": 36494,
      "derived": ["context_peak"], "would_have_stopped": false, "compactions": 0,
      "cost_usd_estimate": 0.0854731}}`.
      Expected: input 10, output 707, cache read 168,160, cache write 11,120; 7 turns; run time
      18 s; peak 36,494 tokens, derived from per-turn usage, 3.6 % of the window, 4.7 % of the
      compaction threshold; would have stopped: no; 0 compactions; estimate $0.09.
    - **A real line with nulls** (origin/main `status/outcomes.jsonl`, the source-checker session
      `Q-011:research-to-source:2026-10-08T16:59:31Z`, the first line after L-0005; quoted with
      `control` and `record_sha` shortened): `{"control": "2c0b…", "item": "Q-011", "record":
      "research/Q-011-source-check.md", "record_sha": "e60a…", "result": "ok", "role":
      "source-checker", "session": "Q-011:research-to-source:2026-10-08T16:59:31Z", "usage":
      {"autocompact_threshold": null, "cache_creation_input_tokens": 20367,
      "cache_read_input_tokens": 29181, "compactions": null, "context_peak": 20369,
      "context_window": 1000000, "cost_usd_estimate": 0.1560012, "derived": ["context_peak"],
      "duration_ms": 40999, "input_tokens": 8, "num_turns": 11, "output_tokens": 2418,
      "would_have_stopped": null}, "verdict": "PASS"}`.
      Expected: input 8, output 2,418, cache read 29,181, cache write 20,367; 11 turns; run time
      41 s; peak 20,369 tokens, derived from per-turn usage, 2.0 % of the window; % of the
      compaction threshold "not available"; would have stopped "not available"; compactions "not
      available"; estimate $0.16.

- **J11 — Project records → Home, Status, Team, Timeline and Queue (MD sandbox, page side; added
  2026-10-14).** v3 projects only; a v2.5 project shows J9's reduced view and "not recorded" for
  everything here, except `README.md`, which is read for every project (AC54, 2026-10-14).
  - **No new request form.** Every record below is a blob in the tree J1 already reads, fetched
    through J6's blob call by its sha, cached by sha, and fetched again only when its sha changes.
    J6's refusals and the function are unchanged. AC14's test is run with these reads included and
    still passes (AC42; A3 counts them).
  - **Optional, not needed:** none of these reads counts toward "read successfully" (Terms, AC4).
    A project whose J11 reads fail, or haven't arrived yet, is never "Can't read" for that reason:
    only the views that need them show "not available" until they arrive, and its waits and
    "Quiet" are judged on J6's needed reads alone. The new dispatch-log months are the exception:
    J6 already needs the latest two, and the older ones are optional.
  - **What the page adds to J6's blob list:**
    - `docs/PROJECT.md` (47,001 bytes at 3117150): section 3's milestone lines and section 9's line
      "The owner is the user.";
    - every `dispatch-log/<YYYY-MM>.jsonl`, not only the latest two months. A past month's sha
      doesn't change, so each is read once;
    - `status/merges.jsonl`, always (J6 reads it today only when a merge card is present);
    - the header lines of every `specs/S-*.md` (the whole blob is fetched; S-001.md is 173,985 bytes
      at 3117150 and is read only when its sha changes);
    - `intake/requests/Q-*.toml`, for the research question the model's timeline reads;
    - `governance/SURFACES.md`, for its roster block (the members, AC49);
    - `.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml`, for its `cron:` line (the Orchestrator's schedule);
    - for an open gate card, its case and the Critic's view, when the tree holds them (AC52).
    - *Added 2026-10-14 (AC54, trial-view notes 1):* `README.md` at the tree's root, for the brief
      project summary on each Home card. This one read is made for **every** connected project, a
      v2.5 project too; it is the only J11 read a v2.5 project makes. It is optional like the
      rest: it counts in A3's arithmetic, never toward "read successfully", and a failed or
      missing read shows only on the card's summary (AC54). Service-Desk's is 1,974 bytes at
      4433c4f.
    - *Added in draft 4 (AC70):* the answer record named by each boundary decision line that starts
      a run of AC57's runs 2 and later (`record`, for example `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<n>.md`),
      when the tree holds it: a few small blobs, read once per sha.
    - *Added 2026-10-16 (AC57 to AC61, trial-view notes 2):* every verdict file under
      `reviews/<n>/` in the tree, for each sprint pull request the Timeline shows (AC60's review
      mark): a small blob each, read once per sha. Names starting `_`, and README.md, are not
      read. Nothing else is added: docs/PROJECT.md, the research requests and
      `status/merges.jsonl` are already read, and the research memos are only looked for in the
      tree, never fetched.
  - **What the page reads from docs/PROJECT.md, from 2026-10-16** (AC59, AC61): besides section
    3's milestone lines and section 9's line, section 3's ` · why: ` ends and its standard-milestone
    lines (AC61 (a) and (b)), and the first two items of the list under `### Purpose` in
    `## Intent` (AC59's two cards). The why end is taken off a milestone line before the line is
    matched to its form, so a line with a why is never "out of form" for that reason. Until the
    model's own timeline (`v3_checks.py trace`) reads lines with a why, the fixture's `NOTE:`
    lines are made from J11's fixture as it is, and AC61's fixture, which has why lines, is
    checked against the same expected runs and sprints: a why changes neither.
  - **The timeline: the model's rules, not the desk's own.** Phase runs, their labels
    ("<Phase> i<n>"), the standard milestones' states, the project milestones' placing and sprints,
    and the Graduated mark follow model S-023 AC7's timeline. Its reference implementation is
    `governance/checks/v3_checks.py` (`Timeline`). It reads the same records:
    - `docs/PROJECT.md`, the dispatch log, the status files, `status/merges.jsonl`, the specs and
      the research requests;
    - a routed line's phase comes from its `stage` (1–2 Discovery, 3 Design, 4–5 Build, 6 Launch),
      and its iteration from the gate words that send work back through a gate, as the model's
      `iterations` does (governance/checks/orchestrator.py:299–317 and :127–130, model S-023 AC6).
    - `python3 governance/checks/v3_checks.py trace --root .` prints that timeline as `NOTE:` lines.
      For Service-Desk at 3117150 it gives the runs Discovery i1, Design i1, Build i1, Design i2,
      Build i2 and Launch i1, and the milestones placed in them (MD1, MD2 and MD3 in Design i2).
  - **The fixture (the test oracle).** The Builder commits, under `tests/desk/fixtures/`,
    Service-Desk's records at a fixed commit and the `NOTE:` lines that command printed for that
    commit. The tests for AC50 and AC51 check the desk's runs, labels, states and sprints against
    those lines.
    - The `NOTE:` lines mark no run as "now". So the fixture also holds the expected now run under
      AC50's rule, which is mine (C), not the model's: at 3117150 it is Design i2, because MD2 is
      its first project milestone with no sprint.
    - If the model's rules change in a later model version, the fixture is regenerated in the pull
      request that adopts that version.
  - **The Orchestrator's schedule.** The page reads the first `- cron: "<expr>"` line of
    `.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml` (today `*/30 * * * *`, line 17).
    - Only the form `*/<n> * * * *` is understood. The next run is the next UTC minute divisible by
      `<n>`, shown as "due <t> by its schedule": GitHub may start a scheduled run late (C).
    - Any other form, or a file it can't read, gives "due time not available".
  - **The roster.** The lines of `governance/SURFACES.md`'s ```` ```roster ```` block, each
    `<id> <class> <status> <authority>`, with `#` lines skipped. A missing or malformed block gives
    the members the dispatch log names, and a note.
  - **Links.** A file link is `https://github.com/<repo>/blob/<defaultBranch>/<path>`, made only for a
    path the tree holds. A pull request link is `https://github.com/<repo>/pull/<n>`. A merge's link
    is `https://github.com/<repo>/commit/<sha>`, with the `merge` sha from `status/merges.jsonl`. A
    workflow run link is that run's `html_url` from J1's list.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **No dispatch-log line for any project item:** no runs. The phases show "not recorded", as
      the model's timeline prints them with every standard milestone "not recorded".
    - **A milestone line out of form** in section 3: skipped with a note, as `v3_checks.py trace`
      fails it; the rest still show.
    - **A spec with no `milestones:` header:** it ties no merge to a milestone.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J11` covers it, with the fixture above, a cron line in each
    form, and a malformed roster block. From 2026-10-14 it also checks that `README.md` is fetched
    for a v2.5 project as well as a v3 one, and that a failed README read leaves the project's
    "read successfully" and "Quiet" unchanged. From 2026-10-16 it also checks the why ends and
    standard lines of AC61's fixture, the Purpose items, and that the `reviews/<n>/` verdict files
    are fetched and the `_` files are not.

- **J12 — Pull requests → Timeline sprints (MD sandbox; added 2026-10-16, AC60, trial-view notes 2).**
  v3 projects only. The Timeline needs, for each sprint that is a pull request, its title, body,
  state, merge time, head branch and check state, and the list of pull requests merged into the
  default branch, for those no milestone names (AC60). Today the desk reads only open pull
  requests (J1's `pulls?state=open`), so it has none of this for a merged one.
  - **Why not J1's REST list** (C): each pull request in GitHub's REST list is about 18 to 20 KB,
    because it carries the head and base repositories in full (tests/desk/fixtures/github/
    pulls-page1.json: two items of 17,602 and 20,114 characters). A hundred merged ones would be
    about 2 MB per page, through the function on every read. GitHub's GraphQL API returns only the
    fields asked for, a hundred to a page.
  - **The request:** one new request form, `POST https://api.github.com/graphql` with the read token
    (Setup G1). Its query text is fixed in the function; the page never sends query text. Its
    variables are the repository's owner and name and its default branch, all from J7, and
    `after`, a cursor the page passes back. It asks for the default branch's pull requests,
    newest first by creation, 100 to a page: for each, `number`, `title`, `body`, `state`,
    `createdAt`, `mergedAt`, `closedAt`, `headRefName`, `mergeCommit { oid }` (from draft 4, for
    AC72), and the check rollup state of its last commit; and the page's `hasNextPage` and
    `endCursor`.
  - **Through the blob call** (J6), as an optional field: `"pulls": { "after": null | "<cursor>" }`.
    It counts 5 against the call's budget of 25 (C). The function refuses (400) a cursor that is
    not 1 to 200 characters of `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `+`, `/`, `=`, `_` or `-`. It returns the
    answer's text raw, unparsed, as `"pulls": "<raw>" | null`, as it returns blobs; `null` is a
    failed read. Only a rate limit or a 401 stops the rest of the call, as for compare (J6).
  - **When the page reads it:** after the needed reads, like J11's reads (optional; never counted
    toward "read successfully"):
    - **cold:** once when the project's records first load on the device, and again on the first
      load each day after midnight in `ownerTimeZone` (C; plan review round 2, N5: so a pull
      request outside the newest 100 that was kept as open and merged later reaches the Timeline
      within a day), following `hasNextPage` for at most 3 pages (300 pull requests, J1's
      `MAX_PAGES`), one page per blob call;
    - **on a head change** (a merge moves the head): the first page only, the newest 100. Each
      pull request on it replaces the one kept under its number; the older ones kept stay as they
      are (plan review round 1, N6; A3 counts this). So a body edited after its merge shows from
      the next head change only if the pull request is among the newest 100 (a known gap, C).
    An open pull request's body and title come from J1's open list on every poll, as today.
  - **What the page keeps** (N6): for each pull request, only what AC60, AC61 and AC72 use: its
    number, title, state, `mergedAt`, `createdAt`, head branch, merge commit, check state (or the
    field error's message),
    and from its body only the `Milestone:` line and the Context and Why sections as AC61 (c)
    reads them; never the rest of the body. At most 300 are kept, the newest. They are kept on the
    device in a storage of their own, under the key `desk-pulls-v1`, wrapped as J5's storage is
    but apart from the blob cache, so that a write that fails there (a full or blocked storage)
    turns off only this key, never the blob cache (src/lib/store.js turns off a whole store on
    one failed write); then they are held in memory for the open window and read again on the
    next load.
  - **What the page uses:** every pull request whose base is the default branch, whatever its
    state; a merged one is one whose `mergedAt` is set (N6). The check rollup state gives AC60's
    checks mark: `SUCCESS`; `FAILURE` or `ERROR`; `PENDING` or `EXPECTED`; none.
  - **The answer's own errors** (N6). The function does not parse the answer, so the page judges
    it:
    - a 200 answer whose `data` is `null` or missing, or that holds no `repository`, is a failed
      read, with the first error's `message` as the reason;
    - an error whose `type` is `RATE_LIMITED`, in a 200 answer or a 403, is a rate limit for J12
      only: the page makes no J12 read until the next head change or for 60 minutes, whichever is
      later (C). The REST polls are not slowed by it;
    - an error whose `path` ends in the check rollup field, with the rest of `data` present: the
      rest is used, and each checks mark reads "– checks" with "the desk's token can't read
      checks" one tap away (AC60).
  - **Not known yet** (C): whether the read token's permissions (Setup G1: Contents, Metadata,
    Pull requests and Actions, each read) let GraphQL give a commit's check rollup. The desk shows
    which it is to the owner, on every checks mark, with its reason one tap away (AC60). The owner
    or the Chief of Staff records it in Setup CS13, outside these sessions (N7). No token
    permission is changed for it.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **More than 300 pull requests:** the older ones are not read, and the Timeline says so once,
      at the bottom: "older pull requests not read (over 300)". A milestone's sprint among them
      shows its code with "not available".
    - **A refusal, a rate limit or a network failure:** every pull request part of AC60 shows "not
      available", with the reason one tap away (AC41). The project is never "Can't read" for it.
    - **A body edited after the merge:** see "on a head change" above (a known gap, C).
    - **A sprint list naming a pull request J12 does not hold** (another base, or a number that is
      an issue): its code with "not recorded in what the desk reads".
  - **GitHub's limits:** GraphQL has its own hourly budget, apart from REST's (5,000 points); a
    page of this query costs about 1 point (E). Three cold loads an hour across 5 projects, at 3
    pages each, is at most 45 points.
  - **The fixture** (plan review round 1, B3): GraphQL is not reachable from the agents' sessions
    (`gh api graphql` answers 403 there: "GitHub GraphQL is not available from Claude Code
    sessions; use the REST API"). So the Builder builds J12's fixture in GraphQL's answer shape
    from REST answers for Service-Desk, a public repository: `gh api
    'repos/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/pulls?state=all&per_page=100'` (and its later pages), each
    pull request's fields mapped to the query's names (`merged_at` → `mergedAt`, `head.ref` →
    `headRefName` and so on), and the check state from `gh api
    repos/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/commits/<head sha>/check-runs` for the few pull requests the
    tests need. It is kept under `tests/desk/fixtures/github/` and listed in
    `tests/desk/fixtures/README.md` as **NOT as GitHub sent it**: built from REST answers, with
    the date and commands. The answers with an error (a `data: null` answer, a `RATE_LIMITED`
    one, and the field error for the check rollup) are **made up**, in the shape GitHub's
    GraphQL documentation gives, and listed so. A real recording replaces the built one once
    Setup CS13 has made it.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J12` covers it, with that fixture: the refused cursor, the
    budget, `null` on a failed read, the three-page limit and its note, a head change reading the
    first page only, each of the answer's own errors above, the kept fields only (no body text
    beyond AC61's parts), a failed write to `desk-pulls-v1` leaving the blob cache usable, and that
    the function sends the fixed query text only.

## Toolchain and deploy route
- **Layout.** Everything the Builder writes for the desk lives under `src/`, and deploys run from
  `src/`.
  - `src/package.json`: Node 20 or later, ES modules. Wrangler is a devDependency pinned to an exact
    version.
  - **No runtime dependencies (decided here; the Reviewer's round-2 non-blocking 2):**
    - `src/package.json` has no `dependencies` block, and the build fails if one appears.
    - The Functions and the page import only from `src/functions/`, `src/lib/` and `src/public/`.
    - So the bundle wrangler makes of `src/functions/` at deploy time contains only reviewed
      repository code. No separate `wrangler pages functions build` step is needed.
    - The deploy job installs nothing under `src/`. A package import in a Function would therefore
      fail the deploy-time bundle, which enforces the rule a second time.
  - **No lifecycle scripts:** `src/package.json` has no `preinstall`, `install`, `postinstall`,
    `preprepare`, `prepare`, `postprepare`, `prepack`, `postpack`, `prepublish`,
    `prepublishOnly`, `publish` or `postpublish` script. desk-build fails the PR otherwise.
  - `src/package-lock.json`.
  - `src/wrangler.toml`: only the keys `name`, `pages_build_output_dir = "public"` and
    `compatibility_date`; desk-build fails the PR on any other key. The
    project name is passed on the command line (below).
  - `src/functions/api/poll.js` and `src/functions/api/blobs.js`: Pages Functions, by file-based
    routing (C).
  - `src/lib/`: the record parsers, shared by the page and the tests.
  - `src/public/`: the page.
  - `src/config/projects.json` (J7).
- **Tests.** They live in `tests/desk/`, with fixtures in `tests/desk/fixtures/`, using Node's
  built-in runner.
  - **Build command:** `npm --prefix src ci && npm --prefix src run build`. The build also checks
    J7.
  - **Test command:** `npm --prefix src test`, which runs `node --test ../tests/desk/`.
  - **No live access:** the tests use the real samples quoted in J2, J9 and J10 and a fake `fetch`.
    No test needs a token or the network.
- **Who holds secrets: nobody but the owner and GitHub/Cloudflare** (PROJECT.md §4 line 115;
  AGENTS.md §1).
  - **The Builder** holds neither the Cloudflare API token nor the read token. It never deploys,
    and it never calls the live function.
  - **The read token** exists only as a Pages secret.
  - **The Cloudflare API token** exists only as a secret of the GitHub environments `preview` and
    `production`.
  - The owner sets each by hand (Setup).
- **Workflow 1, `.github/workflows/desk-build.yml`** (the Chief of Staff's).
  - **Trigger:** `pull_request`, and pushes to `main` that touch `src/**` or `tests/**`.
  - **Access:** `permissions: contents: read`, and no secrets. It uses no environment.
  - **Steps:**
    - `npm --prefix src ci --ignore-scripts`, then the build and test commands.
    - A config check (a script in the Chief of Staff's lane, `.github/scripts/`). It fails the PR if
      `src/wrangler.toml` holds any key other than `name`, `pages_build_output_dir` and
      `compatibility_date`. It also fails if `src/package.json` has any lifecycle script (listed
      under Layout) or a `dependencies` block.
    - It uploads no artifact and hands nothing on to any other workflow.
  - **Workflow files:** a change to `.github/**` outside the Chief of Staff's lane already fails CI
    through `surface_guard.py diff --range`, because `.github/**` is the Chief of Staff's surface in
    governance/SURFACES.md. desk-build doesn't repeat that check.
  - **Branches:** the Builder's milestone branch, `build/builder/<task>`, is pushed to origin,
    which the pull request needs. desk-build is the only desk workflow that runs on it.
- **Workflow 2, `.github/workflows/desk-deploy.yml`** (the Chief of Staff's).
  - **Trigger:** only `push` to `main` with paths `src/**`, and `workflow_dispatch`. It never runs
