    on `pull_request` or `workflow_run`. No pull-request branch name or artifact is ever used.
  - **Access:** `permissions: contents: read` for the whole workflow.
  - **Why `main` alone isn't enough (the Reviewer's round-3 R3-B1):**
    - `main` is unprotected, and agents push under the owner's admin account. So "code on `main`
      is reviewed" isn't enforced by GitHub.
    - So **every deploy job runs in an environment whose required reviewer is the owner.** That
      covers `preview` and `production`, and `exp-002` too.
    - GitHub holds each job until the owner presses Approve, which the GitHub mobile app offers.
  - **Each approval is tied to the commit it deploys (R4-B1).**
    - **Push runs** check out exactly `${{ github.sha }}`, never "the tip of `main`".
    - **`workflow_dispatch` runs** require an input `sha`.
      - The first step receives it through `env:`, never interpolated into the shell.
      - After `git fetch origin main`, it fails unless all three of these hold (R5-B1):
        1. the input matches `^[0-9a-f]{40}$`;
        2. `git merge-base --is-ancestor "$SHA" origin/main` succeeds, so the commit is on `main`;
        3. `git diff --quiet "$SHA" origin/main -- src .github/workflows .github/deploy-tools
           .github/scripts` succeeds, so nothing deployable has changed on `main` since that commit.
      - It then checks out and deploys exactly that SHA.
      - **What this allows:** Orchestrator and `decisions/` commits landing on `main` after the
        reviewed merge don't block a dispatched preview re-deploy or the launch deploy.
      - **What it still refuses:** an unmerged commit, and an older reviewed commit whose deployable
        code newer commits have replaced.
    - **`run-name`** is `deploy ${{ github.event_name == 'push' && github.sha || inputs.sha }} —
      ${{ github.event.head_commit.message || inputs.note }}`.
      - The approval screen shows the full SHA. GitHub expressions have no substring function, but
        its first 7 characters are the short SHA on the PR page.
      - On a push from a merge, it also shows the merge commit's message, which begins
        `Merge pull request #<n>`. A dispatch carries a `note` input, in which the Chief of Staff
        names the PR.
      - Both the message and the note are hints only. The owner matches the SHA.
  - **The owner's approval rule:**
    - Approve only when the run's SHA equals the merge-commit SHA shown on the page of a pull
      request that the Reviewer passed. Fix-forward PRs need the Reviewer's PASS too.
    - Reject every other run.
    - The actor name can't separate the owner's runs from an agent's: agents act under the owner's
      account, so both show as `shumpmaster`. The SHA is the only thing to check.
    - **Production:** approve only a SHA that the preview has already deployed and that the owner
      has accepted on the preview.
    - **`exp-002`:** the owner approves it when they or the Chief of Staff asked for it. It deploys
      nothing.
  - **Concurrency:** each job has its own `concurrency` group, `desk-deploy-preview`,
    `desk-deploy-production` or `desk-deploy-exp-002`, with `cancel-in-progress: false`. So two
    approved runs never deploy over each other, and a waiting run is not silently cancelled.
  - **Every deploy job:**
    - checks out the approved SHA, as above;
    - runs no tests (desk-build already ran them on the same commit);
    - re-runs the `.github/scripts/` config check on that checkout before wrangler: the
      `wrangler.toml` keys, and no lifecycle scripts or `dependencies` in `src/package.json`;
    - installs nothing under `src/`.
  - **The wrangler install step:**
    - It installs the deploy tool in a clean directory outside `src/`, from the Chief of Staff's
      pinned `.github/deploy-tools/package.json` and its lockfile. The command is
      `npm ci --ignore-scripts --prefix .github/deploy-tools`, which pins wrangler at `<x.y.z>`
      and its transitive dependencies.
    - The registry is pinned by a committed `.github/deploy-tools/.npmrc` holding
      `registry=https://registry.npmjs.org/`, so no other registry setting can redirect the
      install.
    - Whether wrangler runs fully without its packages' install scripts is C. The first deploy
      shows it. If it doesn't, the Chief of Staff names the one package whose script must run, in
      a reviewed change to that directory.
  - **The deploy step:**
    - Its `working-directory` is `src`. It calls
      `"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/.github/deploy-tools/node_modules/.bin/wrangler" pages deploy public
      --project-name <project> --branch <branch>`. Nothing from `src/node_modules` exists or runs.
      wrangler reads `src/wrangler.toml`, whose keys are limited as above, and bundles
      `src/functions/`.
    - `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` are in that step's `env:` only, not the
      job's.
  - **Job `preview`:**
    - It runs on every push to `main` touching `src/**`, and on dispatch, in environment `preview`.
    - It deploys to `service-desk-preview` with `--branch main`.
    - `main` is the preview project's production branch (Setup C1). So each approved deploy
      updates `https://service-desk-preview.pages.dev`, a fixed URL, behind its Access application
      (F-cf-04, A).
    - The job writes the URL to its summary. There is no pull-request comment step.
  - **Job `production`:**
    - It runs on `workflow_dispatch` only, from `main`, in environment `production`, with the owner
      as required reviewer.
    - Beyond the dispatch check above, it fails unless the most recent successful `preview` job of
      desk-deploy ran on the same SHA. It reads that with `GITHUB_TOKEN`.
    - Its job-level block is `permissions: { contents: read, actions: read }`. A job-level block
      replaces the workflow-level one, so both are listed.
    - It deploys to `needs-you`, a direct-upload project (EXP-002), with `--branch` set to its
      production branch as EXP-002 records.
    - It is used at M3 and never run before AC28. The owner approves it only after disabling
      Operations-Hub's `needs-you-page` workflow, which would otherwise redeploy the old page over
      it within an hour (AC28, Setup C8).
  - **Job `exp-002`:** it runs on `workflow_dispatch` only, in environment `preview` (owner
    approval). It runs the same pinned wrangler's `pages project list`, read-only, with the token in
    the step's `env:` only.
  - **Possible later hardening, not relied on — option (b):** a ruleset on `main` blocking direct
    pushes to `src/**` and `.github/**`. Whether rulesets are available on this repository's plan
    is C. The owner's approval gate above is the control.
  - **What is still trusted, stated plainly:**
    - **The Reviewer's check:** the Reviewer's reading of `src/package.json`, `src/wrangler.toml`
      and `src/package-lock.json` on each pull request is a security control, not just a quality
      check. desk-build's config check backs it up for the keys and scripts it knows.
    - **wrangler's own dependencies:** wrangler and its transitive dependencies are trusted by
      pinning only, through the Chief of Staff's lockfile. Nobody reviews their code.
    - **The approval click:** the owner's click is the last gate. A mistaken approval of an
      unreviewed push would deploy it.
    - **The gate itself is not airtight (R4-B2).** Agents act under the owner's admin account. An
      agent could, through the API, remove an environment's required reviewer or change its secrets,
      and GitHub would show the change as the owner's.
      - The environment gate stops accidents and unreviewed runs. It doesn't stop a credential that
        can administer the repository.
      - The **recommended backstop** is CS9: at every milestone acceptance, the Chief of Staff shows
        the owner both environments' protection settings, and every audit-log entry for environment
        changes since the last milestone.
      - The **alternative** is to narrow the agents' credential to one without Administration
        permission.
      - The owner chose the backstop, (a) (closed owner question 4, P-001-s001-owner-questions.md).
- **Milestone acceptance:**
  1. The Reviewer passes the milestone's pull request.
  2. The owner merges it.
  3. The owner approves desk-deploy's `preview` job, which deploys `main` to the preview.
  4. The owner tries the preview and accepts the milestone.

  A problem found on the preview is fixed by a fix-forward pull request, not by reopening the
  merged one.

## Areas touched
src/**, tests/**, !tests/acceptance/**, docs/handover/**

*From 2026-10-16 (trial-view notes 2) the same areas and no others: the sandbox in src/public and src/lib for AC57 to AC72; the function's reader in src/lib/github.js and its api files for J12; and the tests and fixtures under tests/desk for every new test marker.*

## Setup outside the Builder's surface
*None of this is in `src/**`, `tests/**` or `docs/handover/**`, and the Builder does none of it.
The owner does everything touching Access, tokens and Cloudflare or GitHub settings by hand,
following the Chief of Staff's steps below, which the Chief of Staff writes out for the owner with
the current screen names. The Chief of Staff does the repository tasks.*

**Chief of Staff, first:**
- CS1. Merge this spec to `main` before the Builder's first commit. CI's `v3_checks scope` judges
  every commit with `Spec: S-001` against this spec's Areas touched as it stands at that commit.
- CS2. **Done:** the V5 line is in PROJECT.md §6 (line 170) in its confirmed form, and §1 no
  longer holds "At least as correct as the Operations-Hub page it replaces".
- CS3. Write `desk-build.yml`, `desk-deploy.yml`, the config-check script in `.github/scripts/`, and
  `.github/deploy-tools/` (package.json and lockfile pinning wrangler), as above, in a pull request
  the owner merges.
- CS8. Get a ledger entry recorded for the exception to AGENTS.md §1, "Specs are frozen before build
  or measurement". The exception is the owner's ruling, decisions/questions/P-001-build-path.md
  question 0: build from the approved brief and the draft spec, outside the pipeline. The entry
  cites that ruling and this draft. `docs/LEDGER.md` is in the Orchestrator's surface, so the Chief
  of Staff files it by the ledger's own route.
- CS4. Wire the Builder's commands into `.github/workflows/governance.yml`, as a job that runs the
  build and test commands, and into AGENTS.md §8's `build:` and `test:` line.
- CS9. **At every milestone acceptance** (R4-B2's backstop), before the owner accepts.
  - **The owner checks it themselves, as the evidence** (about 2 minutes on the phone):
    - **The settings page:** they open Service-Desk → Settings → Environments → `preview` and
      `production`. They check that each has the owner as required reviewer, and the deployment
      branch `main` only.
    - **Their security log:** they open their own GitHub security log (Settings → Security log), filtered
      to Service-Desk environment changes since the last milestone: protection rules, secrets,
      deployment branches.
    - Which log entries GitHub records for these changes on this plan is C. If none appear, the
      settings page alone is the check.
  - **The Chief of Staff's summary** of the same, from `GET /repos/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/
    environments/{preview,production}`, is a convenience only, not the evidence.
  - Any change the owner didn't make stops the acceptance until the owner rules.

**The owner, by hand, before M1's merge** (about 45 minutes once, E, counted against the pre-launch
2 hours a week):
- C1. **Create the preview project.** Cloudflare → Workers & Pages → create a Pages project by
  direct upload, named `service-desk-preview`, with a placeholder page. Its production branch is
  `main`, because desk-deploy deploys it with `--branch main`.
- C2. **Protect the previews.** In that project's settings, turn on "Enable access policy", which
  covers preview deployments (F-cf-04). In Zero Trust → Access → Applications, set the policy it
  created to Include → Emails → the owner's address. Not One-time PIN alone, which lets anyone in
  (F-auth-02, A).
- C3. **Protect the `pages.dev` address.** Edit that Access application, delete the wildcard in its
  Subdomain field, save, and turn "Enable access policy" on again. That gives two applications
  (F-cf-04, A). Set the second one's policy to the owner's email too.
- C4. **Enter the Access settings.** Pages project → Settings → Variables and secrets →
  **Production** → add `OWNER_EMAIL`, `ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN`, and `ACCESS_AUD` holding both
  applications' audience tags, comma-separated (J8).
  - The preview project's live deployment is its production-branch (`main`) deployment, so its
    Production variables are the ones used.
  - That makes the Reviewer's round-2 non-blocking 1 (which variable set the preview uses) moot.
  - No branch preview deployments are made.
- C5. **Create the deploy token.** Cloudflare → My Profile → API Tokens → create a custom token
  with Account → Cloudflare Pages → Edit, for this account only. Copy it once, for G2.
- G1. **Create the read token.** GitHub → Settings → Developer settings → Fine-grained tokens →
  generate one with:
  - repositories: only Service-Desk and Personal-Org-Operating-Model;
  - permissions: Contents: read, Metadata: read, Pull requests: read, and Actions: read (for CI,
    J1 and AC29). A fine-grained token offers no Checks permission, which is why M1's CI line
    reads "can't read checks" on the private repository (EXP-004 Q4);
  - an expiry the owner chooses, with renewal in AC27's notes.

  Paste it straight into the Pages project → Variables and secrets → Production → secret
  `GITHUB_READ_TOKEN`. Never paste it into a chat, a file or GitHub.
- G2. **Create the GitHub environments.** Service-Desk → Settings → Environments:
  - `preview`: required reviewer the owner, deployment branch `main` only, with secrets
    `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` (from C5) and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`;
  - `production`: required reviewer the owner, deployment branch `main` only, with the same two
    secrets.
  - **On both:** tick "Required reviewers" and add the owner's account. GitHub then holds every job
    in either environment until the owner approves it (R3-B1). Leave "Prevent self-review" off,
    because the owner approves runs their own merges start.

**During the build:**
- CS5. **Done:** EXP-002 ran after M1's merge; `needs-you` is direct upload. Hand the result (its
  production branch, its custom domains if any, and the Access state of each hostname) and EXP-004's
  to the Builder, who records them in `docs/handover/experiments/` in M2.
- G3 (owner, before M2's merge; about 3 minutes). **Give the read token Actions: read.** GitHub →
  Settings → Developer settings → Fine-grained tokens → the desk's read token → edit its
  permissions → Repository permissions → Actions: Read-only → save. Both repositories, as in G1.
  The token's value doesn't change, so the Pages secret stays as it is. Until this is done, M2's
  CI line reads "can't read CI (the read token needs Actions: read)" for every project (AC29).
- CS6. At M2: run EXP-003, and carry D7.1 (Personal-Org-Operating-Model) and D7.2 (Service-Desk,
  the Orchestrator's lane, owner-approved). Both done: model S-020, adopted here as L-0005.
- CS10. At MD (AC43, process A): run the design lab.
  - Before the first mockup: have research Q-010 and Q-011 answered, and read the discovery map.
  - Publish mockups as private live pages, filled with a snapshot of the connected projects'
    records (no token or secret in it), and change them from the owner's plain-language requests
    and screenshots.
  - Record the owner's pick of direction as a ruling in `decisions/questions/`, with links to the
    chosen mockups, and hand it to the Builder for the sandbox (process B).
  - Once the sandbox is on the preview, record the owner's pick of one candidate as a second
    ruling in `decisions/questions/` (AC44 step 3), and hand it to the Builder for the pull request
    that makes it the default.
- CS11. At MD (AC45): put the phone-notification channel to the owner as a question, with Q-011's
  findings. Start from what exists (the Orchestrator's `needs-you` issues for Service-Desk cards)
  and its gaps (questions, pull requests, v2.5 projects). Say plainly that only an extra channel
  needs something new: a Web Push sender is an exception to "no server-side state, no write
  credential", and wider issue alerts are a model change. Then hand the ruling to the Definer.
  **Done:** the owner ruled on 2026-10-08 (decisions/questions/P-001-notifications.md, "built in
  M3"); the Definer carries it in AC45 and adds M3's criteria before M3 starts.
- CS12. At MD's end: prepare EXP-005's glance test (experiments/EXP-005-md-glance-test.md) and
  record its result.
- CS13 (the owner, by hand). At MD, for J12 (trial-view notes 2; plan review round 1, B3 and N7;
  round 2, N4). The owner runs it on the owner's own computer, outside every agent session: the
  agents' sessions, the Chief of Staff's included, cannot reach GitHub's GraphQL. About 10 minutes,
  once (E); the Chief of Staff writes out the steps for the owner, as for G1.
  1. **Record a real answer.** In a terminal on that computer, with the GitHub CLI signed in to
     the owner's account, the owner runs J12's fixed query, as the function holds it, for
     Service-Desk: `gh api graphql -F owner=shumpmaster -F name=Service-Desk -F base=main -F
     query=@<the query file the Builder commits beside the fixture>` (`-F` reads a value given
     as `@<file>` from that file). The answer is kept, as
     GitHub sent it, under `tests/desk/fixtures/github/`, and listed in
     `tests/desk/fixtures/README.md` as real, with the date and the command: the owner hands the
     answer file to the Chief of Staff, and the Builder swaps it for the built fixture in a pull
     request, and the tests still pass.
  2. **Check the read token's reach to the check rollup.** After J12 is deployed to the preview,
     the owner opens a sprint on the preview's Timeline and taps its checks mark: "the desk's
     token can't read checks" means the token cannot (AC60). The owner tells the Chief of Staff,
     who records the answer in the sandbox pull request's thread. No
     token permission is changed for it; adding one would be the owner's ruling, with G1 and G3's
     steps.
- **Track B (Personal-Org-Operating-Model, not the desk):** queue the owner's answers 5 and 6
  (safe stopping point before compaction; PRs lead with a practical explanation) with the
  discovery-step model change.
- CS7. Ongoing from M2: after the owner answers a question from the desk, delete its
  `questions/<name>.md`, as today. Until that deletion, `review_check.py questions` (model S-004
  AC3; governance/checks/review_check.py:29–30) still lists the question as open in the owner
  signal. The desk itself already shows it answered (AC3).

**Before M3's launch:**
- C6 (owner). Put Access applications on `needs-you.pages.dev` and each custom domain it already
  has (if any), and set its Production variables and the `GITHUB_READ_TOKEN` secret (G1's
  permissions, Actions: read included).
- C7: no longer needed. EXP-002 found `needs-you` is direct upload, so there is no new project,
  custom domain or Bulk Redirect (P-001-m1-accept.md).
- C8 (owner), in this order (AC28; about 5 minutes):
  1. In Operations-Hub, disable the `needs-you-page` workflow (Actions → `needs-you-page` →
     "…" → Disable workflow), and check no run of it is in progress. It deploys to `needs-you`
     on cron `5 0-5,12-23 * * *` and would overwrite the desk within an hour.
  2. Approve the `production` job.
  3. Open `needs-you` and check it serves the desk; then archive Operations-Hub.

## Facts relied on (each with evidence)
- Pages Functions run on Workers, and their requests count against the Workers quota. Identical
  runtime limits are not documented. Workers Logs and Tail Workers are marked Workers-only. —
  evidence: library/facts/F-cf-06.md:4 (A; the compatibility matrix is one page, B). So EXP-001
  runs on a Pages project, and it may have no per-request CPU log.
- Free plan: 100,000 requests a day, and Error 1027 beyond that — evidence:
  library/F-cf-workers-05.md:8 (A).
- Free plan: 10 ms CPU per request, with network waits excluded, and Error 1102 beyond that —
  evidence: library/F-cf-workers-01.md:8 (A). Occasional overages are tolerated — evidence:
  library/F-cf-workers-08.md:9 (B).
- 50 external subrequests per invocation on Free, and redirect hops count — evidence:
  library/F-cf-workers-02.md:8 (A).
- Six connections may wait for headers at once — evidence: library/F-cf-workers-03.md:8 (A). A
  seventh queues — evidence: library/F-cf-workers-07.md:9 (B). F-cf-workers-03 lists the seventh
  as NOT filed, so it supports only the six.
- No wall-time limit for an HTTP request on Free is filed — evidence: library/F-cf-workers-03.md:8
  (it files Paid's only). EXP-001 measures it.
- A 304 sent with an Authorization header doesn't count against GitHub's primary limit, and a PAT's
  limit is 5,000 an hour — evidence: library/Q-004-facts.md:9 and :8 (A).
- Webhooks can be minutes late and aren't redelivered, so the design polls — evidence:
  library/Q-004-facts.md:6, :14, :15 (A, B).
- `?filename=` prefills the new-file name, and no content parameter is documented — evidence:
  library/F-gh-05.md:8 (B). The owner has used content-prefilled links from the phone's browser and
  the app — evidence: docs/PROJECT.md:318-320 (C, observed by the owner).
- Over-long query links on other GitHub pages return 414, and no limit is documented for the
  new-file page — evidence: library/F-gh-06.md:8 (A). AC17's 6,000 cap is our own margin (E),
  checked in EXP-004.
- The commit step offers the current branch or a new one. Push rulesets may block creating a file,
  and protected branches block web edits — evidence: library/F-gh-07.md:8 (A), library/F-gh-10.md:9
  (A).
- Access: validate the JWT with the public key, preferring the header — evidence:
  library/F-auth-04.md:8 (A; it supports the public-key validation only, and the `exp`, `aud`
  and `email` checks are our design, C). A policy whose only rule is One-time PIN lets anyone in, and policies
  can name emails — evidence: library/F-auth-02.md:8 (A). Pages' "Enable access policy" covers only
  previews; `*.pages.dev` and each custom domain need their own policies — evidence:
  library/facts/F-cf-04.md:4 (A).
- A production deployment changes what `pages.dev` and the custom domains serve, and rollback is
  instant — evidence: library/facts/F-cf-02.md:4 (A). A project can't switch deployment method —
  evidence: library/facts/F-cf-03.md:4 (B). A `pages.dev` address can be 301-redirected to a custom
  domain with Bulk Redirects — evidence: library/facts/F-cf-07.md:4 (A). That was O5's fallback
  route, no longer needed: `needs-you` is direct upload — evidence: EXP-002's run, reported in
  decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md (C until the result file is recorded in M2).
- Fine-grained tokens offer no Checks permission, so a private repository's check runs answer 403
  to the read token — evidence: EXP-004 Q4 on the preview, Personal-Org-Operating-Model's CI line
  "can't read checks" (decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md, "Carried to M2"; C). Actions: read
  is offered and covers `actions/runs` — not in the library (C); M2's join test and the preview
  confirm it.
- Service-Desk's Orchestrator workflow runs on a 30-minute schedule — evidence:
  `.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml:16` (`cron: "*/30 * * * *"`). Its scheduled runs carry the
  default-branch head as `head_sha` — evidence: `gh api …/actions/runs?head_sha=<38722d3…>` on
  2026-10-08 listed a `schedule` run (C).
- On a head the Orchestrator pushed, governance runs by `workflow_dispatch`, not `push` —
  evidence: governance.yml:1–7, orchestrator.yml:459–465, and `gh api …/actions/runs?head_sha=<e2ab9c5…>`
  on 2026-10-08, which listed governance and orchestrator runs with event `workflow_dispatch` and
  none with `push` (C).
- EXP-001's measured load: maximum wall time 1,938 ms, p95 at most 1,413 ms, no 1102 or 1027 —
  evidence: docs/handover/experiments/EXP-001-result.md:8–10.
- Prefilled new-file links opened with their content at every tested length up to 8,000 characters
  on the owner's phone — evidence: EXP-004, reported in decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md (C
  until the result file is recorded in M2).
- A fine-grained PAT is limited to the chosen repositories and permissions — evidence:
  library/facts/L-F5.md:4 (B).
- These GitHub REST behaviours are used but not in the library (C). Each is confirmed against the
  live API by M1's join tests and first deploy:
  - `per_page` up to 100;
  - `Link: rel="next"` paging;
  - check-run `status` and `conclusion` values (M1), and workflow-run `status`, `conclusion`,
    `head_sha` and `event` filters, and latest-attempt listing (M2);
  - `compare/{base}...{head}` giving `status` `ahead`, `identical`, `behind` or `diverged`, and
    taking `per_page` (M2, AC47);
  - `git/trees?recursive=1` with `truncated`;
  - the raw media type for blobs;
  - `commits?path=` listing a deleted path's history;
  - `branches/{b}` giving `commit.commit.tree.sha`.
- Record formats: J2 and J9 quote real files and code lines from Service-Desk at e2ab9c5 and
  Personal-Org-Operating-Model at 677fe5a. These are observations (C), not library facts.
- Trial-view notes 2 (2026-10-16), observed at 2c1acb6 (C):
  - the sandbox reads each project's live records through the desk's function, not fixtures:
    src/public/app.js:638 and src/public/sandbox.js:1766;
  - the answer boxes' text is .88rem, about 14 px: src/public/sandbox.css:297; the viewport line
    does not lock zoom: src/public/index.html:5;
  - the desk's milestone reader fails a line that ends with ` · why: `, since its form ends at
    `· sprints:` or `· dropped`: src/lib/j11.js:161-163;
  - docs/PROJECT.md section 3 holds no why line and no standard-milestone line (its milestone
    lines are docs/PROJECT.md:106-114 at 4efc670), and its Purpose items are docs/PROJECT.md:480-483;
  - pull request #77's body opens with `## What this means for you` and has no `## Context` or
    `## Why` section: `gh api repos/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/pulls/77` (REST; `gh pr view` goes
    through GraphQL, which the agents' sessions cannot reach, plan review round 1, B3);
  - a pull request in GitHub's REST list is 17,602 and 20,114 characters in the recorded page
    tests/desk/fixtures/github/pulls-page1.json;
  - the mockup's Design colour is the agent colour: docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html:11
    and src/public/sandbox.css:10.
- **Request arithmetic for AC14 (our estimate, E).** These assumptions can be checked against
  Cloudflare's request count on the preview after M1:
  - **A1:** open windows total at most 16 hours a day across all devices. Two devices open at once
    count twice.
  - **A2:** one poll call per project per 60 s, and no retry sooner than the next poll.
  - **A3:** a poll cycle makes at most 6 blob calls per project (`MAX_BLOB_CALLS_PER_CYCLE`,
    src/public/lib/scheduler.js), each with a budget of 25 (J6: a blob counts 1, a history read 2,
    a compare 1). The needed reads come first; the optional ruling reads (AC20's "Time asked")
    then use what is left of the 6. Ruling reads that don't fit fill in on later cycles, and each
    is made once per device: the page keeps history times and blobs by path and sha.
    - **Service-Desk today:** about 2 calls of needed reads (13 status files, ROUTING.toml, 2 log
      months, outcomes, open cards, unlogged answers, the newest hold card), then its 18 rulings'
      history reads, their questions' history reads and the ruling blobs (about 90 of budget, so
      at least 4 calls). On today's tree a cold load takes about 5 to 6 calls (E, counted from the
      tree, not measured), so the optional reads use most or all of the first cycle.
    - **Personal-Org-Operating-Model:** 5 (the ledger and the 4 sprint files), plus any open
      questions: 1 call.
    - **From MD (J11, 2026-10-14):** Service-Desk's cold load adds about 18 blobs at 3117150 (13
      research requests, docs/PROJECT.md, S-001.md, SURFACES.md, orchestrator.yml and
      merges.jsonl), so about one more call (E). They are optional reads, like the ruling reads:
      they come after the needed reads, fill in on later cycles if the 6 are used, and are made
      once per device. The 12 calls counted per opening below still cover them. A
      v2.5 project makes none of these but one: README.md (AC54, 2026-10-14).
    - **From AC54 (2026-10-14):** every connected project adds one optional blob, its
      `README.md`, read once per device and again only when its sha changes. Service-Desk's
      cold load goes to about 19 added blobs, still about one more call (E);
      Personal-Org-Operating-Model's goes from 5 to 6 blobs, still 1 call. The 12 calls counted
      per opening still cover it, so the total below is unchanged.
    - **From AC60 (J12, 2026-10-16):** Service-Desk's cold load adds one to three pages of its
      pull requests (about 80 today, so one page), each counting 5 of a blob call's 25, and the
      `reviews/<n>/` verdict files (38 small blobs at 2c1acb6), so about two more
      calls (E), filled in on later cycles like the other optional reads. Each head change adds
      the first page again to that minute's blob call (A4), and the first load each day repeats the
      cold read (at most 3 pages, within that load's 12 calls). Personal-Org-Operating-Model makes
      none of these. The 12 calls counted per opening still cover it, so the total is unchanged.
    - **For the arithmetic** (E), every opening is counted at 12 calls per project: 6 in its first
      cycle and up to 6 more for ruling reads on later cycles. At most 10 openings a day across
      devices: 5 × 12 × 10 = 600. That over-counts: ruling reads are made once per device, not per
      opening.
  - **A4:** each head change is read inside that minute's poll call (the tree), plus at most one
    blob call. At most 100 changes a day across all projects while open: 100. A change to a J11
    file adds at most that file's blob to the same call (a new dispatch-log line already changes a
    needed blob).
  - **Total:** 4,800 (5 × 60 × 16) + 600 + 100 = **5,500**, well under the 10,000 margin.
  - **Worked case outside AC14 (A1 broken):** two devices each open for 16 hours make 9,600 polls.
    That is 10,300 to 11,000 in all, depending on whether openings and changes double. It is above
    the margin, but about a tenth of Free's 100,000, so there is no Error 1027. If the check
    finds A1 broken, the owner chooses between a wider margin and a longer poll interval when several
    devices are open.
  - **GitHub, per hour:** steady polls are 4 × 5 × 60 = 1,200 requests from M2 (3 × 5 × 60 = 900
    in M1), mostly free 304s (PAT-Q004-1, library/Q-004-facts.md:18). Three cold loads in one hour
    add at most 3 × 5 × 6 × 25 = 2,250 full reads in their first cycles (E: only a project with
    many rulings uses all 6 calls). Ruling reads that spill into later cycles are once per device,
    and only Service-Desk has them today. About 3,450 is under 5,000. The function's own request
    count (AC14) doesn't change: still one poll call per project per minute.
- **Known limits:**
  - The content prefill parameter is undocumented (C). GitHub could change it; AC17 is the
    fallback, and its name is config (J7).
  - The dispatch-log times stand in for commit times (J2), checked in three cases only (C).
  - Pages may expose no per-request CPU figure (F-cf-06). EXP-001 then rests on Error 1102 counts,
    which tolerated overages can mask (F-cf-workers-08, B).
  - J10's `usage` form is now model S-020's frozen form (AC2), adopted here as L-0005; J10's
    earlier proposal was its starting point (P-001-s001-owner-questions.md, answer 3). Whether
    `autocompact_threshold`, `compactions` and `would_have_stopped` are reported at the pinned
    agent-tool version is S-020's live check; until then they are null and the desk shows them as
    "not available" (AC23's one rule).
  - CI reads push and dispatch runs only (J1's 3a and 3b). A scheduled run, or a pull-request
    run sharing the head's sha, isn't read (C, my choice). desk-deploy and Orchestrator runs are
    shown as activity, not CI.
  - MD's design answers rest on research Q-010, a graded memo of published guidance, and on one
    owner's judgment; the glance test (EXP-005) is a small sample by design (E).

## Experiments in Build (EXP-001, EXP-002 and EXP-004 on the preview after M1's merge; EXP-003 at M2's start; EXP-005 at MD's end)
- **Outcomes at M1's acceptance** (2026-10-07, decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md):
  - **EXP-001 passed** every set: no Error 1102 or 1027, no call at or over 30 s, maximum wall
    time 1.9 s (1,938 ms), p95 at most 1.4 s (docs/handover/experiments/EXP-001-result.md).
    `BLOB_BATCH` stays 25, so no fallback rerun was needed. The page's 30 s call timeout (J6)
    stands. Cloudflare showed no CPU figure, so the CPU part rests on the 1102 count (Known
    limits).
  - **EXP-002:** `needs-you` is a direct-upload project, not Git-integrated. The O5 fallback, the
    custom domain and Setup C7 are not needed; AC28 deploys straight into `needs-you`.
  - **EXP-004:** every prefilled-link length tested, up to 8,000 characters, opened GitHub's
    new-file page with the content filled in on the owner's phone. `contentPrefill: true`,
    `contentParam: "value"` and `linkCap: 6000` stand. Q4: the read token can't read check runs
    on private Personal-Org-Operating-Model, which AC29 answers.
  - **Still to record:** the EXP-002 and EXP-004 result files, in `docs/handover/experiments/`,
    by the Builder in M2 from the Chief of Staff's hand-over (CS5). If EXP-004's Q1 found a
    repository that doesn't take web commits to `main`, that repository's `webCommitsToDefault`
    becomes `false` in the same pull request (AC18).
- **Ruling:** the owner's ruling of 2026-10-06 (P-001-build-path.md, question 0: "the experiments
  run during the build") replaces "measured in Define" for O3 and O5. The fallbacks are the ones the
  owner already ruled.
- **When they run:** EXP-001, EXP-002 and EXP-004 run on the preview after M1's pull request has
  merged and desk-deploy has deployed `main`. They never run on an M1 branch.
- **Registration:** each experiment is registered in `experiments/` (D-047).
- **Where results go:** `docs/handover/experiments/<id>-result.md` on `main`, by one of two
  routes.
  - **The owner commits it** through the prefilled link the EXP-001 panel offers. It is a human
    commit, which may touch any path.
  - **Or a later pull request records it** from what the owner or the Chief of Staff hands over.
    `docs/handover/**` is the Builder's surface in governance/SURFACES.md, so that pull request is
    the Builder's. A Chief of Staff commit there would fail `surface_guard`.
  - A result that changes code or config (`BLOB_BATCH`, J7's values) is applied in a fix-forward
    pull request before M1 is accepted (AC15).
- **No secrets for the Builder:** the Builder holds no secret and can't reach the Access-protected
  preview, so every live step is either the owner's or a workflow's.
- **Owner time, pre-launch** (estimates, E). The ceiling is the pre-launch 2 hours a week
  (PROJECT.md §6).

  | Item | Minutes |
  |---|---|
  | Setup C1–C5, G1–G2 (once) | 45 |
  | Reviewing and merging the Chief of Staff's PRs: CS1 (this spec), CS3 (workflows), CS4 (CI wiring), CS2 and CS8 (brief and ledger), about 5 each | 25 |
  | Answering this spec's five owner questions | 10 |
  | M1's review, merge, and trying the preview | 20 |
  | Deploy approvals: about 1 minute per merge to `src/**` and per dispatched run (M1, two fix-forwards, `exp-002`) | 4 |
  | EXP-001 | 15 |
  | Each EXP-001 fallback rerun (`BLOB_BATCH` 12, then 6), about 10 each | 0–20 |
  | EXP-004 | 15 |
  | EXP-002, only if the dashboard is needed | 0–5 |
  | CS9's gate check at M1's acceptance, by the owner on the phone | 2 |
  | **Total** | **136–161** |

  That is more than one week's 2 hours, so it is spread over two weeks:
  - **Week 1:** Setup, the Chief of Staff's PRs and the questions, about 80 minutes.
  - **Week 2:** M1's merge, the deploy approvals, the preview and the experiments, 56 to 81 minutes.

  M1's acceptance is planned for the second week accordingly. (It came on 2026-10-07. No EXP-001
  rerun was needed.)

- **Owner time, M1's acceptance to launch** (estimates, E; amendment of 2026-10-08). The same
  ceiling of 2 hours a week applies.

  | Item | Minutes |
  |---|---|
  | Answering this amendment's owner questions | 10 |
  | G3: giving the read token Actions: read (once) | 3 |
  | M2: review, merge, trying the preview, and CS9 | 22 |
  | MD: design-lab rounds, about 4 to 6 of 10 to 15 minutes each, on the phone | 40–90 |
  | MD: the phone-notification ruling, from Q-011's findings (AC45); ruled 2026-10-08, done | 10 |
  | MD: the sandbox pull request, then the pull request that makes the chosen design the default: review, merge, trying the preview | 35 |
  | MD: EXP-005's glance test, five glances of about 2 minutes, plus reading the result | 15 |
  | MD: CS9 at acceptance | 2 |
  | M3: review, merge, trying the preview, and CS9 | 22 |
  | M3: Setup C6 and C8, and the launch approval | 20 |
  | Deploy approvals, about 1 minute each (M2, MD and M3 merges, and about three fix-forwards) | 9 |
  | **Total** | **188–238** |

  That is two weeks' ceiling at the least. Spread over the build, it fits about one hour a week
  for four weeks:
  - **Week of 12 October:** questions, G3, M2.
  - **Weeks of 19 and 26 October:** the design lab, the notification ruling, the sandbox.
  - **Week of 2 November:** the merged design, the glance test, MD's acceptance, then M3 and
    launch.

  That makes V4's date of 2026-11-11 tight but possible, with no slack for a slow lab. Open
  question 1 asks the owner whether V4's date should move.

- **EXP-001 (O3), CPU and wall time** — experiments/EXP-001-o3-cpu-walltime.md.
  - **Runner: the owner's signed-in browser** (about 15 minutes of the owner's time). There is no
    test-only path in J8: an Access service token carries no `email`, so it could never pass J8.
  - **Method:** the owner opens the preview's experiment panel (`?exp=001`, built by the Builder in
    M1) on a desktop browser, starts the run, and leaves the tab in the foreground for about 75
    minutes.
    - The panel drives the real M1 function through the owner's own Access session. It runs steady
      polls, cold loads against both repositories, and heaviest-case blob calls against
      Personal-Org-Operating-Model, the largest connected repository (1,963 KB, 318 tracked files, a
      128,438-byte ledger).
    - The owner then reads the Functions CPU figures in Cloudflare's dashboard, if it shows any, and
      commits the panel's summary to `main` through the prefilled link the panel offers. The raw
      table goes in through AC17's copy fallback.
  - **Pass:**
    - zero Error 1102, zero 1027 and zero wall-time failures in every set;
    - every call completes in under 30 s, as measured by the page;
    - if Cloudflare shows a CPU figure for the Functions, its 99th percentile is under 10 ms.
  - **Fallback**, ruled in decisions/questions/P-001-o3-define.md and P-001-o3-walltime.md:
    - first, smaller batches: `BLOB_BATCH` 25 → 12 → 6, re-run at each size;
    - otherwise, the $5 Paid plan, which needs a new ceiling from the owner.
  - **Result: passed** at `BLOB_BATCH` 25 (above). No fallback was used.
- **EXP-002 (O5), how `needs-you` deploys** — experiments/EXP-002-o5-needs-you-deploy-method.md.
  - **Runner: the Chief of Staff,** through desk-deploy's read-only `exp-002` job. The Chief of
    Staff reads its log (CS5). If the log can't settle it, the owner checks the dashboard (about 5
    minutes).
  - **Pass (Direct Upload):** M3 deploys into `needs-you` through the `production` job, as AC28
    says.
  - **Fallback (Git-integrated)**, ruled in decisions/questions/P-001-o5-define.md: a new Pages
    project with a custom domain, and a permanent redirect from the old address (F-cf-07, A). A 301
    counts as meeting V4 (decisions/questions/P-001-move-to-build-values.md).
  - **Result: Direct Upload** (P-001-m1-accept.md). The fallback is not needed.
- **EXP-004, per-repository values** — experiments/EXP-004-prefilled-link-per-repo.md.
  - **Runners:** the owner's phone test (about 15 minutes), plus what the deployed preview itself
    shows. No step needs the Builder to hold a token.
    - **Q1, web commits:** what GitHub's commit dialog offers the owner during the phone test.
      The Chief of Staff may also check the branch rules with its own GitHub access.
    - **Q4, reading checks:** whether the preview's CI field reads, or says "can't read checks".
  - **What it records:** `webCommitsToDefault`, `contentPrefill`, `contentParam` and `linkCap` for
    each repository, and whether the read token can read check runs.
  - **Pass:** each value is recorded and applied in J7.
  - **Fallbacks:** AC17 if the content doesn't prefill, AC18 if web commits are blocked, and J1's
    "can't read checks" if check runs are refused.
  - **Result:** Q2 and Q3 passed, every length up to 8,000 characters. Q4: check runs were refused
    on Personal-Org-Operating-Model, so from M2 CI is read from workflow runs (AC29, Setup G3).
- **EXP-003 (O6), usage fields** — experiments/EXP-003-o6-session-usage-fields.md.
  - **Runner:** the Chief of Staff, at M2's start, with the results handed to D7.1 and J10.
  - **Fallback**, ruled in decisions/questions/P-001-o6-fallback.md, option A: show what is
    reported, and mark the rest "not available".
- **EXP-005, MD's 5-second glance test** — experiments/EXP-005-md-glance-test.md.
  - **Runner:** the owner, on the phone, on the preview once the chosen design is the default view
    (AC44 step 4). The Chief of Staff prepares the prompts and records the result (CS12). About 15
    minutes.
  - **What it checks:** that a glance of 5 seconds at the first screen tells the owner which
    projects need them, which they would open first and why, and whether any project can't be read
    (AC35, AC38, AC41).
  - **Pass:** as the registration's deciding threshold says.
  - **If it fails:** the design goes back to the lab for the failing question, and the merged
    design is fixed forward, then the test is taken again. At most two such loops; after a second
    failed retest, the owner rules (accept as is, another loop, or a different candidate).

## Risks and mitigations
- **No freeze (process).**
  - This spec stays `draft`. AGENTS.md §1 says "Specs are frozen before build or measurement". The
    recorded exception is the owner's ruling, decisions/questions/P-001-build-path.md question 0:
    build from the approved brief and the draft spec, outside the pipeline. A ledger entry records
    it (Setup CS8).
  - Under the owner's lighter path, no Critic gate or Check author follows.
    The Builder writes the tests too, and the Reviewer judges each milestone against these criteria.
  - So the "builder is never the judge" rule rests on the Reviewer alone.
  - Mitigation:
    - each milestone's PR quotes the ACs it claims, with the test names;
    - the Reviewer checks every AC of the milestone, not a sample;
    - the owner merges.
  - CI's `v3_checks scope` judges Builder commits carrying `Spec: S-001` against this spec's Areas
    touched as it stands at each commit, so this spec must be on `main` before the Builder's first
    commit (Setup CS1).
- **CPU or wall time doesn't fit Free (O3).** The function parses no record content, which shrinks
  the risk. EXP-001 measures it at M1 with the owner's ruled fallback.
- **`needs-you` is Git-integrated (O5).** Closed: EXP-002 found it is direct upload, so AC28
  deploys straight into it.
- **Deploy credential.** The Cloudflare API token can probably deploy any Pages project in the
  account, `needs-you` included. That per-project scoping isn't offered is my inference (C).
  - **Who holds it:** no agent reads it. It lives only in the `preview` and `production` GitHub
    environments. Agents' admin-level access to the repository could still change those
    environments (R4-B2, CS9).
  - **What runs with it:** only desk-deploy, on `push` to `main` or `workflow_dispatch`. Every job
    waits in an environment for the owner's approval (`preview` and `production` alike). The owner
    approves only a run whose SHA equals the merge-commit SHA of a pull request the Reviewer
    passed.
    `main` is not protected and agents push under the owner's account, so this approval is the main
    control (R3-B1), not "main is reviewed". It is not airtight (R4-B2, below).
  - **The read token:** `GITHUB_READ_TOKEN` is reachable only by deployed Functions, and nothing
    deploys without that approval.
  - **What is left:**
    - A change to `desk-deploy.yml` could misuse the token. `.github/**` is the Chief of Staff's
      lane, guarded by `surface_guard`, and only the owner merges it.
    - A mistaken approval click would deploy unreviewed code.
    - wrangler's transitive dependencies are trusted by pinning only.
    - The Reviewer's reading of `src/package.json`, `src/wrangler.toml` and the lockfile is itself
      a security control.
    - Option (b), a ruleset on `main`, is possible later hardening; its availability is C.
    - Agents act under the owner's admin account, so they could remove the environment gate itself
      (R4-B2). The backstop is CS9's check at each milestone acceptance; the alternative is a
      narrower agent credential; the owner chose the backstop (closed owner question 4).
- **The owner's time before M1 is accepted.** It comes to 136–161 minutes (E, see "Experiments in
  Build"), more than one week's 2 hours. So it is planned over two weeks. If the EXP-001 reruns or
  the owner questions run long, M1's acceptance moves, and V4's date with it. (M1 was accepted on
  2026-10-07.) From M1 to launch it comes to 188–238 minutes, spread over about four weeks (the
  second owner-time table); a slow design lab moves launch, and V4's date with it (owner question
  1).
- **GitHub drops the content parameter.** AC17's copy fallback, switched per repository in J7.
- **A missed item while the desk shows "Quiet" (the owner's worst case).** These all guard it:
  - AC4 and AC5 make "Quiet" require a fresh, complete read of every project;
  - paging past 3 pages, or a truncated tree, makes a project `partial`, never quiet;
  - a misparsed card is still flagged;
  - a `waiting-owner` status without its card file is still flagged.
- **D7.1 doesn't land in time.** (Resolved: D7.1 landed as model S-020, adopted as L-0005.) Launch waits for it (owner's ruling, P-001-build-path.md question
  5). AC22's timeline works from today's records, so M2's other parts are not blocked. Only AC23's
  real figures and the launch are.
- **Records grow.** `outcomes.jsonl` and the ledger are not rotated, and dispatch logs grow about
  50 KB a month today. Each is read only when it changes, and parsing happens on the page, so growth
  costs bytes, not function CPU.
  - EXP-001's heaviest case sets the tested size.
  - The Chief of Staff re-runs EXP-001's heaviest case when any single record a call returns grows
    past twice the largest tested blob (J6's `cost.bytes` shows it).
- **Read token leaks.** It is fine-grained and read-only, limited to the connected repositories
  (L-F5), held only as a Pages secret, and never sent to the page or a model (PROJECT.md §5). D5.2
  reviews it at M1.
- **Too many agents for T1.** Build runs with at most two builder or reviewer agents at once
  (PROJECT.md §5).
- **Design work that looks finished but isn't used as a glance** (discovery map trap 11, "judged as
  pictures, used as glances"). The lab fills mockups with real records and H5's extremes, the
  sandbox runs on live data, and EXP-005 tests a real glance on the preview before MD is accepted.
- **The redesign breaks what M1 and M2 promise.** AC36 keeps every view reachable, AC35 keeps
  AC1–AC8 on the first screen, AC42 keeps the request budget, headers and 360 px, and the Reviewer
  checks M1's and M2's tests still pass on each MD pull request.
- **The sandbox is mistaken for the real view.** It is entered only by `?sandbox`, carries a marker
  on every screen, and is removed once the chosen design becomes the default (AC43, AC44).
- **Constraint creep** (discovery map trap 10): a mockup that relies on a chart library, a web font
  or extra requests can't be built. AC43 binds the lab's mockups to AC42's constraints, and the
  Chief of Staff checks each mockup against them before the owner picks.
- **Notifications widen the desk's trust boundary.** Beyond the existing card issues, a desk
  notification channel needs a server-side sender, stored state or a write credential (AC45).
  *Ruled 2026-10-08* (decisions/questions/P-001-notifications.md): Web Push sent by the
  Orchestrator's GitHub run, the subscription a GitHub secret the owner sets, no database and no
  new Cloudflare service, built in M3. Nothing is built until M3's criteria are written, and the
  Reviewer's security review still applies (plan review round 3, N3).
- **Touching the live page before launch.** The preview is a separate Pages project. Nothing
  deploys to `needs-you` before AC28, and EXP-002 is read-only (L-0115).

## Stop rule, rollback, cost
stop rule: three Reviewer FAILs on one milestone's pull request stop that milestone, and the owner
rules (continue, re-aim or stop); V4's date and PROJECT.md §6's miss rule still apply.
rollback: before launch, delete the preview project, and nothing else changes; after launch, roll
`needs-you` back to the last Operations-Hub deployment (instant, F-cf-02), un-archive
Operations-Hub, and re-enable its `needs-you-page` workflow. MD's sandbox and design changes are rolled back on the preview by reverting their
merge and redeploying (owner-approved).
cost: hosting $0/month (Cloudflare Free; EXP-001 passed); pay-per-use AI $0/month (agents on the
owner's subscription); owner time within the pre-launch 2 hours a week (PROJECT.md §6). The
owner steps before M1's acceptance come to 136–161 minutes over two weeks (E; the table in
"Experiments in Build"). From M1's acceptance to launch, M2, MD and M3 come to 188–238 minutes
over about four weeks (E; the second table there), M3's C6 and C8 included. The design lab's pages
are Claude artifacts, private to the owner's account, on the owner's existing plan at no extra cost (E).

**Open** (amendment of 2026-10-08):
1. **Launch date.** With the design step, your part from now to launch comes to about 3 to 4

2. **What the CI line covers.** From M2 the desk reads each project's CI from GitHub Actions (your
   token gains Actions: read). The CI line will show the checks that ran on the latest commit,
   whether you, an agent or the Orchestrator made it. Two kinds of run are shown separately as
   activity, not as CI: deploys (which wait for your approval) and the Orchestrator's own runs.
   The Orchestrator's half-hourly timed runs are not read at all; they would pile up and stop the
   desk reading after a quiet week. Recommended: agree.

**Answered: the design lab's screens** (2026-10-14; the design-lab open questions, answerable in one
pass). *Answered 2026-10-16 in decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md: every answer as recommended except 1,
which drops the bar (see "Where the build stands", the design-lab answers). Answers 5 and 6

9. **The gate document.** The desk links to a gate's case and the Critic's view on GitHub. Do you

    sessions recorded (you've agreed in principle), written plain-English summaries, a recorded
    "who has the ball" and deploy approver, a milestone's "reached", the verdicts a pull request
    still needs with the Reviewer's statement, and dependencies between waiting items. Which
    should go to the model first? Until then each shows "not recorded".

## Brief for the surface owner
spec: specs/S-001.md (draft; the owner's lighter path, no freeze)   surface owner: builder
budget: three Reviewer rounds per milestone; $0 hosting, $0 API
- **Checks:** the Builder writes the tests under `tests/desk/`, including every
  `join-test: S-001/J<n>` and `ac-test: S-001/AC<n>` marker. The Reviewer checks each milestone's
  ACs.
- **In scope:** the areas touched, and the toolchain above. **Out of scope:** everything else,
  including every Setup step and every secret.
- **Commands:** build `npm --prefix src ci && npm --prefix src run build`; test
  `npm --prefix src test`.
- **Order:**
  - **M1 (AC1–AC15): accepted 2026-10-07.**
    - Push the milestone branch to origin and open the M1 pull request. desk-build runs its tests,
      with no secrets.
    - After the Reviewer's PASS and the owner's merge, the owner approves desk-deploy, which
      deploys `main` to the preview.
    - The pull request includes the EXP-001 panel and the EXP-004 links (as data in `src/`).
    - Apply the experiment results in fix-forward pull requests.
  - **M2 (AC16–AC23, AC29–AC34, AC46, AC47):**
    - AC29 replaces the check-runs read with workflow runs (J1, J6), for every project.
    - AC30–AC34 are the Reviewer's PR #41 findings; J2 and J6 now match the code PR #41 built.
    - AC46 flags the Orchestrator's hold cards (J2), extending J6's history-path check.
    - AC47 flags its merge cards (J2), adding J1's compare request and J6's `compare` field and
      check.
    - Record the EXP-002 and EXP-004 results in `docs/handover/experiments/` from the Chief of
      Staff's hand-over (CS5), and apply any J7 value they change.
  - **MD (AC35–AC45, AC48–AC72), after M2 is accepted:**
    - Start only when the Chief of Staff hands over the owner's pick from the design lab (CS10).
      The lab itself is not the Builder's.
